Recent Colorings And Labelings
In Topological Coding

Abstract




Recent Colorings And Labelings

In Topological Coding

Bing
Yao
1
,   Hongyu
Wang
2,3

(July 2, 2026)

1. College of Mathematics and Statistics, Northwest Normal University, Lanzhou, 730070 CHINA

2. National Computer Network Emergency Response Technical Team/Coordination Center of China, Beijing, 100029, CHINA

3. School of Electronics Engineering and Computer Science, Peking University, Beijing, 100871, CHINA

Abstract:
Topological Coding consists of two different kinds of mathematics: topological structure and mathematical relation. The colorings and labelings of graph theory are main techniques in topological coding applied in asymmetric encryption system. Topsnut-gpws (also, colored graphs) have the following advantages: (1) Run fast in communication networks because they are saved in computer by popular matrices rather than pictures. (2) Produce easily text-based (number-based) strings for encrypt files. (3) Diversity of asymmetric ciphers, one public-key corresponds to more private-keys, or more public-keys correspond more private-keys. (4) Irreversibility, Topsnut-gpws can generate quickly text-based (number-based) strings with bytes as long as desired, but these strings can not reconstruct the original Topsnut-gpws. (5) Computational security, since there are many non-polynomial (NP-complete, NP-hard) algorithms in creating Topsnut-gpws. (6) Provable security, since there are many mathematical conjectures (open problems) in graph labelings and graph colorings. We are committed to create more kinds of new Topsnut-gpws to approximate practical applications and anti-quantum computation, and try to use algebraic method and Topsnut-gpws to establish graphic group, graphic lattice, graph homomorphism
etc
.

Mathematics Subject classification
: 05C78, 05C90, 06B30, 68P30

Keywords:
Graph coloring; graph labeling; set-coloring; sequence coloring; graphic coloring; graphic group; topological coding; graph homomorphism; graphic lattice; topological lattices.

1
Research background and preliminary

1.1
Research background

The authors in
[
21
]
point: “There are many important classes of cryptographic systems beyond RSA and DSA and ECDSA, and they are believed to resist classical computers and quantum computers, such as Hash-based cryptography, Code-based cryptography, Lattice-based cryptography, Multivariate-quadratic-equations cryptography, Secret-key cryptography”. Notice that the lattice difficulty problem is not only a classical
number theory
, but also an important research topic of computational complexity theory. Researchers have found that
lattice theory
has a wide range of applications in cryptanalysis and design. Many difficult problems in lattice have been proved to be NP-hard. So, this kind of cryptosystems is generally considered to have the characteristics of quantum attack resistance
[
22
]
.

We started learning
graph theory
from a famous book “Graph Theory With Application” written by Prof. Bondy and Prof. Murty in 1976, And they presented us a good book “Graph Theory” with more algorithms for modern graph theory in 2008
[
4
]
. Many of our research works of graph labelings were based on a famous survey article contributed by Gallian in
[
8
]
, see “
A survey: recent results, conjectures and open problems on labeling graphs
” in
[
7
]
. Prof. Gallian introduces over 200 graph labelings up to 2019 after more than ten years of unremitting collection, his survey has collected over 2830 articles all over the world. It must be admitted that Prof. Gallian’s comprehensive article has greatly helped us to learn new graph labelings quickly, and promotion for the study and research of graph labelings, and powerful tools to carry out effective research works, and obtain our own encouraging achievements.

Topological Coding
is a combinatoric subbranch of
graph theory
and
cryptography
. Topological coding is made up of two different kinds of mathematics: topological structure and mathematical relation. The colorings and labelings of graph theory are main techniques in topological coding. The domain of topological coding involves millions of things, and a graph of topological coding connects things together to form a complete “story” under certain constraints in asymmetric encryption system
[
49
]
. It is known that there is no polynomial quantum algorithm to solve some lattice problems, so is the graph isomorphism problem
[
21
]
.

Encrypting a network wholly to resist full-scale attacks and sabotage by attackers is needed extensively today. On October 23, 2019, according to an article published in Nature, the 53 bit quantum computer “Sycamore” developed by Google is to complete a specific problem computing in 200 seconds, the problem will take 10,000 years for the world’s fastest supercomputer. So, network security will be challenged tremendously in the near future.

By aiming to researching on topics of topological coding we have proposed new graph labelings and have designed new colorings by combining traditional colorings and traditional labelings together. These new colorings and labelings help greatly us to design text-based strings, number-based strings and graphic passwords (Topsnut-gpws) made by topological structures and number theory, graphic groups, Topcode-matrices, graphic lattices, graph homomorphism lattices, and so on. Topcode-matrices can be used to describe topological graphic passwords used in information security and graph connected properties for solving some problems coming in the investigation of Graph Networks and Graph Neural Networks proposed by GoogleBrain and DeepMind
[
3
]
.

Since the colorings and labelings mentioned here are selected from many of articles, for the convenience of research, we have retained their names and notations as that in the original articles, and some of individual colorings, or individual labelings may appear in the different definitions of this article.

1.2
Terminology and graph operations

Standard terminology and notation of graphs and digraphs used here are cited from
[
2
]
,
[
4
]
and
[
8
]
. All graphs are
simple
and no
loops
, unless otherwise stated. The following notation and terminology will be used in the whole article:

\bullet

All non-negative integers are collected in the set
Z^{0}
, and all integers are in the set
Z
, so
Z^{+}=Z^{0}\setminus\{0\}
.

\bullet

A
(p,q)
-graph
G
is a graph having
p
vertices and
q
edges, and
G
has no multiple edge and directed-edge; and
G^{c}
is the complementary graph of the
(p,q)
-graph
G
.

\bullet

The
cardinality
of a set
X
is denoted as
|X|
, so the
degree
of a vertex
x
in a
(p,q)
-graph
G
is denoted as
\textrm{deg}_{G}(x)=|N(x)|
, where
N(x)
is the set of neighbors of the vertex
x
.

\bullet

A vertex
x
is called a
leaf
if
\textrm{deg}_{G}(x)=1
.

\bullet

Adding the new edges of an edge set
E^{*}
to a graph
G
produces a new graph, denoted as
G+E^{*}
, where
E^{*}\cap E(G)=\emptyset
.

\bullet

A
caterpillar
is a tree
T
, such that the deletion of all leaves of
T
produces a path. A
lobster
is a tree
H
, such that the deletion of all leaves of
H
produces a caterpillar.

\bullet

A symbol
[a,b]
stands for an integer set
\{a,a+1,a+2,\dots,b\}
with two integers
a,b
subject to
a<b
, and
[a,b]^{o}
denotes an
odd-set

\{a,a+2,\dots,b\}
with odd integers
a,b
holding
1\leq a<b
true, and
[\alpha,\beta]^{e}
is an
even-set

\{\alpha,\alpha+2,\dots,\beta\}
with even integers
\alpha,\beta
and
\alpha<\beta
.

\bullet

The symbol
[a,b]^{r}
is an
interval
of real numbers.

\bullet

A
text-based password
is abbreviated as
TB-paw
and is made by 52 English characters and numbers of
[0,9]
.

\bullet

A
text-based string

D=t_{1}t_{2}\cdots t_{m}
has its own
reciprocal text string
defined by
D^{-1}=t_{m}t_{m-1}\cdots t_{2}t_{1}
, also, we say that
D
and
D^{-1}
match with each other, where
m
is the
number
of the text-based string
D
.

\bullet

A
number-based string

S(n)=c_{1}c_{2}\cdots c_{n}
with
c_{j}\in[0,9]
, where
n
is the
number
(length) of the number-based string
S(n)
.

\bullet

Let
S
be a set. The set of all subsets is denoted as
S^{2}=\{X:~X\subseteq S\}
, called the
power set
of
S
, and
S^{2}
contains no empty set at all. For example, for a set
S=\{a,b,c,d,e\}
, so
S^{2}
has its own elements
\{a\}
,
\{b\}
,
\{c\}
,
\{d\}
,
\{e\}
,
\{a,b\}
,
\{a,c\}
,
\{a,d\}
,
\{a,e\}
,
\{b,c\}
,
\{b,d\}
,
\{b,e\}
,
\{c,d\}
,
\{c,e\}
,
\{d,e\}
,
\{a,b,c\}
,
\{a,b,d\}
,
\{a,b,e\}
,
\{a,c,d\}
,
\{a,c,e\}
,
\{a,d,e\}
,
\{b,c,d\}
,
\{b,c,e\}
,
\{a,b,c,d\}
,
\{a,b,c,e\}
,
\{a,c,d,e\}
,
\{b,c,d,e\}
and
\{a,b,c,d,e\}
.

\bullet

[a,b]^{2}
is the set of all subsets of an integer set
[a,b]
for
a<b
and
a,b\in Z^{0}
.

\bullet

Let
S(k_{i},d_{i})^{q}_{1}=\{k_{i},k_{i}+d_{i},\dots,k_{i}+(q-1)d_{i}\}
be an
integer set
for integers
k_{i}\geq 1
and
d_{i}\geq 1
, and let
\{(k_{i},d_{i})\}^{m}_{1}=\{(k_{1},d_{1}),(k_{2},d_{2}),\dots,(k_{m},d_{m})\}
be a
matching-pair sequence
. In some articles,
S_{q-1,k,d}=\{k,k+d,k+2d,\dots,k+(q-1)d\}
for integers
q\geq 1
,
k\geq 0
and
d\geq 1
.

\bullet

Topsnut-gpws
. Topsnut-gpws is the abbreviation of “graphical passwords based on topological structure and number theory” in Topological Coding, a mixture subbranch of graph theory and cryptography (Ref.
[
51
]
,
[
96
]
,
[
97
]
and
[
72
]
).

\bullet

A
sequence

\textbf{{d}}=(m_{1},m_{2},\dots,m_{n})=(m_{k})^{n}_{k=1}
consists of positive integers
m_{1},m_{2},\dots,m_{n}
. If a graph
G
has its
degree-sequence

{\textrm{deg}}(G)=\textbf{{d}}
, then
d
is
graphical
, we call
d

degree-sequence
, each
m_{i}
a
degree component
, and
n=L_{ength}(\textbf{{d}})
the
length
of
d
.

\bullet

A
caterpillar

T
is a tree, after removing all of leaves of the caterpillar
T
, the remainder is just a
path
; a
lobster
is tree, and the deletion of all leaves of the lobster produces a caterpillar.

\bullet

\textrm{gcd}(a,b)
is the
maximal common factor
between two positive integers
a
and
b
.

Lemma 1
.

[
4
]
(Erdös-Galia Theorem) A sequence
\textbf{{d}}=(m_{k})^{n}_{k=1}
with
m_{i}\geq m_{i+1}\geq 0
to be the
degree-sequence
of a
(p,q)
-graph graph
G
if and only if the sum
2q=\sum^{n}_{i=1}m_{i}
and

\sum^{k}_{i=1}m_{i}\leq k(k-1)+\sum^{n}_{j=k+1}\min\{k,m_{j}\}.

(1)

Definition 1
.

Let
G
be a
(p,q)
-graph. There are the following basic graph operations:

Opera-1.

[
54
]
A
vertex-splitting operation
is defined in the way: Vertex-split a vertex
u
of
G
into two vertices
u\,^{\prime},u\,^{\prime\prime}
such that
N(u\,^{\prime})=\{x_{1},x_{2},\dots,x_{j}\}
and
N(u\,^{\prime\prime})=\{v,x_{j+1},x_{j+2},\dots,x_{t}\}
with
N(u\,^{\prime})\cap N(u\,^{\prime\prime})=\emptyset
and
N(u)=N(u\,^{\prime})\cup N(u\,^{\prime\prime})
; the resultant graph is written as
G\wedge x
, named as a
vertex-split graph
, see Fig.
1
from (a) to (c), and moreover
|V(G\wedge x)|=|V(G)|+1
,
|E(G\wedge x)|=|E(G)|
.

Opera-2.

[
54
]
A
(non-common neighbor) vertex-coinciding operation
is defined by coinciding two vertices
u\,^{\prime}
and
u\,^{\prime\prime}
in to one
x=u\,^{\prime}\odot u\,^{\prime\prime}
if
N(u\,^{\prime})\cap N(u\,^{\prime\prime})=\emptyset
such that
N(x)=N(u\,^{\prime})\cup N(u\,^{\prime\prime})
; the resultant graph is written as
G(u\,^{\prime}\odot u\,^{\prime\prime})
, called
vertex-coincided graph
, see Fig.
1
from (c) to (a), and moreover
|V(G(u\,^{\prime}\odot u\,^{\prime\prime}))|=|V(G)|-1
,
|E(G(u\,^{\prime}\odot u\,^{\prime\prime}))|=|E(G)|
.

Opera-3.

[
49
]
A
leaf-splitting operation
is defined as: Let
uv
be an edge of the graph
G
with complex degrees
\textrm{deg}_{G}(u)\geq 1
and
\textrm{deg}_{G}(v)\geq 1
. A
leaf-splitting operation
is defined as: Remove the edge
uv
from
G
, and add a new leaf
v\,^{\prime\prime}
, next join it with the vertex
u~(=u\,^{\prime\prime})
of the graph
G-uv
by a new edge
uv\,^{\prime}
, and then add another new leaf
u\,^{\prime}
to join it with the vertex
v~(=v\,^{\prime\prime})
of
G-uv
by another new edge
vu\,^{\prime}
, the resultant graph is written as
G\overline{\wedge}uv
, see Fig.
1
from (a) to (b) for understanding the leaf-splitting operation. And
|V(G\overline{\wedge}uv)|=|V(G)|+2
,
|E(G\overline{\wedge}uv)|=|E(G)|+1
.

Opera-4.

[
49
]
A
leaf-coinciding operation
is defined as: For a graph graph
H
having two leaf-edges
uv\,^{\prime}
and
u\,^{\prime}v
with degrees
\textrm{deg}_{H}(u)\geq 2
and
\textrm{deg}_{H}(v\,^{\prime})=1
, as well as
\textrm{deg}_{H}(u\,^{\prime})=1
and
\textrm{deg}_{H}(v)\geq 2
, if
[N(u)\setminus\{v\,^{\prime}\}]\cap[N(v)\setminus\{u\,^{\prime}\}]
, we coincide two edges
uv\,^{\prime}
and
u\,^{\prime}v
into one edge
uv=uv\,^{\prime}\overline{\ominus}u\,^{\prime}v
with
u=u\odot u\,^{\prime}
and
v=v\odot v\,^{\prime}
. The new graph is written as
H(uv\,^{\prime}\overline{\ominus}vu\,^{\prime})
, and the process of obtaining
H(uv\,^{\prime}\overline{\ominus}vu\,^{\prime})
is called a
leaf-coinciding operation
, see Fig.
1
from (b) to (a), and moreover
|V(H(uv\,^{\prime}\overline{\ominus}vu\,^{\prime}))|=|V(H)|-2
,
|E(H(uv\,^{\prime}\overline{\ominus}vu\,^{\prime}))|=|E(H)|-1
. This operation is very similar with the connection of two train hooks, see Fig.
1
(e).

Opera-5.

[
54
]
An
edge-splitting operation
is defined as: Split the edge
uv
of
G
into two edges
u\,^{\prime}v\,^{\prime}
and
u\,^{\prime\prime}v\,^{\prime\prime}
such that neighbor sets
N(u\,^{\prime})=\{v\,^{\prime},x_{1},x_{2},\dots,x_{j}\}
and
N(u\,^{\prime\prime})=\{v\,^{\prime\prime},x_{j+1},x_{j+2},\dots,x_{t}\}
with
N(u)=N(u\,^{\prime})\cup N(u\,^{\prime\prime})
,
N(v\,^{\prime})=\{u\,^{\prime},w_{1},w_{2},\dots,w_{k}\}
and
N(v\,^{\prime\prime})=\{u\,^{\prime\prime},w_{k+1},w_{k+2},\dots,w_{m}\}
with
N(v)=N(v\,^{\prime})\cup N(v\,^{\prime\prime})
; the resultant graph is written as
G\wedge uv
, named as an
edge-split graph
, with
N(v\,^{\prime})\cap N(u\,^{\prime})=\emptyset
,
N(v\,^{\prime})\cap N(u\,^{\prime\prime})=\emptyset
, and
N(u\,^{\prime\prime})\cap N(v\,^{\prime})=\emptyset
,
N(u\,^{\prime})\cap N(v\,^{\prime\prime})=\emptyset
, see Fig.
1
from (a)
G
to (d)
T
, and moreover
|V(G\wedge uv)|=2+|V(G)|
,
|E(G\wedge uv)|=|E(G)|+1
. Especially, if two neighbor sets
N(u\,^{\prime\prime})=\{v\,^{\prime\prime}\}
and
N(v\,^{\prime\prime})=\{u\,^{\prime\prime},w_{1},w_{2},\dots,w_{m}\}
, as well as two neighbor sets
N(v\,^{\prime})=\{u\,^{\prime}\}
and
N(u\,^{\prime})=\{v\,^{\prime},x_{1},x_{2},\dots,x_{t}\}
, we say
G\wedge uv
is the result of doing a leaf-splitting operation, here
\textrm{deg}(u\,^{\prime\prime})=1
and
\textrm{deg}(v\,^{\prime})=1
.

Opera-6.

[
54
]
An
edge-coinciding operation
is defined by coinciding two edges
u\,^{\prime}v\,^{\prime}
and
u\,^{\prime\prime}v\,^{\prime\prime}
of
G
into one edge
uv=u\,^{\prime}v\,^{\prime}\overline{\ominus}u\,^{\prime\prime}v\,^{\prime\prime}
, when
N(u\,^{\prime})\cap N(u\,^{\prime\prime})=\emptyset
and
N(v\,^{\prime})\cap N(v\,^{\prime\prime})=\emptyset
, such that
N(u)=N(u\,^{\prime})\cup N(u\,^{\prime\prime})\cup\{v=(v\,^{\prime}\odot v\,^{\prime\prime})\}
and
N(v)=N(v\,^{\prime})\cup N(v\,^{\prime\prime})\cup\{u=(u\,^{\prime}\odot u\,^{\prime\prime})\}
; the resultant graph is written as
G(u\,^{\prime}v\,^{\prime}\overline{\ominus}u\,^{\prime\prime}v\,^{\prime\prime})
, called an
edge-coincided graph
, see Fig.
1
from (d) to (a), and moreover
|V(G(u\,^{\prime}v\,^{\prime}\overline{\ominus}u\,^{\prime\prime}v\,^{\prime\prime})|=|V(G)|-2
,
|E(G(u\,^{\prime}v\,^{\prime}\overline{\ominus}u\,^{\prime\prime}v\,^{\prime\prime})|=|E(G)|-1
.

Opera-7.

If two vertex-disjoint colored graphs
G
and
H
have
k
pairs of vertices with each pair of vertices colored with the same colors, then we, by the vertex-coinciding operation, vertex-coincide each pair of vertices from
G
and
H
into one, the resultant graph is denoted as
\odot_{k}\langle G,H\rangle
(called
vertex-coincided graph
), and moreover
|V(\odot_{k}\langle G,H\rangle)|=|V(G)|+|V(H)|-k
,
|E(\odot_{k}\langle G,H\rangle)|=|E(G)|+|E(H)|
. Clearly,
\odot_{k}\langle G,H\rangle
holds for vertex-disjoint uncolored graphs
G
and
H
too.
\square

Figure 1:
Six graph operations.

Theorem 2
.

[
107
]
For the vertex-splitting connectivity
\kappa_{split}(G)
and the leaf-splitting connectivity
\kappa^{leaf}_{split}(G)
of a connected graph
G
, we have

\kappa_{split}(G)=\kappa(G)\quad\textrm{and}\quad\kappa^{leaf}_{split}(G)=\kappa\,^{\prime}(G),

(2)

where
\kappa(G)
is the connectivity and
\kappa\,^{\prime}(G)
is the edge connectivity of the connected graph
G

[
11
,
4
]
.

Theorem 3
.

∗
Suppose that two connected graphs
G
and
H
admit a mapping
f:V(G)\rightarrow V(H)
. In general, a vertex-split graph
G\wedge u
with
\textrm{deg}_{G}(u)\geq 2
is not unique, so we have a vertex-split graph set
S_{G}(u)=\{G\wedge u\}
, similarly, we have another vertex-split graph set
S_{H}(f(u))=\{H\wedge f(u)\}
. If each vertex-split graph
L\in S_{G}(u)
corresponds another vertex-split graph
T\in S_{H}(f(u))
such that
L\cong T
, and vice versa, we write this fact as

G\wedge u\cong H\wedge f(u),

(3)

thereby,
G
is
isomorphic
to
H
, namely,
G\cong H
.
\parallel

Definition 2
.

[
21
]
A
lattice
is a set

\textrm{{L}}(\textbf{B})=\left\{\sum^{m}_{i=1}x_{i}\textbf{b}_{i}:~x_{i}\in Z,1\leq i\leq n\right\}

(4)

based on a
lattice base

\textbf{B}=(\textbf{b}_{1},\textbf{b}_{2},\dots,\textbf{b}_{n})
consisted of linear independent vectors
\textbf{b}_{1},\textbf{b}_{2},\dots,\textbf{b}_{n}
in
R^{m}
with
n\leq m
. If each component of each vector
\textbf{b}_{i}
in the base
B
is an integer, we call
\textrm{{L}}(\textbf{B})
an
integer lattice
, denoted as
\textrm{{L}}(\textbf{ZB})
for distinguishing.
\square

1.3
Basic colorings and labelings, Conjectures

All
colorings
and
labelings
mentioned in this article are on graphs of graph theory, very often, people say
graph colorings
and
graph labelings

[
8
]
.

1.3.1
Basic labelings

Definition 3
.

[
46
]
A
(p,q)
-graph
G
admits a bijection
f:V(G)\rightarrow[m,n]
or
f:V(G)\cup E(G)\rightarrow[m,n]
, we denoted the set of colored vertices of
G
as
f(V(G))=\{f(x):x\in V(G)\}
, the set of colored edges of graph
G
as
f(E(G))=\{f(uv):uv\in E(G)\}
. If
|f(V(G))|=p
, then
f
is called a
vertex normal labeling
of
G
; when as
|f(E(G))|=q
,
f
is called an
edge normal labeling
of
G
; and as
|f(V(G)\cup E(G))|=p+q
, then
f
is called a
totally normal labeling
.
\square

Definition 4
.

[
83
]
A graph
G
admits a coloring
h:S\subseteq V(G)\cup E(G)\rightarrow[a,b]
, and write the color set
h(S)=\{h(x):x\in S\}
. The
dual coloring

h\,^{\prime}
of the coloring
h
is defined as:
h\,^{\prime}(z)=\max h(S)+\min h(S)-h(z)
for
z\in S
. Moreover,
h(S)
is called the
vertex color set
if
S=V(G)
,
h(S)
the
edge color set
if
S=E(G)
, and
h(S)
a
universal color set
if
S=V(G)\cup E(G)
. Furthermore, if
G
is a bipartite graph with its vertex set bipartition
(X,Y)
, and holds
\max h(X)<\min h(Y)
true, we call
h
a
set-ordered coloring
(resp.
set-ordered labeling
) of
G
.
\square

We restate basic
W
-type labelings as follows:

Definition 5
.

[
8
,
54
,
83
,
123
]
Suppose that a connected
(p,q)
-graph
G
admits a mapping
\theta:V(G)\rightarrow\{0,1,2,\dots\}
. For each edge
xy\in E(G)
, the induced edge color is defined as
\theta(xy)=|\theta(x)-\theta(y)|
. Write vertex color set by
\theta(V(G))=\{\theta(u):u\in V(G)\}
, and edge color set by

\theta(E(G))=\{\theta(xy):xy\in E(G)\}
. There are the following constraint conditions:

B-1.
|\theta(V(G))|=p
;

B-2.
\theta(V(G))\subseteq[0,q]
,
\min\theta(V(G))=0
;

B-3.
\theta(V(G))\subset[0,2q-1]
,
\min\theta(V(G))=0
;

B-4.
\theta(E(G))=\{\theta(xy):xy\in E(G)\}=[1,q]
;

B-5.
\theta(E(G))=\{\theta(xy):xy\in E(G)\}=[1,2q-1]^{o}
;

B-6.
G
is a bipartite graph with the bipartition
(X,Y)
such that
\max\{\theta(x):x\in X\}<\min\{\theta(y):y\in Y\}
(
\max\theta(X)<\min\theta(Y)
for short);

B-7.
G
is a tree having a perfect matching
M
such that
\theta(x)+\theta(y)=q
for each matching edge
xy\in M
; and

B-8.
G
is a tree having a perfect matching
M
such that
\theta(x)+\theta(y)=2q-1
for each matching edge
xy\in M
.

Then
:

Blab-1.

A
graceful labeling

\theta
satisfies B-1, B-2 and B-4.

Blab-2.

A
set-ordered graceful labeling

\theta
holds B-1, B-2, B-4 and B-6 true.

Blab-3.

A
strongly graceful labeling

\theta
holds B-1, B-2, B-4 and
B-7 true.

Blab-4.

A
set-ordered strongly graceful labeling

\theta
holds B-1, B-2, B-4, B-6 and B-7 true.

Blab-5.

An
odd-graceful labeling

\theta
holds B-1, B-3 and B-5 true.

Blab-6.

A
set-ordered odd-graceful labeling

\theta
holds B-1, B-3, B-5
and B-6 true.

Blab-7.

A
strongly odd-graceful labeling

\theta
holds B-1, B-3,
B-5 and B-8 true.

Blab-8.

A
set-ordered strongly odd-graceful labeling

\theta
holds B-1, B-3, B-5, B-6 and B-8 true.
\square

Examples for understanding Definition
5
, the red edges are the matching edges.

Definition 6
.

Let
G
be a
(p,q)
-graph.

(1)
[
8
]
An
edge-magic total labeling

f
of
G
holds
f(V(G)\cup E(G))=[1,p+q]
such that for any edge
uv\in E(G)
,
f(u)+f(uv)+f(v)=c
, where the magic constant
c
is a fixed positive integer; and furthermore
f
is
a super edge-magic total labeling
if
f(V(G))=[1,p]
.

(2)
[
8
]
A
felicitous labeling

f
of
G
holds
f(V(G))\subset[0,q]
,
f(u)\neq f(v)
for distinct
u,v\in V(G)
, and all edge colors
f(uv)=f(u)+f(v)~(\bmod~q)
for
uv\in E(G)
are distinct from each other; and furthermore
f
is
q super felicitous labeling
if
f(V(G))=[1,p]
.

(3)
[
121
]
An
odd-elegant labeling

f
of
G
holds
f(V(G))\subset[0,2q-1]
,
f(u)\neq f(v)
for distinct
u,v\in V(G)
, and
f(E(G))=\{f(uv)=f(u)+f(v)~(\bmod~2q):uv\in E(G)\}=[1,2q-1]^{o}
.

(4)
[
8
]
A
k
-graceful labeling

f
of
G
holds
f(V(G))\subset[0,q+k-1]
,
|f(V(G))|=p
and
f(E(G))=\{f(uv)=|f(u)-f(v)|:uv\in E(G)\}=[k,q+k-1]
true.
\square

Definition 7
.

[
8
]
Let
G_{i}
be a
(p,q)
-graph for
i\in[1,m]
, and integers
k_{i}\geq 1
and
d_{i}\geq 1
.

(1) A
(k_{i},d_{i})
-graceful labeling

f
of
G_{i}
hold
f(V(G_{i}))\subseteq[0,k_{i}+(q-1)d_{i}]
,
f(x)\neq f(y)
for distinct
x,y\in V(G_{i})
and
\pi(E(G_{i}))=\{|\pi(u)-\pi(v)|:\ uv\in E(G_{i})\}=S(k_{i},d_{i})^{q}_{1}
.

(2) A labeling
f
of
G_{i}
is said to be
(k_{i},d_{i})
-
arithmetic
if
f(V(G_{i}))\subseteq[0,k_{i}+(q-1)d_{i}]
,
f(x)\neq f(y)
for distinct vertices
x,y\in V(G_{i})
and
\{f(u)+f(v):uv\in E(G_{i})\}=S(k_{i},d_{i})^{q}_{1}
.

(3) A
(k_{i},d_{i})
-
edge antimagic total labeling

f
of
G_{i}
hold
f(V(G_{i})\cup E(G_{i}))=[1,p+q]
and
\{f(u)+f(v)+f(uv):uv\in E(G_{i})\}=S(k_{i},d_{i})^{q}_{1}
, and furthermore
f
is
super
if
f(V(G_{i}))=[1,p]
.

(4) A
(k_{i},d_{i})
-harmonious labeling
of a
(p,q)
-graph
G_{i}
is defined by a mapping
h:V(G)\rightarrow[0,k+(q-1)d_{i}]
with
k_{i},d_{i}\geq 1
, such that
f(x)\neq f(y)
for any pair of vertices
x,y
of
G
,
h(u)+h(v)(\bmod^{*}~qd_{i})
means that
h(uv)-k=[h(u)+h(v)-k](\bmod~qd_{i})
for each edge
uv\in E(G)
, and the edge color set
h(E(G))=S(k_{i},d_{i})^{q}_{1}
holds true.

(5) In
[
10
]
, a labeling
f
of
G
is said to be
(k,d)
-arithmetic
if the vertex labels are distinct nonnegative integers and the edge labels induced by
f(x)+f(y)
for each edge
xy
are
k,k+d,k+2d,\dots,k+(q-1)d
.
\square

Remark 1
.

A
Topsnut-gpw sequence

\{G_{(k_{i},d_{i})}\}^{m}_{1}
is made by an integer sequence
\{(k_{i},d_{i})\}^{m}_{1}
and a
(p,q)
-graph
G
, where each Topsnut-gpw
G_{(k_{i},d_{i})}\cong G
, and each Topsnut-gpw
G_{(k_{i},d_{i})}\in\{G_{(k_{i},d_{i})}\}^{m}_{1}
admits one labeling of four parameterized labelings defined in Definition
7
. A Topsnut-gpw sequence
\{G_{(k_{i},d_{i})}\}^{m}_{1}
has the following properties:

(i)
\{(k_{i},d_{i})\}^{m}_{1}
is a random sequence holding a group of
restrictions
.

(ii)
G_{(k_{i},d_{i})}\cong G
is a
structural regularity
.

(iii) Each
G_{(k_{i},d_{i})}\in\{G_{(k_{i},d_{i})}\}^{m}_{1}
admits randomly one labeling defined in Definition
7
.

(iv) Each
G_{(k_{i},d_{i})}\in\{G_{(k_{i},d_{i})}\}^{m}_{1}
has its matching
H_{(k_{i},d_{i})}\in\{H_{(k_{i},d_{i})}\}^{m}_{1}
under the meaning of dual labeling, image-labeling, inverse labeling and twin labeling, and so on.
\parallel

.

,

,

,

,

-graph

\in\{

.
\square

1.3.2
Basic colorings

Definition 9
.

∗
For a
(p,q)
-graph
G
, let
f:V(G)\rightarrow[a,b]
and
g:E(G)\rightarrow[a,b]
be two mappings of
G
, and write colored vertex set as
f(V(G))=\{f(w):w\in V(G)\}
and colored edge set
g(E(G))=\{g(uv):uv\in E(G)\}
, and moreover let
h=(f,g)
be a
compound mapping
induced by both
f
and
g
, write
h(V(G)\cup E(G))=f(V(G))\cup g(E(G))
. We have the following constraint conditions:

Cond-1.

|f(V(G))|=p
;

Cond-2.

|g(E(G))|=q
;

Cond-3.

|f(V(G))|<p
;

Cond-4.

|g(E(G))|<q
;

Cond-5.

f(u)\neq f(v)
for any edge
uv\in E(G)
; and

Cond-6.

g(uv)\neq g(uw)
for any pair of adjacent edges
uv,uw\in E(G)
.

Then
:

CL-1.

G
admits a
labeling

f
if Cond-
1
holds true, namely,
f(x)\neq f(y)
for any pair of distinct vertices
x,y\in V(G)
.

CL-2.

G
admits a
coloring

f
if Cond-
3
holds true, namely,
f(u)=f(w)
for some two distinct vertices
u,w\in V(G)
.

CL-3.

G
admits a
proper coloring

f
if Cond-
3
and Cond-
5
hold true.

CL-4.

G
admits a
proper edge coloring

g
if Cond-
4
and Cond-
6
hold true.

CL-5.

G
admits a
total labeling

h=(f,g)
if both Cond-
1
and Cond-
2
hold true.

CL-6.

G
admits a
total coloring

h=(f,g)
if one of Cond-
3
and Cond-
4
holds true.

CL-7.

G
admits a
proper total coloring

h=(f,g)
if one of Cond-
3
and Cond-
4
holds true, and both Cond-
5
and Cond-
6
hold true.

CL-8.

G
admits a
v-coloring e-labeling

h=(f,g)
if Cond-
3
and Cond-
2
hold true.

CL-9.

G
admits an
e-coloring v-labeling

h=(f,g)
if Cond-
1
and Cond-
4
hold true.

CL-10.

G
admits a
v-proper-coloring e-labeling

h=(f,g)
if Cond-
3
, Cond-
2
and Cond-
5
hold true.

CL-11.

G
admits an
e-proper-coloring v-labeling

h=(f,g)
if Cond-
1
, Cond-
4
and Cond-
6
hold true.
\square

Remark 2
.

A total labeling can be transformed into a proper total coloring through a series of v-coloring e-labelings, e-coloring v-labelings, v-proper-coloring e-labelings and e-proper-coloring v-labelings. In fact, a proper total coloring is a composition of v-proper-coloring and e-proper-coloring. There are three parameters for various proper colorings: the number
\chi(G)=\min_{f}\{|b-a|:f(V(G))\subseteq[a,b]\}
over all proper colorings
f
of a graph
G
is called
chromatic number
; the number
\chi\,^{\prime}(G)=\min_{g}\{|b-a|:g(E(G))\subseteq[a,b]\}
over all proper edge colorings
g
of
G
is called
chromatic index
; and the number
\chi\,^{\prime\prime}(G)=\min_{h}\{|b-a|:f(V(G))\subseteq[a,b],g(E(G))\subseteq[a,b]\}
over all proper total colorings
h=(f,g)
of
G
is called
total chromatic number
.

Since there are hundreds of mutually different labelings and mutually different colorings, the sentence “a
W
-type coloring” or “a
W
-type labeling” stands for one of the existing colorings and labelings of graph theory hereafter.
\parallel

Definition 10
.

[
19
]
If there exists a constant
k\geq 0
, such that a
(p,q)
-graph
G
admits a total labeling
f:V(G)\cup E(G)\rightarrow[1,p+q]
, each edge
uv\in E(G)
holds

|f(u)+f(v)-f(uv)|=k
, and
f(V(G)\cup E(G))=[1,p+q]
true, we call
f
an
edge-magic graceful labeling
of
G
, and
k
a
magic constant
. Moreover,
f
is called a
super edge-magic graceful labeling
if
f(V(G))=[1,p]
.
\square

Definition 11
.

[
80
]
A
Topcode-matrix
(or
topological coding matrix
) is defined as

\centering{\begin{split}T_{code}=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccccc}x_{1}&x_{2}&\cdots&x_{q}\\
e_{1}&e_{2}&\cdots&e_{q}\\
y_{1}&y_{2}&\cdots&y_{q}\end{array}\right)_{3\times q}=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}X\\
E\\
Y\end{array}\right)=(X,~E,~Y)^{T}\end{split}}\@add@centering

(5)

where
v-vector

X=(x_{1},x_{2},\cdots,x_{q})
,
e-vector

E=(e_{1}
,
e_{2}
,
\cdots
,
e_{q})
, and
v-vector

Y=(y_{1},y_{2},\cdots,y_{q})
consist of non-negative integers
e_{i}
,
x_{i}
and
y_{i}
for
i\in[1,q]
. We say
T_{code}
to be
evaluated
if there exists a function
f
such that
e_{i}=f(x_{i},y_{i})
for
i\in[1,q]
, and call
x_{i}
and
y_{i}
to be the
ends
of
e_{i}
, and
q
is the
size
of
T_{code}
.
\square

1.4
Famous conjectures

A good conjecture has something like a magnetic pull for the mind of a mathematician. At its best, a mathematical conjecture states something extremely profound in an extremely precise and succinct way, crying out for proof or disproof. Mordechai Rorvig pointed: “
Given more conjectures, there is more grist for the mill of the mathematical mind, more for mathematicians to prove and explain
”.
The following long-standing conjectures can be found in
[
4
]
,
[
8
]
and the books of graph theory.

Conjecture 1
.

(Alexander Rosa, 1966) Each tree admits a graceful labeling.

Conjecture 2
.

(Cahit, 1994) Each tree admits an ordered graceful labeling.

Conjecture 3
.

(H. J. Broersma and C. Hoede, 1999) Every tree containing a perfect matching admits a strongly
graceful labeling.

Conjecture 4
.

(Bermond, 1979) Every lobster admits a graceful labeling.

Conjecture 5
.

(Truszczyński) All unicyclic connected graphs, except
C_{n}
,
n=1
or
2

(\emph{mod}\ 4)
, admit graceful labelings.

Conjecture 6
.

(R.B. Gnanajothi, 1991) Any tree admits an odd-graceful labeling.

Conjecture 7
.

(Kelly-Ulam’s Reconstruction Conjecture, 1942) Let both
G
and
H
be graphs with
n
vertices. If there is a bijection
f
:
V(G)\rightarrow V(H)
such that
G-u\cong H-f(u)
for each vertex
u\in V(G)
, then
G\cong H
.

Conjecture 8
.

(Gyárás and Lehel, 1978; Béla Bollobás, 1995) For integer
n\geq 3
, given
n
vertex-disjoint trees
T_{k}
of
k
vertices with respect to
1\leq k\leq n
. Then complete graph
K_{n}
can be decomposed into the union of
n
edge-disjoint trees
H_{k}
, namely
K_{n}=\bigcup^{n}_{k=1}H_{k}
, such that
T_{k}\cong H_{k}
whenever
1\leq k\leq n
. Also, write this case as
\langle T_{1},T_{2},\dots,T_{m}\mid K_{n}\rangle
.

Conjecture 9
.

(A. G. Chetwynd and A. J. W. Hilton, 1985) Let
G
be a graph on
2m
vertices. If
G
is a regular graph with degree at
least
m
(when
m
is odd) or
m-1
(when
m
is even), then
G
is
1
-factorable, as the
1
-Factorization
Conjecture
.

Conjecture 10
.

(Anton Kotzig, 1964; Perfect 1-Factorization Conjecture) For integer
n\geq 2
, complete graph
K_{2n}
can be decomposed into
2n-1
perfect matchings such that the union of any two matchings forms a hamiltonian cycle of
K_{2n}
.

Conjecture 11
.

(Hajós, 1961) If
\chi(G)=m
, then
G
contains a subdivision of
K_{m}
.

Conjecture 12
.

(Hadwiger, 1943) If
\chi(G)=m
, then
G
is “contractible” to a graph which contains
K_{m}
.

Conjecture 13
.

(Bruce Reed, 1998)
\chi(G)\leq\big\lceil\frac{1}{2}(\Delta(G)+1+K(G))\big\rceil
, where
K(G)
is the clique number of
G
, that is, it is maximal one of vertex numbers of complete graphs contained by
G
.

Remark 3
.

Rosa
[
28
]
said: “
When does a combinatorial problem become a disease? Certainly the extreme ease of formulating the problem has something to do with it: most identified “diseases” are understandable to undergraduates or even to good high school students. They are highly contagious, and so they attract the attention of not only professional mathematicians but also of scores of layman mathematicians
.”

The origins of graceful labelings lie in the problem of packing isomorphic copies of a given tree into a complete graph (Gerhard Ringel and Anton Kotzig, 1963; Alexander Rosa, 1967). If each tree admits a graceful labeling, then this will settle a longstanding and well-known Ringel-Kotzig Decomposition Conjecture
[
27
]
: “
A complete graph
K_{2n+1}
can be decomposed into
2n+1
subgraphs that are all isomorphic with a given tree having
n
edges.
” (see Fig.
3
,
E(K_{9})=\bigcup^{9}_{k=1}E(T_{k})
)

In Wikipedia (TheFreeDictionary.com mirror): (1) Deciding whether the clique number of a graph is greater than or equal to
k
for
k\geq 3
is NP-complete.
(2) Deciding subgraph is NP-complete. (3) Others’ NP-complete problems are: Complete coloring (
[
23
,
25
]
),
b
-coloring, Edge-coloring
[
12
]
, Strong edge coloring
[
24
]
, Graph isomorphism, Cut (graph theory), Maximum common subgraph isomorphism problem, K-edge-connected graph, Cubic graph, Clique (graph theory), Vertex cover problem, Graph isomorphism, Hamiltonian path problem, and so on.
\parallel

Figure 3:
An example for illustrating the Ringel-Kotzig Decomposition Conjecture,
E(K_{9})=\bigcup^{9}_{k=1}E(T_{k})
with
E(T_{i})\cap E(T_{j})=\emptyset
if
i\neq j
.

2
Recent graph labelings

Definition 12
.

[
53
]
Suppose that a connected graph
G
admit a
\theta
-labeling (resp.
\theta
-coloring), where
\theta
-labeling (resp.
\theta
-coloring) is one of the existing graph labelings (resp. the existing graph colorings). If every connected proper subgraph of
G
also admits this type of
\theta
-labeling (resp.
\theta
-coloring), then we call
G
a
perfect
\theta
-labeling graph
(resp. perfect
\theta
-coloring graph).
\square

Remark 4
.

Since a set-ordered graceful labelings is equivalent to a group of labelings on trees admitting set-ordered graceful labelings (Ref.
[
33
,
41
,
77
,
87
]
), so each caterpillar is a perfect
\theta
-labeling graph for this group of labelings. So, we can conjecture: Each tree is a perfect
\theta
-labeling graph for this group of labelings (resp. the existing graph colorings).
\parallel

2.1
Total graceful labelings

Definition 13
.

[
101
,
100
]
Suppose that a
(p,q)
-graph
G
admits a
labeling

\theta:V(G)\cup E(G)\rightarrow[1,M]
, and write

\theta(V(G))=\{\theta(u):u\in V(G)\}
,

\theta(E(G))=\{\theta(xy):xy\in E(G)\}
. There are the following
constraint conditions:

(a)

|\theta(V(G))|=p
,
|\theta(E(G))|=q
and
\theta(xy)=|\theta(x)-\theta(y)|
for every edge
xy\in E(G)
;

(b)

\theta(V(G))\cup\theta(E(G))=[1,p+q]
;

(c)

\theta(E(G))=[1,q]
;

(d)

G
is a bipartite graph with the bipartition
(X,Y)
such that
\max\{\theta(x):x\in X\}<\min\{\theta(y):y\in Y\}
(
\max\theta(X)<\min\theta(Y)
for short); and

(e)

\theta(V(G))\cup\theta(E(G))\subset[1,M]
with
M\geq 2q+1
and
\max[\theta(V(G))\cup\theta(E(G))]=M
.

Then
:

Tog-1.

A
total graceful labeling

\theta
holds (
a
) and (
b
) true.

Tog-2.

A
super total graceful labeling

\theta
holds (
a
), (
b
) and (
c
) true.

Tog-3.

A
set-ordered total graceful labeling

\theta
holds (
a
), (
b
) and (
d
) true.

Tog-4.

A
super set-ordered total graceful labeling

\theta
holds (
a
), (
b
), (
c
) and (
d
) true.

Tog-5.

A
generalized total graceful labeling

\theta
holds (
a
) and (
e
) true.

Tog-6.

A
super generalized total graceful labeling

\theta
holds (
a
), (
e
) and (
c
) true.

Tog-7.

A
set-ordered generalized total graceful labeling

\theta
holds (
a
),
(
e
) and (
d
) true.

Tog-8.

A
super set-ordered generalized total graceful labeling

\theta
holds (
a
), (
e
), (
c
) and (
d
) true.
\square

Definition 14
.

[
101
,
100
]
In Definition
13
, we substitute the constraint conditions “
\theta(V(G))\cup\theta(E(G))=[1,p+q]
and
\theta(E(G))=[1,q]
” by “
\theta(V(G))\cup\theta(E(G))\subseteq[1,p+2q-1]
and
\theta(E(G))=[1,2q-1]^{o}
”, then we get a total odd-graceful labeling, and moreover we have
W
-type total odd-graceful labelings
for
W
-type
~\in\{
super, set-ordered, super set-ordered, generalized, super generalized, set-ordered generalized, super set-ordered generalized
\}
, respectively.
\square

2.2
Twin-type of labelings

Definition 15
.

[
98
]
For two connected
(p_{i},q)
-graphs
G_{i}
with
i=1,2
, and let
p=p_{1}+p_{2}-2
, if a
(p,q)
-graph
G=\odot\langle G_{1},G_{2}\rangle
admits a vertex labeling
f
:
V(G)\rightarrow[0,q]
such that

(i)
f
is just an odd-graceful labeling of
G_{1}
, so
f(E(G_{1}))=\{f(uv)=|f(u)-f(v)|:uv\in E(G_{1})\}=[1,2q-1]^{o}
;

(ii)
f(E(G_{2}))=\{f(uv)=|f(u)-f(v)|:uv\in E(G_{2})\}=[1,2q-1]^{o}
; and

(iii)
|f(V(G_{1}))\cap f(V(G_{2}))|=k\geq 0
and
f(V(G_{1}))\cup f(V(G_{2}))\subseteq[0,2q-1]
.

Then
f
is called a
twin odd-graceful labeling
(Tog-labeling) of
G
.
\square

Figure 4:
An example for understanding Definition
15
, cited from
[
98
]
.

Definition 16
.

[
99
]
For two connected
(p_{i},q)
-graphs
G_{i}
with
i=1,2
, and let
p=p_{1}+p_{2}-2
, if a
(p,q)
-graph
G=\odot\langle G_{1},G_{2}\rangle
admits a vertex labeling
f
:
V(G)\rightarrow[0,q-1]
such that

(i)
f
is just an odd-elegant labeling of
G_{i}
with
i=1,2
;

(ii)
|f(V(G_{1}))\cap f(V(G_{2}))|=k\geq 0
and
f(V(G_{1}))\cup f(V(G_{2}))\subseteq[0,q-1]
.

Then
f
is called a
twin odd-elegant labeling
(Toe-labeling) of
G
, and
G=\odot\langle G_{1},G_{2}\rangle
is called a
Toe-matching partition
, where
G_{1}
is the
Toe-source
and
G_{2}
is a
Toe-association
.
\square

In Fig.
5
, a
(8,7)
-graph
G
admits an odd-graceful labeling
f
, each graph
H_{\textrm{(k)}}
in
\odot_{2}\langle G,H_{\textrm{(k)}}\rangle
admits an odd-elegant labeling
g_{\textrm{(k)}}
for k
=
a, b, c; and furthermore both labelings
f
and
g_{\textrm{(k)}}
are a
twin (odd-graceful, odd-elegant) labeling
. Clearly,
H_{\textrm{(k)}}\not\cong H_{\textrm{(i)}}
for
\textrm{(k)}\neq\textrm{(i)}
.

A scheme for illustrating Definition
16
, cited from
[
99
]
.

Definition 17
.

[
98
,
53
]
Suppose
f:V(G)\rightarrow[0,2q-1]
is an odd-graceful labeling of a
(p,q)
-graph
G
, and
g:V(H)\rightarrow[1,2q]
is a labeling of another
(p\,^{\prime},q\,^{\prime})
-graph
H
such that each edge
uv\in E(H)
has its own color defined as
g(uv)=|g(u)-g(v)|
and the edge color set
g(E(H))=[1,2q-1]^{o}
. We call both labelings
f
and
g
a
twin odd-graceful labeling
when
f(V(G))\cup g(V(H))\subseteq[0,2q]
, both graphs
G
and
H
are a matching of
twin odd-graceful graphs
.
\square

Definition 18
.

[
99
]
For two connected
(p_{i},q)
-graphs
G_{i}
with
i=1,2
, and let
p=p_{1}+p_{2}-2
, if a
(p,q)
-graph
G=\odot\langle G_{1},G_{2}\rangle
admits a vertex labeling
f
:
V(G)\rightarrow[0,q]
such that

(i)
f
is an odd-graceful labeling of
G_{1}
;

(ii)
f:V(G_{2})\rightarrow[0,2|E(G_{2})|]
holding
f(E(G_{2}))=\{f(uv)=f(u)+f(v)~(\bmod~2|E(G_{2})|):uv\in E(G_{2})\}=[1,2|E(G_{2})|-1]^{o}
true.

Then
f
is called a
2-odd graceful-elegant labeling
(a 2odd2-labeling) of
G
(called a
2odd2-graph
), and
\odot\langle G_{1},G_{2}\rangle
is called a
2odd2-matching partition
.
\square

Definition 19
.

[
53
]
A
(p,q)
-graph
G
admits a
(k,d)
-labeling
f
, and another
(p\,^{\prime},q\,^{\prime})
-graph
H
admits another
(k,d)
-labeling
g
. If
(X_{0}\cup S_{q-1,k,d})\setminus f(V(G)\cup E(G))=g(V(H)\cup E(H))
with
X_{0}=\{0,d,2d,\dots,(q-1)d\}
and
S_{q-1,k,d}
, then
g
is called a
complementary
(k,d)
-labeling
of
f
, and both
f
and
g
are a
twin
(k,d)
-labeling
.
\square

Definition 20
.

[
68
]
If a
(p,q)
-graph
G
admits a
W_{f}
-type coloring
f:V(G)\cup E(G)\rightarrow[a,A]
, and another
(p\,^{\prime},q\,^{\prime})
-graph
H

W_{g}
-type coloring
g:V(H)\cup E(H)\rightarrow[a,A]
, such that
f(V(G))\cup f(V(H))\subseteq[a,A]
, and
k=|f(V(G))\cap f(V(H))|
, we call both colorings
f
and
g
a
twin (
W_{f}
,
W_{g}
)-type coloring
, and

the
coinciding rank
. Moreover, we coincide a vertex
x_{i}
of
G
with a vertex
u_{i}
of

if
f(x_{i})=g(u_{i})
for

, such that the resultant graph has no two vertices colored with the same color, denoted this graph admitting the
(W_{f},W_{g})
-type coloring as
\odot_{k}\langle G,H\rangle
.
\square

Definition 21
.

[
54
]
If a
(p,q)
-graph
G
admits an e-set v-proper
W
-type coloring (resp. labeling)
(F,f)
defined by
f:V(G)\rightarrow[0,a(p,q)]
and
F:E(G)\rightarrow[0,b(p,q)]^{2}
, where
a(p,q)
and

are linear functions of

and

, such that

can be decomposed into spanning trees
T_{1},T_{2},\dots,T_{m}
with
m\geq 2

(allow

for some

), and each spanning tree

admits a

-type coloring (resp. labeling)

(F,f)
. We call

a
multiple-tree matching partition
, denoted as
G=\oplus_{F}\langle T_{i}\rangle^{m}_{1}
. (see Fig.

)
\square

Definition 22
.

[
54
]
If a
(p,q)

, such that

Theorem 4
.

[
54
]
If a tree
T
admits a set-ordered graceful labeling
f
, then
T
matches with a multiple-tree matching partition
\oplus_{f}\langle T_{i}\rangle^{m}_{1}
with
m\geq 10
(see Definition
22
).

Definition 23
.

[
86
,
112
]
Let
(X,Y)
be the bipartition of a complete bipartite graph
K_{n,n}
, where
X=\{u_{i}:i\in[1,n]\}
,
Y=\{v_{j}:j\in[1,n]\}
with
n\geq 2
. For integers
k,p\geq 2
,
K_{n,n}
has a labeling
\pi_{k}
defined as:
\pi_{k}(u_{i})=(k-1)n+i-1
and
\pi_{k}(v_{i})=(p-k)n+i-1
for
i\in[1,n]
with
2k<p+1
. Call two vertices
u_{t_{0}}\in X
,
v_{t_{0}}\in Y

roots
for a fixed
t_{0}\in[1,n]
. A pair of vertex-disjoint
rooted trees

T(u_{t_{0}}),T(v_{t_{0}})\subset K_{n,n}
is called a
matchable pair
, denoted as
M\langle T(u_{t_{0}}),T(v_{t_{0}})\mid k,n,p\rangle
, if the following facts hold:

(1)
u_{t_{0}}\in V(T(u_{t_{0}}))
and
v_{t_{0}}\in V(T(v_{t_{0}}))
, and
|T(u_{t_{0}})|+|T(v_{t_{0}})|=2n
;

(2)
\pi_{k}(V(T(u_{t_{0}}))\cup V(T(v_{t_{0}})))=[n(k-1),nk-1]\cup[n(p-k),n(p-k+1)-1]
; and

(3)
\pi_{k}(E(T(u_{t_{0}}))\cup E(T(v_{t_{0}})))=[n(p-2k)+1,n(p-2k+2)-1]\setminus\{n(p-2k+1)\}
.
\square

Two matchable pairs shown in Fig.
7
are for understanding Definition
23
. Fig.
7
(a) is a matchable pair

M\langle T(8),T(32)\mid 2,8,6\rangle
, where
|T(8)|+|T(32)|=16
,
\pi(V(T(8))\cup V(T(32)))=[8,15]\cup[32,39]
and
\pi(E(T(8))\cup E(T(32)))=[17,31]\setminus\{24\}
. Fig.
7
(b) shows a matchable pair
M\langle T(10),T(34)\mid 2,8,6\rangle
, where
|T(10)|+|T(34)|=16
,
\pi(V(T(10))\cup V(T(34)))=[8,15]\cup[32,39]
and
\pi(E(T(10))\cup E(T(34)))=[17,31]\setminus\{24\}
.

Figure 7:
Two illustrative examples of Definition
23
.

Theorem 5
.

Let
G
be a tree with vertices
x_{1},x_{2},\dots,x_{p}
, and
\pi
be a (set-ordered) graceful labeling of
G
with
\pi(x_{i})<\pi(x_{i+1})
for
i\in[1,p-1]
. Then

(i)
For even
p
and a collection of
\frac{p}{2}
matchable pairs
M\langle T(u_{t_{0}}),T(v_{t_{0}})\mid k,n,p\rangle
with
k\in[1,\frac{p}{2}]
, we have a (strongly, set-ordered) graceful tree obtained by identifying the roots
u_{t_{0}}
,
v_{t_{0}}
of each matchable pair
M\langle T(u_{t_{0}}),T(v_{t_{0}})\mid k,n,p\rangle
with the vertices
x_{k}
,
x_{l}
of
G
respectively, where
k+l=p+1
and
k\in[1,\frac{p}{2}]
.

(ii)
For odd
p
and a collection of
\frac{p-1}{2}
matchable pairs
M\langle T(u_{t_{0}}),T(v_{t_{0}})\mid k,n,p\rangle
with
k\in[1,\frac{p-1}{2}]
, and a (strongly, set-ordered) graceful tree
T\,^{*}
on
n
vertices, we have a (strongly, set-ordered) graceful tree obtained by identifying the roots
u_{t_{0}}
,
v_{t_{0}}
of each pair
M\langle T(u_{t_{0}}),T(v_{t_{0}})\mid k,n,p\rangle
with the vertices
x_{k}
,
x_{l}
of
G
respectively, where
k+l=p+1
and
k\in[1,\frac{p-1}{2}]
, and identifying a vertex of
T\,^{*}
with the vertex
x_{(p+1)/2}
of
G
.

Figure 8:
An example for illustrating the assertion
(ii)
of Theorem
5
, where
M\langle T(2),T(50)\mid 1,8,7\rangle
,
M\langle T(10),T(42)\mid 2,8,7\rangle
,
M\langle T(18),T(34)\mid 3,8,7\rangle
and
T\,^{*}=T(26)
, and the black vertices are the roots.

2.3
Magic-type of labelings

2.3.1
Edge-magic labelings

Definition 24
.

[
95
]
Let
G
be a bipartite
(p,q)
-graph with bipartition
(X,Y)
, and let
G
admit an
edge-magic total labeling

f
defined in Definition
6
. There are two constraints:

(C1)
f(V(G))=[1,p]
; and

(C2)
\max\{f(x):x\in X\}<\min\{f(y):y\in Y\}
(
\max f(X)<\min f(Y)
).

We call
f
:

(i)

A
super edge-magic total labeling
of
G
if
f
holds (C1) true.

(ii)

A
set-ordered edge-magic total labeling
of
G
if
f
holds (C2) true.

(iii)

A
super set-ordered edge-magic total labeling
(super-so-edge-magic total labeling) of
G
if
f
holds both (C1) and (C2) true.
\square

Definition 25
.

[
95
]
Let
G
be a
(p,q)
-graph. If there exist a constant
\mu
and a mapping
f:V(G)\cup E(G)\rightarrow[1,2q+1]
such that
f(u)+f(v)+f(uv)=\mu
for every edge
uv\in E
, then we call
f
a
generalized edge-magic total labeling
of
G
, and
\mu
a
generalized magic constant
. Furthermore, if
G
is a bipartite graph with bipartition
(X,Y)
, and the labeling
f
holds
f(V(G))=[1,q+1]
and
\max f(X)<\min f(Y)
true, we call
f
a
generalized super set-ordered edge-magic total labeling
.
\square

Definition 26
.

[
105
]
Let
G
be a
(p,q)
-graph. If there exists a bijection
f:V(G)\cup E(G)\rightarrow[1,p+q]
such that
\{f(u)+f(v)+f(uv):uv\in E(G)\}=\{k,k+d,k+2d,\cdots,k+(q-1)d\}
for some values
k,d\in Z^{0}
, then we call
f
an
anti-edge-magic total labeling
of
G
. Furthermore, if
G
is a bipartite graph with bipartition
(X,Y)
, and
f
holds
f(V(G))=[1,p]

and
\max f(X)<\min f(Y)
, we call
f
a
super set-ordered anti-edge-magic total labeling
.
\square

Definition 27
.

[
95
]
A
(p,q)
-graph
G
admits a bijection
f:V(G)\cup E(G)\rightarrow\{d,2d,\dots,\mu d,k+(\mu+1)d,k+(p+q-1)d\}
with
\mu\in[1,p+q-1]
, such that
f(u)+f(v)+f(uv)=\lambda
for each edge
uv\in E(G)
, we call
f
a
(k,d)
-edge-magic total labeling
,
\lambda
a
magic constant
. Moreover, if
G
is a bipartite graph with bipartition
(X,Y)
, and
f
holds
f(X)=\{d,2d,\dots,|X|d\}
,
f(Y)=\{k+|X|d,k+(|X|+1)d,\dots,k+(|X|+|Y|-1)d
, we call
f
a
super set-ordered
(k,d)
-edge-magic total labeling
of
G
.
\square

Definition 28
.

[
74
]
An
edge-magic total graceful labeling

g
of a
(p,q)
-graph
G
is defined as:
g:V(G)\cup E(G)\rightarrow[1,p+q]
such that
g(x)\neq g(y)
for any two elements
x,y\in V(G)\cup E(G)
, and each edge
uv\in E(G)
holds
g(uv)+|g(u)-g(v)|=k
with a constant
k
. Moreover,
g
is
super
if
\max g(E(G))<\min g(V(G))
(or
\max g(V(G))<\min g(E(G))
).
\square

Definition 29
.

[
19
]
If there exists a constant
k\geq 0
, a
(p,q)
-graph
G
admits a total labeling
f:V(G)\cup E(G)\rightarrow[1,p+q]
, such that each edge
uv\in E(G)
holds
|f(u)+f(v)-f(uv)|=k
and
f(V(G)\cup E(G))=[1,p+q]
true, we call
f
an
edge-magic graceful labeling
of
G
, and
k
a
magic constant
. Moreover,
f
is called a
super edge-magic graceful
k
-labeling
if
f(V(G))=[1,p]
.
\square

Definition 30
.

[
70
]
A
ve-exchanged matching labeling

h
of an edge-magic graceful labeling
f
of a
(p,q)
-graph
G
is defined as:
h:V(G)\cup E(G)\rightarrow[1,p+q]
, each edge
uv\in E(G)
holds
h(uv)=|h(u)-h(v)|
true (
h(uv)+|h(u)-h(v)|=k
, or
h(uv)=h(u)+h(v)~(\bmod~q)
, or
|h(u)+h(v)-h(uv)|=k
, or
h(u)+h(uv)+h(v)=k
), such that
h(V(G)\cup E(G))=[1,p+q]
,
h(V(G))\setminus\{a_{0}\}=f(E(G))
and
h(E(G))=f(V(G))\setminus\{a_{0}\}
, where
a_{0}=\lfloor\frac{p+q+1}{2}\rfloor
is the singularity of two labelings
f
and
h
.
\square

Definition 31
.

[
53
]
Suppose that a
(p,q)
-graph
G
admits an
edge-magic graceful labeling

f
, and a
(q,p)
-graph
H
admits another
edge-magic graceful labeling

g
. If
f(E(G))=g(V(H))
and
f(V(G))=g(E(H))
, we say that both labelings
f
and
g
are
ve-inverse
from each other, and both graphs
G
and
H
are
inverse
from each other under the edge-magic graceful labelings.
\square

Definition 32
.

∗
Suppose that a graph
G
admits a

and
f(V(G))=g(E(H))
, we call
(f,g)
a
ve-inverse
(W_{f},W_{g})
-type labeling
(resp. coloring).
\square

Definition 33
.

[
53
]
Suppose that a
(p,q)

L

\theta

, and a

\varphi

\theta(E(L))=\varphi(V(S))

, we say that both labelings
\theta

are

from each other, and moreover both graphs

Definition 34
.

[
29
]
A
(p,q)
-graph
G

f:V(G)\cup E(G)\rightarrow[0,2(p+q-1)]^{e}
. If there exists a constant

, such that

for each edge

, we call

an

, and

an

Definition 35
.

[
110
]
An
odd-even separable edge-magic (total) labeling

f
of a

G
is an edge-magic (total) labeling and holds
f(V(G))=[1,2L+1]^{o}
and
f(E(G))=[2,2M]^{e}
.

Definition 36
.

[
29
]
If a
(p,q)
-graph
G
admits a bijection
f:V(G)\cup E(G)\rightarrow[1,2(p+q)+1]
, and there exists an odd constant
k
such that
|f(u)+f(v)-f(uv)|=k
for each edge
uv\in E(G)
, we say
f
an
odd-totally edge-magic graceful labeling
, and
k
an
odd magic constant
.
\square

2.3.2
Parameterized magic-type total labelings

Definition 37
.

[
85
]
Let
G
be a connected
(p,q)
-graph. If there are integers
k
and
\lambda~(\neq 0)
such that a proper total labeling
f
of
G
from
V(G)\cup E(G)
to
[1,p+q]
satisfies
f(u)+f(v)=k+\lambda f(uv)
whenever
uv\in E(G)
. Then
f
is called a
(k,\lambda)
-
magically total labeling
(or
(k,\lambda)
-
mtl
for short) of
G
,
k
and
\lambda
are called a
magical constant
and a
balanced number
, respectively, and moreover
f
is
super
if the vertex color set
f(V(G))=[1,p]
, and
G
is
f
-
saturated
if
f
is no longer a
(k,\lambda)
-
mtl
of
G+uv
for any
uv\in E(\overline{G})
, where
\overline{G}
is the complement of
G
.
\square

Lemma 6
.

Let
G
be a connected
(p,q)
-graph admitting a
(k,\lambda)
-magically total labeling
f
defined in Definition
37
.

(i)
Then the complementary labeling
h
of the labeling
f
is a
(k\,^{\prime},\lambda)
-magically total labeling of
G
where the magical constant
k\,^{\prime}=(2-\lambda)(p+q+1)-k
; and

(ii)
If
f
is supper, then the partial complementary labeling
h
of
f
is a supper
(k\,^{\prime\prime},\lambda)
-magically total labeling of
G
where the magical constant
k\,^{\prime\prime}=2(p+1)-k-\lambda(2p+q+1)
.

Lemma 7
.

A connected graph
G
admits a
(k,\lambda)
-magically total labeling
f
defined in Definition
37
if and only if any two incident edges
uv
and
vw
of
G
hold
f(u)-f(w)=\lambda[f(uv)-f(vw)]
true.

Lemma 8
.

Let
G
be a connected
(p,q)
-graph admitting a supper
(k,\lambda)
-magically total labeling
f
defined in Definition
37
. We have:

(i)
If
k>0
, then
\lambda\leq-1
;

(ii)
Let
\Delta
be the maximal degree of
G
, then

\lambda\Delta\leq p-1
;

(iii)

3-\lambda(p+q)\leq k\leq 2(p+q)-\lambda-1
; and

(iv)
If
G
is regular, then

2k=2(p+1)-\lambda(2p+q+1)
.

Theorem 9
.

Let
G
be a connected
(p,q)
-graph possessing a supper
(k,\lambda)
-magically total labeling
f
defined in Definition
37
. If
\lambda\leq 2
, there then is a graph,
H=G+uw
, obtained by adding a new vertex
u
to
G
and adjoining
u
and a certain vertex
w
of
G
, such that it admits a supper
(k-\lambda,\lambda)
-magically total labeling.

Theorem 10
.

Let
\lambda\geq 1
. A graph
G
admits a supper

(k,\lambda)
-magically total labeling
f
defined in Definition
37
if and only if it admits a supper
(k\,^{\prime},-\lambda)
-magically total labeling
h
where the magical constant
k\,^{\prime}=k+\lambda(M+m)
for
m=\min\{f(uv):uv\in E(G)\}
and
M=\max\{f(uv):uv\in E(G)\}
, And
f
is supper if and only if
h
is supper too.

Theorem 11
.

Let
G
be a connected
(p,q)
-graph admitting a supper
(k,\lambda)
-magically total labeling
f
defined in Definition
37
. Then
q\leq\lceil(2p-3)/\lambda\rceil
, the equality holds if and only if
G
is
f
-saturated.

Theorem 12
.

Every caterpillar admits a supper
(k,\lambda)
-magically total labeling defined in Definition
37
.

Definition 38
.

∗
Let
G
be a connected
(p,q)
-graph. If there are integers
k
and
\lambda~(\neq 0)
such that
G
admits a proper total labeling
h:V(G)\cup E(G)\rightarrow[1,p+q]
holding
|h(u)-h(v)|=k+\lambda h(uv)
for each edge
uv\in E(G)
true, then we call
h
a
(k,\lambda)
-
edge-difference magically total labeling
of
G
,
k
a
magical constant
and
\lambda
a
balanced number
, and moreover
f
is
super
if the vertex color set
f(V(G))=[1,p]
.
\square

Remark 5
.

It is noticeable: (i) a
(k,\lambda)
-magically total labeling defined in Definition
37
implies some
edge-magic total labeling
defined in Definition
6
and some
felicitous-difference total coloring
defined in Definition
118
, since
f(u)+f(v)=k+\lambda f(uv)
is related with
f(u)+f(uv)+f(v)=k
and
f(u)+f(v)-f(uv)=k
as
\lambda=-1
.

(ii) A
(k,\lambda)
-edge-difference magically total labeling defined in Definition
38
implies some edge-magic graceful total labeling defined in Definition
52
, some edge-difference magic total coloring and some graceful ev-difference magic total coloring defined in Definition
118
, since
|h(u)-h(v)|=k+\lambda h(uv)
is related with
h(uv)+|h(u)-h(v)|=k
and
\big|h(uv)-|h(u)-h(v)|\big|=k
as
\lambda=-1
.
\parallel

2.3.3
Couple edge-magic total labelings

Let
G\,^{\prime}
be a copy of
G
, and join a vertex
u
of
G
with a vertex
u\,^{\prime}
of
G\,^{\prime}
by an edge together, the resultant graph is denoted by
\widehat{GG\,^{\prime}}
, called an
edge-symmetric graph
since
\widehat{GG\,^{\prime}}-uu\,^{\prime}
contains just two components
G
and
G\,^{\prime}
holding
G\cong G\,^{\prime}
true. We call
\widehat{GG\,^{\prime}}
a
formally edge-symmetric graph
if
u\,^{\prime}
is just the imagine vertex of
u
. An
pseudo-edge-symmetric graph

\widehat{GH}
is obtained by joining a vertex
u
of
G
with a vertex
v
of
H
by an edge if
G\not\cong H
.

Definition 39
.

[
79
]
If there are two constants
k^{+}>0
and
k^{-}\geq 0
, a
(p,q)
-graph
G
admits a bijection
f:V(G)\cup E(G)\rightarrow[1,p+q]
such that every edge
uv\in E(G)
holds one of
f(u)+f(v)+f(uv)=k^{+}
and
|f(u)+f(v)-f(uv)|=k^{-}
true, then we call
f
a
(k^{+},k^{-})
-couple edge-magic total labeling
(
(k^{+},k^{-})
-CEMTL for short).
\square

Definition 40
.

[
79
]
The
dual labeling

g
of a labeling
f
defined in Definition
39
is a
(v>e)
-
(k^{+}_{new},k^{-}_{new})
-CEMTL (resp. a
(e>v)
-
(k^{+}_{new},k^{-}_{new})
-CEMTL) if and only if
f
is an
(e>v)
-
(k^{+},k^{-})
-CEMTL (resp. a
(v>e)
-
(k^{+},k^{-})
-CEMTL), where
k^{+}_{new}=3(p+q+1)-k^{+}
and
k^{-}_{new}=p+q+1+k^{-}
(see Fig.
9
).

Lemma 13
.

[
79
]
Suppose that
T
is a tree admitting set-ordered graceful labelings. Then there exists an edge-symmetric tree
\widehat{TT\,^{\prime}}
admitting a super
(k^{+},k^{-})
-CEMTL.

Lemma 14
.

[
79
]
Suppose that a connected
(p,q)
-graph
G
admits an
(e>v)
-EMGL
f
with a magic constant
k
, there are two vertices
w,w\,^{\prime}
holding
f(w)+f(w\,^{\prime})=p+q+1-k
. Then there exists an edge-symmetric graph
\widehat{GG\,^{\prime}}
admitting a
(k^{+},k^{-})
-CEMTL.
