Left tree admits an
(e>v)
-
(33,0)
-CEMTL
f
, and Right tree admits a
(v>e)
-
(34,1)
-CEMTL.

Theorem 15
.

[
79
]
Let
T
be a graceful tree. Then there exists an edge-symmetric tree
\widehat{HH\,^{\prime}}
admitting a super
(k^{+},k^{-})
-CEMTL, where
H=\widehat{TT\,^{\prime}}
, where
T\,^{\prime}
is a copy of
T
and
H\,^{\prime}
is a copy of
H
.

Theorem 16
.

[
79
]
Suppose that both trees
T
and
H
have the same vertex number,
T
admits an EMGL,
H
admits a
(v>e)
-EMGL (resp.
(e>v)
-EMGL). Then the pseudo-edge-symmetric tree
\widehat{TH}
admits a
(k^{+},k^{-})
-CEMTL.

Theorem 17
.

[
79
]
Suppose that both graphs
G
and
H
have
p
vertices and
q
edges.
G
admits an EMGL
f
with magic constant
k_{1}
, and
H
admits an
(e>v)
-EMGL
g
with magic constant
k_{2}
. If there exist vertices
w\in V(G)
and
w\,^{\prime}\in V(H)
such that
f(w)+g(w\,^{\prime})=p+q+1-k_{1}
(resp.
f(w)+g(w\,^{\prime})=p+q+1+k_{1}
), then the pseudo-edge-symmetric graph
\widehat{GH}
obtained by joining
w
with
w\,^{\prime}
by an edge admits a
(k^{+},k^{-})
-CEMTL, where
k^{-}=2k_{1}
,
k^{+}=2(2p+q)-1-2k_{2}
.

Remark 6
.

Based on our new labelings, we have applied the labeling orders, that is,
(e>v)
and
(v>e)
here in our algorithms for building up network models admitting
(k^{+},k^{-})
-CEMTLs (
(k^{+},k^{-})
-couple edge-magic total labelings). However, many EMGLs (edge-magic graceful labelings) and
(k^{+},k^{-})
-CEMTLs have no dual labelings to be EMGLs, or
(k^{+},k^{-})
-CEMTLs. We point out that there exist graphs admitting no
(k^{+},k^{-})
-CEMTLs defined in Definition
39
.
\parallel

So we generalize Definition
39
in the following definition:

Definition 41
.

[
79
]
If there are two sequences
(k^{+}_{i})^{s}_{1}=(k^{+}_{1},k^{+}_{2},\dots,k^{+}_{s})
and
(k^{-}_{j})^{t}_{1}=(k^{-}_{1},k^{-}_{2},\dots
,
k^{-}_{t})
, a
(p,q)
-graph
G
admits a bijection
f:V(G)\cup E(G)\rightarrow[1,p+q]
such that every edge
uv\in E(G)
holds
f(u)+f(v)+f(uv)=k^{+}_{i}
or
|f(u)+f(v)-f(uv)|=k^{-}_{j}
for some
k^{+}_{i},k^{-}_{j}
, then we call
f
a
\langle(k^{+}_{i})^{s}_{1},(k^{-}_{j})^{t}_{1}\rangle
-couple edge-magic total labeling
, rewrite
\langle(k^{+}_{i})^{s}_{1},(k^{-}_{j})^{t}_{1}\rangle
-CEMTL for short.
\square

Remark 7
.

In Definition
41
, we can classify the edges of the
(p,q)
-graph
G
into two sets
E^{+}=\{uv:f(u)+f(v)+f(uv)=k^{+}_{i}\}
and
E^{-}=\{xy:|f(x)+f(y)-f(xy)|=k^{-}_{j}\}
by the labeling
f
. So,
f
is called a super
\langle(k^{+}_{i})^{s}_{1},(k^{-}_{j})^{t}_{1}\rangle
-CEMTL if
f(V(G))=[1,p]
,
f
an
(e>v)
-
\langle(k^{+}_{i})^{s}_{1},(k^{-}_{j})^{t}_{1}\rangle
-CEMTL if
f(uv)-[f(u)+f(v)]=k^{-}_{j}>0
for each edge
uv\in E^{-}
(resp. a
(v>e)
-
\langle(k^{+}_{i})^{s}_{1},(k^{-}_{j})^{t}_{1}\rangle
-CEMTL if
[f(u)+f(v)]-f(uv)=k^{-}_{j}>0
for each edge
uv\in E^{-}
). We have some particular cases: If
t=0
(resp.
s=0
),
f
is a
(k^{+}_{i})^{s}_{1}
-CEMTL (resp. a
(k^{-}_{j})^{t}_{1}
-CEMTL); if
t=0
and
s=1
,
f
is an EMTL (
edge-magic total labeling
); if
t=1
and
s=0
,
f
is an EMGL (
edge-magic graceful labeling
); if
t=1
and
s=1
,
f
is a
(k^{+},k^{-})
-CEMTL.

Let
C_{emtl}(G)=\min\{s+t\}
over all
\langle(k^{+}_{i})^{s}_{1},(k^{-}_{j})^{t}_{1}\rangle
-CEMTLs of
G
. We can confirm: “
Every simple graph admits a
\langle(k^{+}_{i})^{s}_{1},(k^{-}_{j})^{t}_{1}\rangle
-CEMTL
”. However, it seems that the work for determining
\langle(k^{+}_{i})^{s}_{1},(k^{-}_{j})^{t}_{1}\rangle
-CEMTLs of simple graphs is not slight, so is for
C_{emtl}(G)
.
\parallel

Figure 10:
The complete graph
K_{5}
admits two
\langle(k^{+}_{i})^{s}_{1},(k^{-}_{j})^{t}_{1}\rangle
-CEMTLs for illustrating Definition
41
, and
C_{emtl}(K_{5})=3
, where (a)
\langle(k^{+}_{i})^{s}_{1},(k^{-}_{j})^{t}_{1}\rangle=\langle(12),(5,6)\rangle
and (b)
\langle(k^{+}_{i})^{s}_{1},(k^{-}_{j})^{t}_{1}\rangle=\langle(25),(2,5)\rangle
.

Definition 42
.

[
79
]
If a
(p,q)
-graph
H
admits a mapping
f:V(H)\rightarrow[0,q]
such that
f(x)\neq f(y)
for any pair of distinct vertices
x,y
, and
E_{i}=\{uv\in E(H):~|f(u)-f(v)|=i\}
,
k_{i}=|E_{i}|
with
\sum^{q}_{i=1}k_{i}=q
, we call
f
a
(k_{i})^{q}_{1}
-graceful labeling
, where
(k_{i})^{q}_{1}=(k_{1},k_{2},\dots,k_{q})
. Let
K_{f}=|\{k_{i}\neq 0:~i\in[1,q]\}|
and
G_{race}(H)=\max\{K_{f}:~\textrm{all $(k_{i})^{q}_{1}$-graceful labelings $f$ of $H$}\}
. And if
K_{f}=q
,
f
is the proper graceful labeling introduced in
[
8
]
; if
K_{f}<q
,
f
is a
pseudo-graceful labeling
.

2.3.4
Magic-type of labelings, Magic-type of matching labelings

Definition 43
.

[
104
]
If a
(p,q)
-graph
G
admits a proper vertex labeling
f:V(G)\rightarrow[0,q]
and a proper edge labeling
f:E(G)\rightarrow[1,q]
, and there exist two constants
k_{1}
and
k_{2}
such that
|f(u)+f(v)-f(uv)|=k_{1}
or
|f(u)+f(v)-f(uv)|=k_{2}
for any edge
uv\in E(G)
, and
f(E(G))=[1,q]
, we call
f
a
(k_{1},k_{2})
-edge-magic graceful pseudo-labeling
(abbreviated as
(k_{1},k_{2})
-EMG pseudo-labeling).
\square

Definition 44
.

[
104
]
If a
(p,q)
-graph
G
admits a proper total labeling
f:V(G)\cup E(G)\rightarrow[1,p+q]
, and there exist two non-negative constants
k_{1}
and
k_{2}
with respect to
k_{1}<k_{2}
, such that each edge
uv\in E(G)
satisfies
|f(u)+f(v)-f(uv)|=k_{1}
or
|f(u)+f(v)-f(uv)|=k_{2}
, we call
f
a
(k_{1},k_{2})
-edge-magic graceful total labeling
(abbreviated as
(k_{1},k_{2})
-EMGTL).
\square

Definition 45
.

[
104
]
If a
(p,q)
-graph
G
admits a proper total labeling
f:V(G)\cup E(G)\rightarrow[1,p+q]
, and there exist different non-negative integers
k_{i}\geq 0
with
i\in[1,m]
, such that every edge
uv\in E(G)
satisfies
|f(u)+f(v)-f(uv)|=k_{i}
for some
k_{i}
, we call
f
a
(k_{i})^{m}_{1}
-edge-magic graceful total labeling
(
(k_{i})^{m}_{1}
-EMGTL for short).
\square

Definition 46
.

[
46
]
For a connected
(p,q)
-graph
G
, if (i) there are different integers
k_{i}\geq 0
with
i\in[1,m]
, such that
0\leq k_{1}<k_{2}<\cdots<k_{m}
; (ii)
G
admits a total labeling
f
:
V(G)\cup E(G)\rightarrow[1,p+q]
, such that each edge
uv\in E(G)
corresponds a magic constant
k_{i}
and holds
|f(u)+f(v)-f(uv)|=k_{i}
true. Then we call
f
a
(k_{i})_{1}^{m}
-
edge-magic graceful total labeling
of
G
, also, called a
generalized edge-magic total labeling
(
(k_{i})_{1}^{m}
-EMGTL for short). The number
E_{mgtl}(G)=\min\{m\}
over all
(k_{i})_{1}^{m}
-edge-magic graceful total labelings of
G
is called
edge-magic total labeling number
, and write the number
K_{mgtl}(G)=\min_{f}\{k_{m_{0}}\}
over all
(k_{i})_{1}^{m_{0}}
-edge-magic graceful total labelings
f
of
G
with
m_{0}=E_{mgtl}(G)
.
\square

Definition 47
.

[
54
]
Let
f:V(G)\cup E(G)\rightarrow[1,p+q]
be a total labeling of a
(p,q)
-graph
G
. If there is a constant
k
such that
f(u)+f(uv)+f(v)=k
, and each edge
uv
corresponds another edge
xy
holding
f(uv)=|f(x)-f(y)|
true, then we call
f
a
relaxed edge-magic total labeling
.
\square

Definition 48
.

[
54
]
Suppose that a
(p,q)
-graph
G
admits a vertex labeling
f:V(G)\rightarrow[0,2q-1]
and an edge labeling
g:E(G)\rightarrow[1,2p-1]^{o}
. If there is a constant
k
such that
f(u)+g(uv)+f(v)=k
for each edge
uv\in E(G)
, and
g(E(G))=[1,2p-1]^{o}
, then we call
(f,g)
an
odd-edge-magic matching labeling
.
\square

Definition 49
.

[
54
]
Suppose that a
(p,q)
-graph
G
admits a vertex labeling
f:V(G)\rightarrow[0,2q-1]
and an edge labeling
g:E(G)\rightarrow[1,2q-1]^{o}
, and let
s(uv)=|f(u)-f(v)|-f(uv)
for
uv\in E(G)
. If

(i) each edge
uv
corresponds an edge
u\,^{\prime}v\,^{\prime}
such that
g(uv)=|f(u\,^{\prime})-f(v\,^{\prime})|
;

(ii) and there exists a constant
k\,^{\prime}
such that each edge
xy
has a matching edge
x\,^{\prime}y\,^{\prime}
holding
s(xy)+s(x\,^{\prime}y\,^{\prime})=k\,^{\prime}
true;

(iii) there exists a constant
k
such that
f(uv)+|f(u)-f(v)|=k
for each edge
uv\in E(G)
.

Then we call
(f,g)
an
ee-difference odd-edge-magic matching labeling
.
\square

Definition 50
.

[
54
]
Suppose that a
(p,q)
-graph
G
admits a vertex labeling
f:V(G)\rightarrow[0,p-1]
and an edge labeling
g:E(G)\rightarrow[1,q]
, and let
s(uv)=|f(u)-f(v)|-g(uv)
for
uv\in E(G)
. If there are:

(i) each edge
uv
corresponds an edge
u\,^{\prime}v\,^{\prime}
such that
g(uv)=|f(u\,^{\prime})-f(v\,^{\prime})|
(resp.
g(uv)=p-|f(u\,^{\prime})-f(v\,^{\prime})|
);

(ii) and there exists a constant
k\,^{\prime\prime}
such that each edge
xy
has a matching edge
x\,^{\prime}y\,^{\prime}
holding
s(xy)+s(x\,^{\prime}y\,^{\prime})=k\,^{\prime\prime}
true;

(iii) there exists a constant
k
such that
|f(u)-f(v)|+f(uv)=k
for each edge
uv\in E(G)
;

(iv) there exists a constant
k\,^{\prime}
such that each edge
uv
matches with one vertex
w
such that
f(uv)+f(w)=k\,^{\prime}
, and each vertex
z
matches with one edge
xy
such that
f(z)+f(xy)=k\,^{\prime}
, except the
singularity

f(x_{0})=0
.

Then we name
(f,g)
an
ee-difference graceful-magic matching labeling
(Dgemm-labeling) of
G
, and
G
is called a
Dgemm-graph
.
\square

Definition 51
.

[
54
]
Let
U
be a universal graph, and its two subgraphs
G,H
of
U
hold
E(G)\cap E(H)=\emptyset
and
V(U)=V(G)\cup V(H)
true. If
E(U)=E(G)\cup E(H)
, both
G
and
H
are
U
-complementary
from each other, and moreover we call
G
to be
self-matching
(resp.
self-complementary
) if
G
is isomorphic to
H
, that is,
G\cong H
.
\square

Remark 8
.

In Definition
51
,
G
is a
public-key
, and
H
is a
private-key
,
U
is a topological authentication is
G
and
H
both are
U
-complementary from each other. We say that
G
and
H
both are
k
-vertex-coinciding
for
k=V(G)\cap V(H)
.
\parallel

2.3.5
Edge-magic total-even/total-odd graceful labelings

Definition 52
.

[
92
]
Let
G
be a connected
(p,q)
-graph. If there are a fixed even-magic constant
k
and a fixed odd-magic constant
k^{*}
:

(i)
G
admits a labeling
f:~V(G)\cup E(G)\rightarrow[0,2(p+q-1)]^{e}
, such that each edge
uv\in E(G)
holds
|f(u)+f(v)-f(uv)|=k
true, then
f
is called an
edge-magic total-even graceful labeling
;

(ii)
G
admits a labeling
\alpha:V(G)\cup E(G)\rightarrow[1,2(p+q)-1]^{o}
, such that each edge
uv\in E(G)
holds
|\alpha(x)+\alpha(y)-\alpha(xy)|=k^{*}
true, then
f
is called an
edge-magic total-odd graceful labeling
.

Moreover, if
G
is a bipartite graph with bipartition
(X,Y)
, such that
\max f(V(G))<\min f(E(G))
and
\max f(X)<\min f(Y)
, we call
f
a
set-ordered super edge-magic total-even (resp. total-odd) graceful labeling
.
\square

Definition 53
.

[
92
]
Suppose that
E(H)=E(G)\cup E(T)
with
E(G)\cap E(T)=\emptyset
for a
(p,q)
-graph
H
and its two subgraphs
G
and
T
. If the
(p,q)
-graph
H
admits a labeling
f:~V(H)\cup E(H)\rightarrow[0,2(q-1)]
such that

(i) there exists a constant
k
,
|f(u)+f(v)-f(uv)|=k
for each edge
uv\in E(G)
, and both
f(u)
and
f(v)
are even;

(ii) each edge
xy\in E(T)
induces an odd
f^{*}(xy)=|f(x)-f(y)|
;

(iii)
|f(E(G))|+|f^{*}(E(T))|=q
.

Then we call
f
an
odd-even-edge bi-labeling
, and
k
an
odd-even-edge even-magic constant
.
\square

Definition 54
.

[
30
]
A generalized
d_{m}
-uniformly lobster
T
is a lobster and has a
main path

P=a_{0}a_{1}a_{2}\cdots a_{n}
, such that the distance
d(x,P)
between any leaf
x
and the main path in
T
is just
m
, that is,
d(x,P)=m
. Let
p=|V(T)|
and
q=|E(T)|
, so
q=p-1
. If there are two positive integers
\lambda,\mu
,
T
admits a total labeling
f:V(T)\cup E(T)\rightarrow[0,p+q-1]
holding (1)
f(a_{i})+f(a_{i}a_{i+1})+f(a_{i+1})=\lambda
for
a_{i}a_{i+1}\in E(P)
; and (2) for each path
P_{m}=a_{i}x_{1}x_{2}\cdots x_{m}\subset T-E(P)
, we have

f(a_{i})+f(a_{i}x_{1})+\sum^{m}_{i=1}f(x_{i})+\sum^{m-1}_{i=1}f(x_{i}x_{i+1})=\mu

then we call
f
a
(\lambda,\mu)
-
magic total labeling
of the generalized
d_{m}
-uniformly lobster
T
.
\square

Theorem 18
.

[
30
]
By Definition
54
, each generalized
d_{m}
-uniformly lobster admits a
(\lambda,\mu)
-magic total labeling
for some pair of two positive integers
\lambda
and
\mu
.

2.4
Parameterized harmonious labeling

Gallian
[
8
]
extended the notion of harmoniousness to arbitrary finite Abelian groups as follows. Let
G
be a
(p,q)
-graph and let
H
be a finite Abelian group (under addition) of order
q
. Define
G
to be
H
-harmonious
if there is an injection
f:V(G)\rightarrow H
such that the resulting edge colors
f(xy)=f(x)+f(y)
for edge
xy\in E(G)
are distinct. Motivated from Gallian’s
H
-harmonious labeling, we define:

Definition 55
.

[
75
]
A
(k,d)
-harmonious labeling
of a
(p,q)
-graph
G
is defined by a mapping
h:V(G)\rightarrow[0,k+(q-1)d]
with
k,d\geq 1
, such that
f(x)\neq f(y)
for any pair of vertices
x,y
of
G
,
h(u)+h(v)~(\bmod^{*}qd)
means that
h(uv)-k=[h(u)+h(v)-k](\bmod~qd)
for each edge
uv\in E(G)
, and the edge color set
h(E(G))=\{k,k+d,\dots,k+(q-1)d\}
.
\square

Figure 11:
(a) and (b) are a procedure of obtaining a
(k,d)
-harmonious labeling of a caterpillar
T
; (c) adding leaves to the caterpillar
T
.

Figure 12:
A lobster
H
obtained by adding leaves randomly to the tree
T
shown in Fig.
11
admits a
(k,d)
-harmonious labeling, cited from
[
75
]
.

Remark 9
.

Harmonious graphs naturally arose in the study by Graham and Sloane
[
20
]
of modular versions of additive bases problems stemming from error-correcting codes.
\parallel

Definition 56
.

[
8
]
For a
(p,q)
-graph
G
admitting a labeling
f
, we have
f(V(G))=\{f(u):u\in V(G)\}
with
f(x)\neq f(y)
for distinct vertices
x,y\in V(G)
and
f(E(G))=\{f(uv):uv\in E(G)\}
. There are the following constraint conditions:

(C-1)

f(V(G))\subseteq[0,q-1]
;

(C-2)

f(V(G))\subseteq[0,q]
;

(C-3)

f(uv)=f(u)+f(v)~(\bmod~q)
and
f(E(G))=[0,q-1]
;

(C-4)

f(uv)=f(u)+f(v)
and
f(E(G))=[c,c+q-1]
;

(C-5)

f(V(G))\subseteq[k-1,k+2q-1]
with
k\geq 1
;

(C-6)

f(V(G))\subseteq[k-1,k+2q-2]
with
k\geq 1
;

(C-7)

f(uv)=f(u)+f(v)+\epsilon
, where
\epsilon=0
for even
f(u)+f(v)
, and
\epsilon=1
for odd
f(u)+f(v)
;

(C-8)

f(uv)=f(u)+f(v)~(\bmod~2qk)
with
k\geq 1
;

(C-9)

f(E(G))=[2k,2k+2q-2]^{e}
with
k\geq 1
; and

(C-10)

f(E(G))=[2k-1,2k+2q-3]^{o}
with
k\geq 1
.

We call
f
:

Harmo-1.

A
harmonious labeling
if (C-
1
) and (C-
3
) hold true, it allows that there exists at most an edge
uv
satisfies
f(u)=f(v)
if
G
is a tree.

Harmo-2.

A
strongly c-harmonious labeling
if (C-
1
) and (C-
4
) hold true.

Harmo-3.

An
even sequential harmonious labeling
if (C-
5
) with
k=1
, (C-
7
), (C-
8
) with
k=1
and (C-
9
) with
k=1
hold true.

Harmo-4.

A
k
-even sequential harmonious
if (C-
5
), (C-
7
), (C-
8
) with
k\geq 1
and (C-
9
) with
k\geq 1
hold true.

Harmo-5.

An
odd-harmonious labeling
if (C-
6
) with
k=1
and (C-
10
) with
k=1
hold true.

Harmo-6.

A
strongly odd-harmonious labeling
if (C-
2
) and (C-
10
) with
k=1
hold true.

Harmo-7.

An
even-harmonious labeling
if (C-
5
) with
k=1
and (C-
9
) with
k=0
hold true.
\square

Definition 57
.

[
75
]
Suppose that each graph
T_{i}
of
q
edges with
i=1,2
admits an (resp. even)odd-harmonious labeling
f_{i}
defined in Definition
56
. If
f_{1}(V(T_{1}))\cup f_{2}(V(T_{2}))=[0,2q]
, and
|f_{1}(V(T_{1}))\cap f_{2}(V(T_{2}))|=s
, we say
T_{1}
and
T_{2}
to be an
(resp. even)odd-harmonious
s
-matching pair
, the vertex-coinciding graph
\odot_{s}\langle T_{1},T_{2}\rangle
is a
topological authentication
.
\square

Theorem 19
.

[
75
]
A tree
T
has its own vertex set bipartition
V(T)=X\cup Y
with
X\cap Y=\emptyset
, where
X=\{x_{1},x_{2},\dots x_{s}\}
and
Y=\{y_{1},y_{2},\dots y_{t}\}
,
p=|V(T)|=s+t
. Suppose that
T
admits a set-ordered graceful labeling
f
such that
f(x_{i})=i-1
with
i\in[1,s]
, and
f(y_{j})=s-1+j
with
j\in[1,t]
, so
\max f(X)<\min f(Y)
. Hence, this tree
T
admits each of the following harmonious labelings:

(E-1)

A
harmonious labeling

g_{1}
is defined as:
g_{1}(x_{i})=f(x_{i})
with
i\in[1,s]
, and
g_{1}(y_{j})=f(y_{t-j+1})
with
j\in[1,t]
, and
g_{1}(x_{i}y_{j})=g_{1}(x_{i})+g_{1}(y_{j})=f(x_{i})+f(y_{t-j+1})=f(y_{j})-f(x_{i})+2f(x_{i})+f(y_{t-j+1})-f(y_{j})
.

(E-2)

An
even-harmonious labeling

g_{2}
is defined in the way:
g_{2}(x_{i})=2f(x_{i})
with
i\in[1,s]
, and
g_{2}(y_{j})=2f(y_{t-j+1})
with
j\in[1,t]
.

(E-3)

An
odd-harmonious labeling

g_{3}
is defined as:
g_{3}(x_{i})=2f(x_{i})
with
i\in[1,s]
, and
g_{3}(y_{j})=2f(y_{t-j+1})-1
with
j\in[1,t]
.

(E-4)

A
k
-even sequential harmonious labeling

g_{4}
is defined by: For
k\geq 1
,
g_{4}(x_{i})=2k\cdot f(x_{i})
with
i\in[1,s]
, and
g_{4}(y_{j})=2k\cdot f(y_{t-j+1})
with
j\in[1,t]
.

(E-5)

A
strongly c-harmonious labeling

g_{5}
is defined by
g_{5}(x_{i})=f(x_{i})
with
i\in[1,s]
, and
g_{5}(y_{j})=f(y_{t-j+1})
with
j\in[1,t]
.

(E-6)

Under the condition
|s-t|=1
, a
strongly odd-harmonious labeling

g_{6}
is defined as:
g_{6}(x_{i})=2f(x_{i})
with
i\in[1,s]
, and
g_{6}(y_{j})=2f(y_{t-j+1})-1
with
j\in[1,t]
.

(E-7)

A
(k,d)
-harmonious labeling

g_{7}
is defined by
g_{7}(x_{i})=f(x_{i})\cdot d
with
i\in[1,s]
, and
g_{7}(y_{j})=k+f(y_{t-j+1})\cdot d
with
j\in[1,t]
.
\square

Definition 58
.

[
75
]
A
(k,d)
-harmonious labeling

h
of a
(p,q)
-graph
G
is defined by a mapping
h:V(G)\rightarrow[0,k+(q-1)d]
with
k,d\geq 1
, such that
h(x)\neq h(y)
for any pair of vertices
x,y
of
G
,
h(u)+h(v)(\bmod^{*}~qd)
means that
h(uv)-k=[h(u)+h(v)-k](\bmod~qd)
for each edge
uv\in E(G)
, and the edge color set
h(E(G))=\{k,k+d,\dots,k+(q-1)d\}
.
\square

2.5
Topsnut-matchings

Let
G
be a
(p,q)
-graph, and let
f
be a coloring (resp. labeling) defined as
f:X\rightarrow M
, where
X
is a subset of
V(G)\cup E(G)
, and
M\subset Z^{0}
. We call this colored graph
G
a
Topsnut-gpw
. Let
O_{r}
be an operation defined on graphs. If each graph
G_{n}
of a graph sequence
\{G_{n}\}^{m}_{n=0}
can be produced from
G_{n-1}
by the operation
O_{r}
, and
G_{0}
is not generated by doing the operation
O_{r}
to some graph, then we call
G_{n}
a
recursive
O_{r}
-graph
and
G_{0}
a
O_{r}
-root
, as well as
O_{r}
a
recursive operation
. Let
l
stand up a lock (authentication),
k
be a key (password), and
h
be the password rule (a procedure of authentication). The function
l=h(k)
represents the state of “a key
k
opens a lock
l
through the password rule
h
, and call directly
l=h(k)
a
Topsnut-gpw
.

It is not hard to show that each lobster admits a
(k,d)
-harmonious labeling with
k\geq 1
and
d\geq 2
. Thereby, we can use
(k,d)
-harmonious labelings of graphs to design more complicated Topsnut-gpws. Let
\{(k_{i},d_{i})\}_{1}^{n}
be a sequence obtained from two sequences
\{k_{1},k_{2},\dots,k_{n}\}
and
\{d_{1},d_{2},\dots,d_{n}\}
. We have a Topsnut-gpw set

F(k_{i},d_{i})=\{G:~G\textrm{ admits a $(k_{i},d_{i})$-harmonious labeling}\}

for each matching
(k_{i},d_{i})
with
i\in[1,n]
. There are random
Topsnut-chains

G_{i_{1}},G_{i_{2}},\dots,G_{i_{m}}
with
G_{i_{j}}\in\bigcup^{n}_{i=1}F(k_{i},d_{i})
, such that we can apply them to encrypt more objects once time.

One want to look for all odd-graceful labelings of a
(p,q)
-graph
G
admitting an odd-graceful labeling. An odd-graceful labeling of the Topsnut-gpw
G
may determine some matching Topsnut-gpws
H
admitting matching odd-graceful labelings:

Definition 59
.

[
75
]
A
k
-matching odd-graceful labeling

g
of an odd-graceful labeling
f
of a
(p,q)
-graph
G
is a vertex labeling defined on another graph
H
as:
g:V(H)\rightarrow[0,2q-1]
, every edge
uv\in E(H)
has its color
g(uv)=|g(u)-g(v)|
holding
g(E(H))=[1,2q-1]^{o}
true, such that
f(V(G))\cup g(V(H))=[0,2q-1]
with
|f(V(G))\cap g(V(H))|=k\geq 0
. We call
H
admitting a
k
-matching odd-graceful labeling as a
Topsnut-matching
of
G
.
\square

In general, a Topsnut-gpw
G
made by an odd-graceful labeling may have two or more Topsnut-matchings. Let
G
be an (a pan-)odd-graceful graph with vertices
v_{1},v_{2},\dots,v_{p}
. If each vertex
v_{i}
of
G
matches with an (a pan-)odd-graceful graph
H_{i}
with
i\in[1,p]
such that there exists an odd-graceful Topsnut-matching
\odot_{1}\langle G,H_{i}\rangle^{p}_{1}
obtained by vertex-coinciding the vertex
v_{i}
with some vertex of
H_{i}
into one, these two vertices have been labeled with the same labels. We say
\odot_{1}\langle G,H_{i}\rangle^{p}_{1}
a
(pan-)odd-graceful Topsnut-matching team
(see an example shown in Fig.
13
). Moreover,
\odot_{1}\langle G,H_{i}\rangle^{p}_{1}
is called a
uniformly (pan-)odd-gracefully Topsnut-matching team
if
H_{i}\cong H_{j}
for
i\neq j
.

Figure 13:
Two odd-graceful Topsnut-matching teams
\odot_{1}\langle G_{2},H_{2,i}\rangle^{7}_{1}
and
\odot_{1}\langle G_{3},H_{3,i}\rangle^{7}_{1}
, cited from
[
93
]
.

Theorem 20
.

[
93
]
Each caterpillar
G
of
p
vertices has an (a
pan
-)
odd-graceful Topsnut-matching team

\odot_{1}\langle G,H_{i}\rangle^{p}_{1}
.

Definition 60
.

[
75
]
A
k
-sequential odd-graceful labeling

h:V(G)\rightarrow[k,2q-1+k]
such that each induced edge color
h(uv)=|h(u)-h(v)|
for
uv\in E(G)
holds
h(E(G))=[1,2q-1]^{o}
.
\square

Theorem 21
.

[
77
]
Let
G
be a bipartite graph with the bipartition
(X,Y)
, and let
f
be a labeling
V(G)\rightarrow\{0,1,2,\dots\}
such that
f(u)\neq f(v)
for all distinct vertices
u,v\in V(G)
, and
f(xy)=f(y)-f(x)\geq 1
for each edge
xy\in E(G)
with
x\in X
and
y\in Y
. Write
f(V(G))=\{f(w):w\in V(G)\}
. Then we have

(i)
The bipartite graph
G
admits a labeling
g_{1}
induced by
f
such that
g_{1}(u)\neq g_{1}(v)
for distinct vertices
u,v\in V(G)
, and
g_{1}(x)+g_{1}(y)=\max f(V(G))+\min f(V(G))-f(xy)
for each edge
xy\in E(G)
with
x\in X
and
y\in Y
.

(ii)
For all values of positive integers
d
and
k
, the bipartite graph
G
admits a labeling
g_{2}
such that
g_{2}(u)\neq g_{2}(v)
for all distinct vertices
u,v\in V(G)
, and
g_{2}(y)-g_{2}(x)=k+d\cdot f(xy)
for each edge
xy\in E(G)
with
x\in X
and
y\in Y
.

(iii)
There are a labeling
g_{3}
and a constant
\lambda>0
such that
g_{3}(u)\neq g_{3}(v)
for all distinct vertices
u,v\in V(G)
, and
g_{3}(x)+g_{3}(xy)+g_{3}(y)=\lambda
for each edge
xy\in E(G)
.

(iv)
The bipartite graph
G
admits a labeling
g_{4}
such that
g_{4}(u)\neq g_{4}(v)
for all distinct vertices
u,v\in V(G)
, and
g_{4}(x)+g_{4}(y)=k+d\cdot\big[\max f(V(G))+\min f(V(G))-f(xy)\big]
for each edge
xy\in E(G)
with
x\in X
and
y\in Y
.

Remark 10
.

Suppose that a
(p,q)
-graph
G
contains two subgraphs
G_{1}
and
G_{2}
such that
V(G_{1})\cap V(G_{2})=\{w_{1},w_{2},\dots,w_{n}\}
and
E(G)=E(G_{1})\cup E(G_{2})
with
E(G_{1})\cap E(G_{2})=\emptyset
, we by
G=\odot_{n}\langle G_{1},G_{2}\rangle
denote
G
, and call
G
a
n
-vertex-coincided graph
(
n
-vi-
(p,q)
-graph). Moreover, we call
G
a
uniformly
n
-vertex-coincided
(p,q)
-graph
(uniformly
n
-vi-
(p,q)
-graph) if
|E(G_{1})|=|E(G_{2})|
in
[
103
]
. Obviously, every tree is a
1
-vi-tree.
\parallel

Definition 61
.

[
103
]
Suppose that a
1
-vi-
(p,q)
-graph
G=\odot_{1}\langle G_{1},G_{2}\rangle
admits a mapping
f
:
V(G)\rightarrow[0,q]
such that

(i)
f(x)\neq f(y)
for any pair of vertices
x,y\in V(G)
;

(ii)
f
is an odd-graceful labeling of
G_{1}
;

(iii)
f:V(G_{2})\rightarrow[0,2|E(G_{2})|]
such that the edge color set
f(E(G_{2}))=\{f(uv)=f(u)+f(v)~(\bmod~2|E(G_{2})|):~uv\in E(G_{2})\}=[1,2|E(G_{2})|-1]^{o}
.

Then
G
is called a
complementary edge-odd-graceful
1
-vi-
(p,q)
-graph
(a CEOG
1
-vi-graph for short),
f
a
complementary edge-odd-graceful labeling
(a CEOG-labeling) of
G
.
\square

Remark 11
.

We have the following particular graphs:

CEOG-1.

[
103
]
If a uniformly
1
-vi-
(p,q)
-graph
G=\odot_{1}\langle G_{1},G_{2}\rangle
admits a CEOG-labeling
f
, where
G_{1}
is an odd-graceful graph. Then
G_{1}
is called an
E-lock-graph
, and
G_{2}
is called an
E-key-graph
of
G_{1}
.

CEOG-2.

[
103
]
Let
G_{j}
be a
(p_{j},q_{j})
-graph with
j=1,2
. A graph
G
is obtained by identifying a vertex
x_{i,1}
of
G_{1}
with another vertex
x_{i,2}
of
G_{2}
into one vertex
x_{i}
(denoted as
x_{i}=x_{i,1}\odot x_{i,2}
hereafter) for
i\in[1,m]
, and call these vertices
x_{1}
,
x_{2}
,
\dots
,
x_{m}
the
identification-vertices
. Write
G=\odot_{m}\langle G_{1},G_{2}\rangle
, and called it an
m
-identification graph
. So,
G=\odot_{m}\langle G_{1},G_{2}\rangle
has
p_{1}+p_{2}-m
vertices and
q_{1}+q_{2}
edges. Conversely, we can vertex-split the identification-vertex
x_{i}
into two vertices
x_{i,1}
and
x_{i,2}
(called
splitting-vertices
), so
G
can be split into two vertex-disjoint subgraphs
G_{1}
and
G_{2}
.

CEOG-3.

[
103
]
For two connected
(p_{i},q)
-graphs
G_{i}
with
i=1,2
, and
p=p_{1}+p_{2}-2
, if the
2
-identification
(p,q)
-graph
G=\odot_{2}\langle G_{1},G_{2}\rangle
admits a mapping
f
:
V(G)\rightarrow[0,q-1]
such that

(i)
f(x)\neq f(y)
for any pair of vertices
x,y\in V(G)
;

(ii)
f
is just an odd-elegant labeling of
G_{i}
with
i=1,2
.

Then we say
G
a
twin odd-elegant graph
(a TOE-graph),
f
a
TOE-labeling
,
G_{1}
a
TOE-source graph
,
G_{2}
a
TOE-associated graph
, and
(G_{1},G_{2})
a
TOE-matching pair
.

CEOG-4.

[
103
]
Let
T_{i}
be a tree of order
n
with
i=1,2
. If the 2-identification tree
G_{T}=\odot_{2}\langle T_{1},T_{2}\rangle
admits a mapping
f:

V(G_{T})\rightarrow[0,2n-3]
such that

(i)
f(x)\neq f(y)
for any pair of vertices
x,y\in V(G_{T})
;

(ii)
f
is an odd-elegant labeling of
T_{i}
with
i=1,2
.

Then
G_{T}
is called a
twin odd-elegant tree
(a TOE-tree),
f
a
TOE-labeling
, and
T_{1}
a
TOE-source tree
, and
T_{2}
a
TOE-associated tree
of
T_{1}
. Furthermore, we say
T_{1}
a
TOE-source self-associated tree
if
T_{1}
is isomorphic to
T_{2}
.
\parallel

Definition 62
.

[
120
]
Suppose that a
(p,q)
-graph
G
admits a mapping
f:V(G)\cup E(G)\rightarrow[1,p+q]
such that
f(u)\neq f(v)
for distinct vertices
u,v\in V(G)
, and each edge color
f(uv)
for
uv\in E(G)
is defined as
f(uv)=|f(u)-f(v)|+k~(\bmod~p+q)
for some integer
k\in Z^{0}\setminus\{0\}
, and the set of all edge colors is equal to
[1,p+q]
, we call
f
a
Module-
k
super graceful labeling
.
\square

Figure 14:
(a) A module-
0
super-graceful labeling; (b) a module-
2
odd-elegant labeling.

Definition 63
.

[
120
]
Suppose that a
(p,q)
-graph
G
admits a mapping
f:V(G)\rightarrow[0,2q-1]
such that
f(u)\neq f(v)
for distinct vertices
u,v\in V(G)
, and each edge color
f(uv)
for
uv\in E(G)
is defined as
f(uv)=f(u)+f(v)+k~(\bmod~2q)
for some integer
k\in Z^{0}\setminus\{0\}
, and the set of all edge colors is equal to
[1,2q-1]^{o}
or
[2,2q]^{e}
, we call
f
a
Module-
k
odd-elegant labeling
and
G
an
Module-
k
odd-elegant graph
. Moreover, if
(V_{1},V_{2})
is the bipartition of
G
, and the Module-
k
odd-elegant labeling
f
obeys
\max\{f(u):~u\in V_{1}\}<\min\{f(v):~v\in V_{2}\}
, then we call
f
a
set-ordered Module-
k
odd-elegant labeling
, and write this case as
\max f(V_{1})<\min f(V_{2})
.
\square

Definition 64
.

[
120
]
Let
G
be a
(p,q)
-graph and admit a mapping
g:V(G)\rightarrow[0,2q-1]
such that
g(u)\neq g(v)
for distinct vertices
u,v\in V(G)
, and each edge color
g(uv)
for
uv\in E(G)
is defined as
g(uv)=|g(u)-g(v)|+k~(\bmod~2q)
for even
k
.

(1) If the edge color set
g(E(G))=\{g(uv):~uv\in E(G)\}=[1,2q-1]^{o}
, we call
g
an
edge module-k odd-graceful labeling
(Em(k)og-labeling) and
G
an
edge module-k odd-graceful graph
(Em(k)og-graph). Especially,
G
is
odd-graceful
when
k=0
.

(2) If
G
is a bipartite graph with its bipartition
(V_{1},V_{2})
, such that an
Em(k)og-labeling

g
holding
\max\{g(u):~u\in V_{1}\}<\min\{g(v):~v\in V_{2}\}
, then we call
g
a
set-ordered Em(k)og-labeling
, and write this case as
\max g(V_{1})<\min g(V_{2})
.
\square

Definition 65
.

[
120
]
Let
G_{j}
be a
(p_{j},q_{j})
-graph with
j=1,2
, and let
p=p_{1}+p_{2}-1
and
q=q_{1}+q_{2}
. If the
(p,q)
-graph
G=\odot\langle G_{1},G_{2}\rangle
admits a mapping
f
:
V(G)\rightarrow[0,q]
such that

(i)
f(x)\neq f(y)
for any pair of vertices
x,y\in V(G)
;

(ii)
f
is an Em(k)og-labeling of
G_{1}
;

(iii) the edge color set

f(E(G_{i}))=\{f(uv)=|f(u)-f(v)|+k~(\bmod~2q):~uv\in E(G_{i})\}=[1,2q-1]^{o}

for some even integer
k
and
i=1,2
.

Then we call
G
a
twin edge module-k odd-graceful graph
(twin Em(k)og-graph),
f
an
Em(k)og-labeling
,
G_{1}
a
source graph
, and
G_{2}
an
associated graph
.
\square

Definition 66
.

[
111
]
Suppose that a connected
(p,q)
-graph
G
admits a set-ordered graceful labeling
f
defined in Definition
5
and
V(G)=X\cup Y
such that there exist an integer
M>0
and another labeling
g
defined by
g(x)=f(x)
for
x\in X
and
g(y)=f(y)+M
with
y\in Y
. If
g(E(G))=[1,s]\cup[s+1+M,q]
, then
f
is called an
edge-ordered graceful labeling
of
G
, and
G
an
edge-ordered graceful graph
.
\square

Definition 67
.

[
111
]
Suppose that a connected
(p,q)
-graph
G
admits a set-ordered graceful labeling
f
defined in Definition
5
and
V(G)=X\cup Y
such that there exist an integer
M>0
and another labeling
g
defined by
g(x)=f(x)
for
x\in X
and
g(y)=f(y)+M
with
y\in Y
. If
g(E(G))=[1,2s-1]\cup[2(s+M)-1,2q-1]
, then we call
f
an
edge-ordered odd-graceful labeling
of
G
, and
G
an
edge-ordered odd-graceful graph
.
\square

Definition 68
.

[
38
,
33
]
Let
G
be a
(p,q)
-graph.

(1)

If there is an injection of
f:V(G)\rightarrow[0,2q+1]^{o}
such that the edge color set
\{f(uv)=|f(u)-f(v)|-1:~uv\in E(G)\}=[1,2q-1]^{o}
,
f
is called an
all odd-graceful labeling
of
G
.

(2)

G
is called a
super odd-graceful graph
if there is a bijective function
f
with
f(V(G)\cup E(G))=[1,2(p+q)-1]^{o}
, such that edge color
f(uv)=|f(u)-f(v)|-1
for each edge
uv\in E(G)
.

(3)

G
is called a
super odd-elegant graph
if there is a bijective function
f
with
f(V(G)\cup E(G))=[1,2(p+q)-1]^{o}
, such that each edge
uv\in E(G)
holds
f(uv)=f(u)+f(v)~(\bmod~M
) with
M=2p+3q-1
.
\square

Definition 69
.

[
102
]
Let
G
be a
(p,q)
-graph.

New-1.

Suppose that
G
admits a mapping
f:V(G)\rightarrow[0,2q]
such that
f(u)\neq f(v)
for distinct vertices
u,v\in V(G)
, and the edge color set
\{f(uv)=|f(u)-f(v)|:uv\in E(G)\}=[1,2q-1]^{o}
, then we call
f
an
edge-odd-graceful labeling
, and
G
an
edge-odd-graceful graph
.

New-2.

Suppose that the graph
G=H\odot\langle G_{k}\rangle^{n}_{k=1}
obtained by coinciding a vertex
u_{k}
of each graph
G_{k}
with a vertex
x_{k}\in V(H)=\{x_{1},x_{2},\dots,x_{n}\}
is a multiple-vertices graph and admits a mapping
f:V(G)\rightarrow[0,q]
, such that

(i)
f(x)\neq f(y)
for any pair of vertices
x,y\in V(G)
;

(ii)
f
is an edge-odd-graceful labeling of
H
;

(iii)
f
is an edge-odd-graceful labeling of
G_{k}
for
k\in[1,n]
.

Then we call
G
an
edge-odd-multiple graph
,
f
an
edge-odd multiple labeling
, and
H
a
base
, and each
G_{k}
with
k\in[1,n]
a
seed
.
\square

Definition 70
.

[
53
]
A
(p,q)
-graph
G
admits an
edge-odd-graceful total labeling

h
when
h:V(G)\rightarrow[0,q-1]
and
h:E(G)\rightarrow[1,2q-1]^{o}
such that
\{h(u)+h(uv)+h(v):uv\in E(G)\}=[a,b]
with
b-a+1=q
.
\square

Definition 71
.

(i)
[
102
]
If
f
holds the edge color set
\{f(uv)=|f(u)-f(v)|:uv\in E(G)\}\neq[1,2q-1]^{o}
in Definition
70
, then we call
f
a
blemished (edge-)odd-graceful labeling
, and
G
a
blemished (edge-)odd-graceful graph
.

(ii)
[

]
In Definition
69
, if each graph
G_{k}
with
k\in[1,|V(H)|]
admits a set-ordered edge-odd-graceful labeling, then
G=H\odot\langle G_{k}\rangle^{n}_{k=1}
with
n=|V(H)|
is called an
set-ordered multiple-vertices
(p,q)
-graph
, so we call

a
set-ordered-uniformly mv-
(p,q)

,
f
a
set-ordered edge-odd multiple labeling
of

.

2.6
Labelings and perfect matchings

2.6.1
Labelings on graphs having perfect matchings

Definition 72
.

[
33
]
Let
G
ba a
(p,q)
-graph with a perfect matching
M
, and “AM”=arithmetic matching.

M-1.

If
G
admits a super graceful labeling
h
holding
h(u)+h(v)=3q+2
for each matching edge
uv\in M
, then
h
is called an
AM-super graceful labeling
.

M-2.

If there is an injection
h:V(G)\rightarrow[0,2q+1]^{o}
such that the edge color set
\{h(uv)=|h(u)-h(v)|-1:uv\in E(G)\}=[1,2q-1]^{o}
, then
h
is called an
all-odd graceful labeling
of
G
. Moreover, if
h
holds
h(u)+h(v)=2(q+1)
for each matching edge
uv\in M
, we call
h
an
AM-all-odd graceful labeling
.

M-3.

G
is called a
super odd-graceful labeling
if
h(V(G)\cup E(G))=[1,2(4q+1)]^{o}
with
h(uv)=|h(u)-h(v)|-1
for each edge
uv\in E(G)
. Moreover, if
h
holds
h(u)+h(v)=2(3q+1)
for each matching edge
uv\in M
, we call
h
an
AM-super odd-graceful labeling
.

M-4.

If
G
is a bipartite graph with the bipartition
(X,Y)
, and
G
is said to be a
super odd-elegant graph
if there is a mapping
h
holding
h(V(T)\cup E(T))=[1,2(4q+1)]^{o}
with
h(uv)=h(u)+h(v)
(
\bmod~M^{*}
) for
uv\in E(T)
, where the constant
M^{*}=2p+3q+(|X|-|Y|)
. Moreover,
h
is called an
AM-super odd-elegant labeling
if
h
holds
|h(u)-h(v)|=q+1
for each matching edge
uv\in M
.

M-5.

If
G
admits one modulo three graceful labeling
h
holding
h(u)+h(v)=3q-2
for each matching edge
uv\in M
, then we call
h
an
AM-one modulo three graceful labeling
.

M-6.

If
G
admits a Skolem-graceful labeling
h
holding
h(u)+h(v)=q+2
for each matching edge
uv\in M
, then
h
is called an
AM-Skolem-graceful labeling
.

M-7.

If
G
admits an odd-even graceful labeling
h
holding
h(u)+h(v)=2(q+1)
for each matching edge
uv\in M
, then
h
is called an
AM-odd-even graceful labeling
.

M-8.

If
G
admits a
k
-graceful labeling
h
holding
h(u)+h(v)=k+q-1
for each edge
uv\in M
, then
h
is called an
AM-
k
-graceful labeling
.
\square

Theorem 22
.

[
33
]
A
leaf-matching

(p,q)
-
tree

T
contains a perfect matching
M
such that each matching edge has an end of degree one. Then we have the following pairwise equivalent assertions:

(i)

T
admits a strongly set-ordered graceful labeling;

(ii)

T
admits an AM-set-ordered super graceful labeling;

(iii)

T
admits an AM-set-ordered all-odd graceful labeling.

(iv)

T
admits an AM-set-ordered super odd-graceful labeling;

(v)

T
admits an AM-set-ordered super odd-elegant labeling;

(vi)

T
admits an AM-set-ordered one modulo three graceful labeling;

(vii)

T
admits an AM-set-ordered Skolem-graceful labeling;

(viii)

T
admits an AM-set-ordered odd-even graceful labeling; and

(ix)

T
admits an AM-
k
-graceful labeling.

2.6.2
Parameterized labelings on trees having perfect matchings

Definition 73
.

[
36
,
37
]
Let
T
be a
(p,q)
-tree having a perfect matching
M
, and let
S_{q-1,k,d}=\{k,k+d,\dots,k+(q-1)d\}
for integers
k\geq 1
and
d\geq 1
. Suppose that each leaf of
T
is an end of some matching edge of
M
, and
M(L)
is the set of all leaves of
T
, and furthermore the graph
T-M(L)
obtained by deleting all leaves of
T
is just a tree having
k=|V(T-M(L))|
vertices.

(1)
[
36
]
Suppose that
T
admits an odd-elegant labeling
f_{1}
. If
|f_{1}(u)-f_{1}(v)|=1
for each matching edge
uv\in M
holds true, and the elements of
f_{1}(E(M))
form an arithmetic progression having
|M|
terms with the first term
1
and the common difference 4. Then we say
f_{1}
an
arithmetic matching odd-elegant labeling
(
AM-odd-elegant labeling
) of
T
, and
T
is an
AM-odd-elegant graph
.

(2)
[
36
]
Suppose that
T
admits a super felicitous labeling
f_{2}
. If
|f_{2}(u)-f_{2}(v)|=k
for every matching edge
uv\in M
, we call
f_{2}
an
arithmetic matching super felicitous labeling
(
AM-super felicitous labeling
) of
T
, and
T
an
AM-super felicitous graph
.

(3)
[
36
]
Suppose that
T
admits a super edge-magic graceful labeling
f_{3}
. If
|f_{3}(u)-f_{3}(v)|=k
for each matching edge
uv\in M
, and the elements of
f_{3}(E(T))
produce an arithmetic progression having
|M|
terms with the first term
p+1
and the common difference
2
. Then we call
f_{3}
an
arithmetic matching super edge-magic graceful labeling
(
AM-super edge-magic graceful labeling
) of
T
, and
T
an
AM-super edge-magic graceful graph
.

(4)
[
37
]
Suppose that
T
admits a super edge-magic total labeling
f_{4}
. If
|f_{4}(u)-f_{4}(v)|=k
for each matching edge
uv\in M
, and the elements of
f_{4}(E(T))
yield an arithmetic progression having
|M|
terms with the first term
2p-1
and the common difference
-2
. Then we call
f_{4}
an
arithmetic matching super edge-magic total labeling
(
AM-super edge-magic total labeling
) of
T
, and
T
an
AM-super edge-magic total graph
.
\square

Definition 74
.

[
37
]
Let integers
k,d\geq 0
.

(1) Suppose that
T
admits a super
(k,d)
-edge antimagic labeling
g
defined in Definition
7
. If
|g(u)-g(v)|=k
for each matching edge
uv\in M
, and

(i) the elements of
g(E(T))
produce an arithmetic progression having
|M|
terms with the first term
p+1
and the common difference
2
;

(ii)
g(u)+g(v)=b_{i}
forms a sequence
\{b_{i}\}
being an arithmetic progression having
|M|
terms with the first term
k+2
and the common difference
2
, where
n=|M|
.

Then we call
g
an
arithmetic matching super
(k,d)
-edge antimagic total labeling
(
AM-super
(k,d)
-edge antimagic total labeling
) of
T
, and
T
an
AM-super
(k,d)
-edge antimagic total labeling graph
.

(2) Suppose that
T
admits a
(k,d)
-arithmetic labeling
h
defined in Definition
7
. If
|h(u)-h(v)|=k
for each matching edge
uv\in M
, and the elements of
h(E(T))
produce an arithmetic progression having
|M|
terms with the first term
k
and the common difference
2d
. Then we call
h
an
arithmetic matching
(k,d)
-arithmetic labeling
(
AM-
(k,d)
-arithmetic labeling
) of

, and

-arithmetic graph
.
\square

For illustrating Definition
73
and Definition

Definition 75
.

[
39
]
Let
T
be a
(p,q)
-tree having a perfect matching
M
, and let
S_{q-1,k,d}=\{k,k+d,\dots,k+(q-1)d\}
,
S\,^{\prime}_{k,d}=\{k,k+2d,\dots,k+2(q-1)d\}
,
S\,^{\prime\prime}_{k,d}=\{k,k+d,\dots,k+(p+q-1)d\}
for integers
k\geq 1
and
d\geq 1
. Suppose that each leaf of
T
is an end of some matching edge of
M
, and
M(L)
is the set of all leaves of
T
, and furthermore the graph
T-M(L)
obtained by deleting all leaves of
T
is a tree having
c=|V(T-M(L))|
vertices. AM is the abbreviation of “arithmetic matching”.

S-1.

An
AM-
(k,d)
-totally odd (resp. even) graceful labeling

f
of
G
holds
f(V(G))\subseteq[0,k+2(q-1)d]
,
f(x)\neq f(y)
for distinct vertices
x,y\in V(G)
and
f(E(G))=\{|f(u)-f(v)|:\ uv\in E(G)\}=S\,^{\prime}_{k,d}
. Meanwhile,
f(u)+f(v)=k+2(q-1)d
for each matching edge
uv\in M
.

S-2.

An
AM-
(k,d)
-felicitous labeling

f
of
G
holds
f(V(G))\subseteq[0,k+(q-1)d]
,
f(x)\neq f(y)
for distinct vertices
x,y\in V(G)
and
f(E(G))=\{f(uv)=f(u)+f(v)~(\bmod~qd):uv\in E(G)\}=S_{q-1,k,d}
; and furthermore,
f
is
super
if
f(V(G))=[0,(\frac{p}{2}-1)d]\cup[k+pd/2,k+(p-1)d]
. Meanwhile,
|f(u)-f(v)|=k+cd
for each matching edge
uv\in M
.

S-3.

An
AM-
(k,d)
-edge-magic total labeling

f
of
G
holds
f(V(G)\cup E(G))\subseteq[0,k+(p+q-1)d]
such that
f(u)+f(v)+f(uv)=a
for any edge
uv\in E(G)
, where the magic constant
a
is a fixed integer; and furthermore,
f
is
super
if
f(V(G))=[1,pd/2]\cup[k+pd/2,k+qd]
. Meanwhile,
|f(u)-f(v)|=k+(c-1)d
for each matching edge
uv\in M
and the elements of
f(E(T))
yield an arithmetic progression having
|M|
terms with the first term
k+2(p-1)d
and the common difference
-2d
.

S-4.

An
AM-
(k,d)
-edge-magic graceful labeling

f
of
G
holds
f(V(G)\cup E(G))\subseteq[0,k+(p+q-1)d]
such that
\mid f(u)+f(v)-f(uv)\mid=a
for any edge
uv\in E(G)
, where the magic constant
a
is a fixed integer; and furthermore,
f
is
super
if
f(V(G))=[1,pd/2]\cup[k+pd/2,k+qd]
. Meanwhile,
|f(u)-f(v)|=k+(c-1)d
for each matching edge
uv\in M
and the elements of
f(E(T))
yield an arithmetic progression having
|M|
terms with the first term
k+pd
and the common difference
2d
.

S-5.

Suppose that
T
admits a
(k,d)
-arithmetic
f
. If
|f(u)-f(v)|=k
for each matching edge
uv\in M
, and the elements of
f(E(T))
produce an arithmetic progression having
|M|
terms with the first term
k
and the common difference
2d
. Then we call
f
an
AM-
(k,d)
-arithmetic labeling
of
T
.

S-6.

An
AM-
(k,d)
-totally odd (resp. even)-elegant labeling

f
of
G
holds
f(V(G))\subset[0,k+2(q-1)d]
,
f(u)\neq f(v)
for distinct vertices
u,v\in V(G)
, and
f(E(G))=\{f(uv)=f(u)+f(v)~(\bmod~k+2qd):uv\in E(G)\}=S\,^{\prime}_{k,d}
. Meanwhile,
|f(u)-f(v)|=k
for each matching edge
uv\in M
and the elements of
f(E(T))
yield an arithmetic progression having
|M|
terms with the first term
k
and the common difference
4d
.
\square

For illustrating Definition

Definition 76
.

[
32
]
Suppose that
T
is a tree having
p
vertices,
q
edges and a perfect matching
M
, and its vertex bipartition
V(T)=X\cup Y
with
X\cap Y=\emptyset
such that each edge
xy\in E(T)
holds
x\in X
and
y\in Y
. Let
S_{m,\alpha,k\beta}=\{\alpha,\alpha+k\beta,\dots,\alpha+k(m-1)\beta\}
with integers
k\geq 1
,
m\geq 2
,
\alpha\geq 0
and
\beta\geq 1
, and let
c
be a constant. There are the following constraint conditions:

C-1.

\sigma:V(T)\rightarrow S_{p-1,0,\beta}\cup S_{2p-3,\alpha,\beta}
, and
\sigma(x)\neq\sigma(w)
for any pair of vertices
x,w\in V(T)
;

C-2.

\sigma:V(T)\rightarrow S_{\frac{p}{2},0,\beta}\cup S_{p,\alpha,\beta}
, and
\sigma(x)\neq\sigma(w)
for any pair of vertices
x,w\in V(T)
;

C-3.

\sigma(E(T))=\{\sigma(xy)=\sigma(x)+\sigma(y):xy\in E(T)\}=S_{q,\alpha,2\beta}
;

C-4.

\sigma(E(T))=\{\sigma(xy)=\sigma(x)+\sigma(y):xy\in E(T)\}=S_{q,\alpha,\beta}
;

C-5.

induced edge color
\sigma(xy)=|\sigma(x)-\sigma(y)|
for each edge
xy\in E(T)
, and
\sigma(E(T))=\{\sigma(xy):xy\in E(T)\}=S_{q,\alpha,\beta}
;

C-6.

induced edge color
\sigma(xy)=|\sigma(x)-\sigma(y)|
for each edge
xy\in E(T)
, and
\sigma(E(T))=\{\sigma(xy):xy\in E(T)\}=S_{q,\alpha,2\beta}
;

C-7.

induced edge color
\sigma(xy)-\alpha=\sigma(x)+\sigma(y)-\alpha~(\bmod~q\beta)
for each edge
xy\in E(T)
, and
\sigma(E(T))=\{\sigma(xy):xy\in E(T)\}=S_{q,\alpha,\beta}
;

C-8.

\sigma:V(T)\cup E(T)\rightarrow S_{\frac{p}{2}+1,0,\beta}\cup S_{p+q,\alpha,\beta}
, and
\sigma(x)\neq\sigma(w)
for any pair of vertices
x,w\in V(T)
;

C-9.

\sigma(X)=S_{\frac{p}{2},0,\beta}
and
\sigma(Y)=S_{p-1,\alpha,\beta}\setminus S_{\frac{p}{2}-1,\alpha,\beta}
;

C-10.

\sigma(X)=S_{\frac{p}{2},0,\beta}
and
\sigma(Y)=S_{p,\alpha,\beta}\setminus S_{\frac{p}{2},\alpha,\beta}
;

C-11.

\sigma(X)=S_{\frac{p}{2},0,\beta}\setminus\{0\}
and
\sigma(Y)=S_{p,\alpha,\beta}\setminus S_{\frac{p}{2},\alpha,\beta}
;

————
matching-set

C-12.

|\sigma(x)-\sigma(y)|=\alpha+p\beta/2
for each matching edge
xy\in M
;

C-13.

|\sigma(x)-\sigma(y)|=\alpha+(p-2)\beta/2
for each matching edge
xy\in M
;

C-14.

\sigma(x)+\sigma(y)=\alpha+2(q-1)\beta
for each matching edge
xy\in M
;

C-15.

|\sigma(x)-\sigma(y)|=\alpha
for each matching edge
xy\in M
;

C-16.

|\sigma(x)-\sigma(y)|=\alpha+(q-1)\beta
for each matching edge
xy\in M
;

C-17.

\sigma(M)=S_{|M|,\alpha,2\beta}
;

C-18.

\sigma(M)=S_{|M|,\alpha,4\beta}
;

C-19.

\sigma(M)=\{\alpha+2(p-1)\beta,\alpha+2(p-2)\beta,\dots,\alpha+2(p-|M|)\beta\}
;

C-20.

\sigma(M)=\{\alpha+p\beta,\alpha+(2+p)\beta,\dots,\alpha+[2(|M|-1)+p]\beta\}
;

————
edge-magic

C-21.

\sigma(x)+\sigma(y)+\sigma(xy)=c
for each edge
xy\in E(T)
;

C-22.

\sigma(xy)+|\sigma(x)-\sigma(y)|=c
for each edge
xy\in E(T)
;

C-23.

|\sigma(x)+\sigma(y)-\sigma(xy)|=c
for each edge
xy\in E(T)
; and

C-24.

\big|\sigma(xy)-|\sigma(x)-\sigma(y)|\big|=c
for each edge
xy\in E(T)
.

We call
\sigma
:

Label-1.

A

(\alpha,\beta)
-graceful labeling

, C-

Remark 12
.

Let
f
be a strongly set-ordered
(\alpha,\beta)
-graceful labeling of a tree
T
, we have the following equivalent transformations:

(1) An AM-
(\alpha,\beta)
graceful-difference labeling
\sigma
is defined by
\sigma(w)=f(w)
for
w\in V(T)
,
\sigma(xy)=2\alpha+(q-1)\beta-f(xy)
for
xy\in E(T)
.

(2) An AM-
(\alpha,\beta)
edge-difference labeling
\sigma
is defined by
\sigma(x)=\max f(X)+\min f(X)-f(x)
for
x\in X
,
\sigma(y)=\max f(Y)+\min f(Y)-f(y)
for
y\in Y
, and
\sigma(xy)=\max f(E(T))+\min f(E(T))-f(xy)
for
xy\in E(T)
.
\parallel

Before to define six new labelings on trees, we introduce the concept of a
leaf-matching tree
. Let
M(L)
be the set of all leaves of
(p,q)
-tree
T
, if a tree
T
with a perfect matching
M
satisfies that one end of each matching edge of
M
is just an leaf of
T
, and the remainder
T-M(L)
is a tree of
p=|V(T-M(L))|
vertices after removing all leaves of
T
, so call
T
a
leaf-matching tree
. Furthermore, ”arithmetic matching” and ”arithmetic progression” are abbreviated as “AM” and “arp”, respectively.

Definition 78
.

[
32
]
Suppose that
T
is a leaf-matching tree on
p
vertices and
q
edges, and
M
is a perfect matching set of
T
.

(1)

A labeling
\sigma:V(T)\rightarrow[0,\alpha+2(q-1)\beta]
is called an
AM-2-parameter totally odd/even-graceful labeling
if
\sigma(V(T))\subseteq[0,\alpha+2(q-1)\beta]
,
\sigma(E(T))=S\,^{\prime}_{\alpha,\beta}
and
|\sigma(x)-\sigma(y)|
with each edge
xy\in E(T)
. Meanwhile, each matching edge
xy\in M
holds
\sigma(x)+\sigma(y)=\alpha+2(q-1)\beta
true.

(2)

A labeling
\sigma:V(T)\rightarrow[0,\alpha+(q-1)\beta]
is called an
AM-2-parameter felicitous labeling
if the vertex color set
\sigma(V(T))\subseteq[0,\alpha+(q-1)\beta]
,
\sigma(E(T))=S_{\alpha,\beta}
and
\sigma(xy)=\sigma(x)+\sigma(y)~(\bmod~q\beta)
with edge
xy\in E(T)
, and furthermore,
\sigma
is
super
if
\sigma(V(T))=[0,(\frac{p}{2}-1)\beta]\cup[\alpha+\frac{p}{2}\beta,\alpha+(p-1)\beta]
. Moreover, each matching edge
xy\in M
holds
|\sigma(x)-\sigma(y)|=\alpha+\rho\beta
true.

(3)

A labeling
\sigma:V(T)\rightarrow[0,\alpha+(p+q-1)\beta]
is called an
AM-2-parameter edge-magic total labeling
if the total color set
\sigma(V(T)\cup E(T))\subseteq[0,\alpha+(p+q-1)\beta]
such that
\sigma(x)+\sigma(y)+\sigma(xy)=a
with
xy\in E(T)
, where the
a
is a magic constant. Then we say
\sigma
is
super
if
\sigma(V(T))=[1,\frac{p}{2}\beta]\cup[\alpha+\frac{p}{2}\beta,\alpha+q\beta]
. Moreover, each matching edge
xy\in M
holds
|\sigma(x)-\sigma(y)|=\alpha+(\rho-1)\beta
; and the set
\sigma(M)
has
|M|
elements in total and is obtained by an arp with the first term
\alpha+2(p-1)\beta
and the common difference
-2\beta
.

(4)

A labeling
\sigma:V(T)\rightarrow[0,\alpha+(p+q-1)\beta]
is called an
AM-2-parameter edge-magic graceful labeling
if
\sigma(V(T)\cup E(T))\subseteq[0,\alpha+(p+q-1)\beta]
such that
|\sigma(x)+\sigma(y)-\sigma(xy)|=a
when
xy\in E(T)
, where the
a
is a magic constant and, we say
\sigma
to be
super
if
\sigma(V(T))=[1,p\beta/2]\cup[\alpha+p\beta/2,\alpha+q\beta]
. Furthermore, each matching edge
xy\in M
holds
|\sigma(x)-\sigma(y)|=\alpha+(\rho-1)\beta
true; and the set
\sigma(M)
has
|M|
elements in total and its elements are obtained by an arp having the first term
\alpha+p\beta
and the common difference
2\beta
.

(5)

A labeling
\sigma:V(T)\rightarrow[0,\alpha+(p+q-1)\beta]
is called an
AM-2-parameter arithmetic labeling
if each matching edge
xy\in M
holds
|\sigma(x)-\sigma(y)|=\alpha
true, and the set
\sigma(M)
has
|M|
elements in total and its elements yield an arp with the first term
\alpha
and the common difference
2\beta
.

(6)

A labeling
\sigma:V(T)\rightarrow[0,\alpha+2(q-1)\beta]
is called an
AM-2-parameter totally odd/even-elegant labeling
if
\sigma(V(T))\subset[0,\alpha+2(q-1)\beta]
and
\sigma(E(T))=\{\sigma(xy)=\sigma(x)+\sigma(y)(\bmod~\alpha+2q\beta):xy\in E(T)\}=S\,^{\prime}_{\alpha,\beta}
. Furthermore, each matching edge
xy\in M
holds
|\sigma(x)-\sigma(y)|=\alpha
true, and the elements of
\sigma(M)
form an arp having
|M|
terms, the first term
\alpha
and the common difference
4\beta
.
\square

Remark 13
.

Notice that the parity property of value of
\alpha
will determine an AM-2-parameter totally odd-graceful (odd-elegant) labeling if
\alpha=
odd, and an AM-2-parameter totally even-graceful (even-elegant) labeling when
\alpha=
even. Some examples for illustrating Definition
78
can be found in Fig.
21
, Fig.
22
and Fig.
23
.
\parallel

Lemma 23
.

[
32
]
For the following eight labelings:

(1) strongly set-ordered graceful labeling;

(2) strongly
(\alpha,\beta)
-graceful labeling;

(3) AM-2-parameter totally odd/even-graceful labeling;

(4) AM-2-parameter totally odd/even-elegant labeling;

(5) AM-2-parameter arithmetic labeling;

(6) AM-2-parameter super felicitous labeling;

(7) AM-2-parameter super edge-magic total labeling; and

(8) AM-2-parameter super edge-magic graceful labeling.

Then a
leaf-matching tree

T
(see Fig.
24
) admits an
(i)
-labeling if and only if
T
admits a
(j)
-labeling too for
i,j\in[1,8]
and
i\neq j
.

Figure 24:
A process of obtaining a
leaf-matching tree
shown in (b), cited from
[
32
]
.

Lemma 24
.

[
32
]
Suppose that a tree
T
has a perfect matching
M(T)
and admits
a strongly graceful labeling
\phi
. There are three operations:

(a)

Exchanging-edge operation
: A new strongly
graceful labeling
\varphi
of
T
holding
\varphi(x)=\phi(y)
and
\varphi(y)=\phi(x)
for
each matching edge
xy\in M(T)
.

(b)

Decreasing-leaf operation
: If a matching edge
xy\in M(T)
satisfies the neighborhood
N(x)=\{y,x_{i}:i\in[1,d_{x}-1]\}
with
d_{x}=\textrm{deg}_{T}(x)\geq 3
and
deg_{T}(y)=1
. Deleting the edges
xx_{j}
and joins
y
with
x_{j}
for
j\in[s+1,d_{x}-1]
with
0\leq s<d_{x}-1
produces a new tree
H
such that
H
is also strongly graceful, and its perfect matching
M(H)
keeping
|L(T)\cap V(M(T))|-1=|L(H)\cap V(M(H))|
.

(c)

Increasing-leaf operation
: If a matching edge
xy\in M(T)
satisfies
deg_{T}(x)\geq 2
and
deg_{T}(y)\geq 2
. A process of (i) moving the edge
xy
from
T
; (ii) identifying the vertex
x
with the vertex
y
into one denoted as
x_{0}
; and (iii) adding a new vertex
y_{0}
to join with
x_{0}
will build up another tree
T\,^{\prime}
admitting a strongly graceful labeling. Furthermore, the perfect matching
M(T\,^{\prime})=\{x_{0}y_{0}\}\cup\big(M(T)\setminus\{xy\}\big)
golding
|L(T)\cap V(M(T))|+1=|L(T\,^{\prime})\cap V(M(T\,^{\prime}))|
true.

By Lemma
23
and Lemma
24
we have

Theorem 25
.

[
32
]
For a tree
T
having a perfect matching
M
and admitting a set-ordered graceful labeling defined in Definition
5
, there exist eight strongly 2-parameter graph labelings defined in Definition
78
, such that they are equivalent from each other.

2.7
Meta-type of labelings

Definition 79
.

[
88
]
Suppose that a connected
(p,q)
-graph
G
admits a mapping
\pi:V(G)\rightarrow\{0,1,2,\dots\}
. The induced edge colors of edges
xy\in E(G)
are defined as
\pi(xy)=|\pi(x)-\pi(y)|
. Write
\pi(V(G))=\{\pi(u):u\in V(G)\}
and
\pi(E(G))=\{\pi(xy):xy\in E(G)\}
, and there are constraints:

(a)

|\pi(V(G))|=p
;

(b)

|\pi(E(G))|=q
;

(c)

\pi(V(G))\subseteq[0,q]
,
\min\pi(V(G))=0
;

(d)

\pi(V(G))\subset[0,2q-1]
,
\min\pi(V(G))=0
;

(e)

\pi(E(G))=\{\pi(xy):xy\in E(G)\}=[1,q]
;

(f)

\pi(E(G))=\{\pi(xy):xy\in E(G)\}=[1,2q-1]^{o}
;

(g)

G
is a bipartite graph with bipartition
(X,Y)
such that
\max\{\pi(x):x\in X\}<\min\{\pi(y):y\in Y\}
,
and write this case as
\pi(X)<\pi(Y)
for short;

(h)

Each
u\in V(G)
holds
\pi(x)<\pi(u)
for
x\in N(u)
(resp.
\pi(z)>\pi(u)
for
z\in N(u)
) true;

(i)

G
is a bipartite graph with bipartition
(X,Y)
such that every vertex
y\in Y
holds
\pi(x)<\pi(y)
when
x\in N(y)
;

(j)

G
has a perfect matching
M
such that
\pi(x)+\pi(y)=q
for
xy\in M
; and

(k)

G
has a perfect matching
M
such that
\pi(x)+\pi(y)=2q-1
for
xy\in M
.

We have
: A
proper labeling

\pi
holds (
a
) true and, a
proper total labeling

\pi
holds (
a
) and
(
b
) true.

Graceful class
: (1) a
graceful labeling

\pi
holds (
a
), (
c
) and (
e
) true; (2) a
set-ordered graceful labeling

\pi
holds (
a
),
(
c
), (
e
) and (
g
) true; (3) a
strongly graceful labeling

\pi
holds (
a
), (
c
), (
e
) and (
j
) true; (4) a
strongly set-ordered graceful labeling

\pi
holds (
a
), (
c
), (
e
), (
g
) and (
j
) true; (5) a
vertex-ordered graceful
labeling

\pi
holds (
a
), (
c
), (

) and (
h
) true; (6) a
meta-set-ordered graceful labeling

\pi
holds (

), (

), (
e
) and (
i
) true; (7) a
strongly meta-set-ordered graceful labeling

\pi
holds (
a
), (
c
), (

), (
i
) and (
j
) true.

Odd-graceful class
: (1) an
odd-graceful labeling

\pi
holds (
a
), (

) and (

) true; (2) a

\pi
holds (

), (

), (

) and (

) true; (3) a

\pi
holds (

), (

), (

) and
(

), (

), (

), (

) and (

) true; (5) a

\pi
holds (

), (

), (

) and (

) true; (6) a

), (

Remark 14
.

The condition ‘set-ordered’ is stronger than ‘meta-set-ordered’, and furthermore ‘meta-set-ordered’ is stronger than ‘vertex-ordered’ in Definition

79
. Clearly, a set-ordered graceful labeling is a meta-set-ordered graceful labeling, also, a vertex-ordered graceful labeling.
\parallel

Definition 80
.

[
88
]
Let
G
be a bipartite
(p,q)
-graph with bipartition
(X,Y)
, where
X=\{x_{i}:i\in[1,s]\}
and
Y=\{y_{j}:j\in[1,t]\}
. We have the
following labelings generated from a proper labeling
\pi
with
\pi(x_{i})<\pi(x_{i+1})
for
i\in[1,s-1]
and
\pi(y_{j})<\pi(y_{j+1})
for
j\in[1,t-1]
.

(i)
The
dual labeling

\pi\,^{\prime}
of
\pi
is defined as
\pi\,^{\prime}(u)=\max\pi(V(G))+\min\pi(V(G))-\pi(u)
for
u\in V(G)
.

(ii)
The
partially
X
-dual labeling

\phi
of
\pi
is defined as
\phi(x)=\pi(x_{s})+\pi(x_{1})-\pi(x)
for
x\in X
, and
\phi(y)=\pi(y)
for
y\in Y
; and the
partially
Y
-dual
labeling

\varphi
of
\pi
defined as
\varphi(y)=\pi(y_{t})+\pi(y_{1})-\pi(y)
for
y\in Y
, and
\varphi(x)=\pi(x)
for
x\in X
.

(iii)
The
partially
X
-reciprocal transformation

\pi^{-1}_{X}
of
\pi
is defined as
\pi^{-1}_{X}(x_{i})=\pi(x_{s-i+1})
for
x_{i}\in X
, and
\pi^{-1}_{X}(y_{j})=\pi(y_{j})
for
y_{j}\in Y
. The
partially
Y
-reciprocal transformation

\pi^{-1}_{Y}
of
\pi
is defined as
\pi^{-1}_{Y}(y_{j})=\pi(y_{t-j+1})
for
y_{j}\in Y
, and
\pi^{-1}_{Y}(x_{i})=\pi(x_{i})
for
x_{i}\in X
.

(iv)
A
(a,b)
-linear transformation

\psi
of
\pi
is defined as
\psi(x)=a\cdot\pi(x)
for
x\in X
and
\psi(y)=b+a\cdot\pi(y)
for
y\in Y
, where integers
b\geq 0
and
a\geq 1
.
\square

Remark 15
.

By the above Definitions
79
and
80
we have: Suppose that a proper labeling
\pi
of a bipartite graph
G
with the bipartition
(X,Y)
holds
\pi(X)<\pi(Y)
true, where
G
and
\pi
are defined in Definition
79
. Then

(i)
\pi\,^{\prime}(X)<\pi\,^{\prime}(Y)
for the dual labeling
\pi\,^{\prime}
of
\pi
;

(ii)
\phi(X)<\phi(Y)
for the partially
X
-dual labeling
\phi
of
\pi
; and
\varphi(X)<\varphi(Y)
for the partially
Y
-dual labeling
\varphi
of
\pi
; and

(iii)
\max\{\psi(x):x\in X\}<\min\{\psi(y)-b:y\in Y\}
for a
(a,b)
-linear transformation
\psi
of
\pi
, where integers
b\geq 0
and
a\geq 1
.
\parallel

Definition 81
.

[
88
]
Let
Ao(q;k,d)^{o}=\{k+d,k+3d,\dots,k+(2q-1)d\}
for integers
q,d\geq 1
and
k\geq 0
. Suppose a
(p,q)
-graph
G
admits a proper labeling

\pi:V(G)\rightarrow[0,k+(2q-1)d]
for some integers
d\geq 1
and

k\geq 0
.

(i)
If the induced edge color set
\pi(E(G))=\{\pi(uv)=|\pi(u)-\pi(v)|:uv\in E(G)\}=Ao(q;k,d)^{o}
, we say
G
to be
(k,d)
-odd-graceful
, and call
\pi
a
(k,d)
-odd-graceful labeling
of
G
.

(ii)
If the induced edge color set
\{\pi(u)+\pi(v)-k~(\bmod~2qd):uv\in E(G)\}=Ao(q;0,d)^{o}
, we say
G
to be
(k,d)
-odd-elegant
, and call
\pi
a
(k,d)
-odd-elegant labeling
of
G
.
\square

Definition 82
.

[
88
]
If the graph
G
in Definition
81
has a perfect matching
M
such that
\pi(u)+\pi(v)=k+(2q-1)d
for every edge
uv\in M
, then
\pi
is called a
strongly
(k,d)
-odd-graceful labeling
. Similarly, we can define
strongly set-ordered
(k,d)
-odd-graceful labelings
. Suppose that a bipartite
(p,q)
-graph
H
with bipartition
(X,Y)
admits a
(k,d)
-odd-graceful labeling
\pi
such that
\pi(x)=i_{x}d
for
x\in X
and
\pi(y)=k+i_{y}d
for
y\in Y
.

(1) If
\max\{\pi(x):x\in X\}<\min\{\pi(y)-k:y\in Y\}
, we call
\pi
a
set-ordered
(k,d)
-odd-graceful labeling
(resp.
set-ordered
(k,d)
-odd-elegant labeling
).

(2)
\pi
is called a
meta-set-ordered
(k,d)
-odd-graceful labeling
(resp. meta-set-ordered
(k,d)
-odd-elegant labeling) if there are some
x_{0}\in X
and
y_{0}\in Y
such that
\pi(x_{0})>\pi(y_{0})-k
, and
\pi(u)-k>\max\{\pi(v):v\in N(u)\}
for each vertex
u\in Y
.

(3)
\pi
is called a
separately meta-set-ordered
(k,d)
-odd-graceful labeling
(resp. separately meta-set-ordered
(k,d)
-odd-elegant labeling) if there are some
x_{0}\in X
and
y_{0}\in Y
such that
\pi(x_{0})>\pi(y_{0})-k
, and every vertex
u\in Y
holds
\pi(u)-k>\max\{\pi(v):v\in N(u)\}
and
\pi(u)-k+d\neq\pi(x)
for every vertex
x\in X
.
\square

Remark 16
.

In Definition
81
, a proper labeling
\pi
means that integers
d\geq 1
and
k\geq 0
together hold
\pi(u)\neq\pi(v)
for distinct vertices
u,v\in V(G)
. A separately
meta-set-ordered
(0,1)^{o}
-graceful labeling is a meta-set-ordered odd-graceful labeling, and a strongly separated and meta-set-ordered
(0,1)^{o}
-graceful labeling is a strongly meta-set-ordered odd-graceful labeling. A
(0,1)^{o}
-elegant labeling is an odd-elegant labeling defined in
[
122
]
.
\parallel

Figure 25:
For illustrating Definition
81
: (a) A set-ordered
(k,d)
-odd-graceful labeling
f
for all integers
d\geq 1
and
k\geq 0
; (b) the dual labeling of
f
is set-ordered
(k,d)
-odd-graceful for all integers
d\geq 1
and
k\geq 0
; (c) a meta-set-ordered
(k,d)
-odd-elegant labeling for
k\neq d
; (d) a graph
G
admitting a set-ordered
(k,d)
-odd-graceful labeling for all integers
d\geq 1
and
k\geq 0
, cited from
[
88
]
.

Figure 26:
For illustrating Definition
81
, a tree
T
admits: (a) a meta-set-ordered
(k,d)
-odd-graceful labeling for
(k,d)\not\in\{(d,d),(5d,d)\}
; (b) a separately meta-set-ordered
(k,d)
-odd-graceful labeling for
k\neq 3d,5d
; (c) a
(k,d)
-graceful labeling for
k\not\in\{2d,3d,4d\}
is balanced if
k>m=4d
, cited from
[
88
]
.

2.8
Labelings with more restrictions

Definition 83
.

[
54
]
A total labeling
f:V(G)\cup E(G)\rightarrow[1,p+q]
for a bipartite
(p,q)
-graph
G
is a bijection and holds:

(i) (e-magic)
f(uv)+|f(u)-f(v)|=k
;

(ii) (ee-difference) each edge
uv
matches with another edge
xy
holding one of
f(uv)=|f(x)-f(y)|
and
f(uv)=2(p+q)-|f(x)-f(y)|
true;

(iii) (ee-balanced) let
s(uv)=|f(u)-f(v)|-f(uv)
for
uv\in E(G)
, then there exists a constant
k\,^{\prime}
such that each edge
uv
matches with another edge
u\,^{\prime}v\,^{\prime}
holding one of
s(uv)+s(u\,^{\prime}v\,^{\prime})=k\,^{\prime}
and
2(p+q)+s(uv)+s(u\,^{\prime}v\,^{\prime})=k\,^{\prime}
true;

(iv) (EV-ordered)
\min f(V(G))>\max f(E(G))
, or
\max f(V(G))<\min f(E(G))
, or
f(V(G))\subseteq f(E(G))
, or
f(E(G))

\subseteq f(V(G))
, or
f(V(G))
is an odd-set and
f(E(G))
is an even-set;

(v) (ve-matching) there exists a constant
k\,^{\prime\prime}
such that each edge
uv
matches with one vertex
w
such that
f(uv)+f(w)=k\,^{\prime\prime}
, and each vertex
z
matches with one edge
xy
such that
f(z)+f(xy)=k\,^{\prime\prime}
, except the
singularity

f(x_{0})=\lfloor\frac{p+q+1}{2}\rfloor
;

(vi) (set-ordered)
\max f(X)<\min f(Y)
(resp.
\min f(X)>\max f(Y)
) for the bipartition
(X,Y)
of
V(G)
.

We refer to
f
as a
6C-labeling
of
G
.
\square

Definition 84
.

[
53
]
A
(p,q)
-graph
G
admits a total labeling
f:V(G)\cup E(G)\rightarrow[1,4q-1]
. If this labeling
f
holds:

(i) (e-magic)
f(uv)+|f(u)-f(v)|=k
, and
f(uv)
is odd;

(ii) (ee-difference) each edge
uv
matches with another edge
xy
holding
f(uv)=2q+|f(x)-f(y)|
;

(iii) (ee-balanced) let
s(uv)=|f(u)-f(v)|-f(uv)
for
uv\in E(G)
, then there exists a constant
k\,^{\prime}
such that each edge
uv
matches with another edge
u\,^{\prime}v\,^{\prime}
holding one of
s(uv)+s(u\,^{\prime}v\,^{\prime})=k\,^{\prime}
and
(p+q+1)+s(uv)+s(u\,^{\prime}v\,^{\prime})=k\,^{\prime}
true;

(iv) (EV-ordered)
\max f(V(G))<\min f(E(G))
, and
\{|a-b|:a,b\in f(V(G))\}=[1,2q-1]^{o}
;

(v) (ve-matching) there exists two constant
k_{1},k_{2}
such that each edge
uv
matches with one vertex
w
such that
f(uv)+f(w)=k_{1}
or
k_{2}
;

(vi) (set-ordered)
\max f(X)<\min f(Y)
for the bipartition
(X,Y)
of
V(G)
.

We call
f
an
odd-6C-labeling
of
G
.
\square

Definition 85
.

[
54
]
A total labeling
f:V(G)\cup E(G)\rightarrow[1,p+q]
for a bipartite
(p,q)
-graph
G
is a bijection and holds:

(i) (e-magic)
f(uv)+|f(u)-f(v)|=k
;

(ii) (ee-difference) each edge
uv
matches with another edge
xy
holding one of
f(uv)=|f(x)-f(y)|
and
f(uv)=2(p+q)-|f(x)-f(y)|
true;

(iii) (ee-balanced) let
s(uv)=|f(u)-f(v)|-f(uv)
for
uv\in E(G)
, then there exists a constant
k\,^{\prime}
such that each edge
uv
matches with another edge
u\,^{\prime}v\,^{\prime}
holding one of
s(uv)+s(u\,^{\prime}v\,^{\prime})=k\,^{\prime}
and
2(p+q)+s(uv)+s(u\,^{\prime}v\,^{\prime})=k\,^{\prime}
true;

(iv) (EV-ordered)
\min f(V(G))>\max f(E(G))
(resp.
\max f(V(G))<\min f(E(G))
, or
f(V(G))\subseteq f(E(G))
, or
f(E(G))

\subseteq f(V(G))
, or
f(V(G))
is an odd-set and
f(E(G))
is an even-set);

(v) (ve-matching) there exists a constant
k\,^{\prime\prime}
such that each edge

w
such that
f(uv)+f(w)=k\,^{\prime\prime}
, and each vertex
z
matches with one edge
xy
such that
f(z)+f(xy)=k\,^{\prime\prime}
, except the

f(x_{0})=\lfloor\frac{p+q+1}{2}\rfloor
;

(vi) (set-ordered)
\max f(X)<\min f(Y)
(resp.
\min f(X)>\max f(Y)
) for the bipartition

of

.

(vii) (odd-even separable)
f(V(G))
is an odd-set containing only odd numbers, as well as
f(E(G))
is an even-set containing only even numbers.

We refer to

as an

.
\square

Definition 86
.

[
53
,
61
]
A
(p,q)
-graph
G
admits a
multiple edge-meaning vertex labeling

f:V(G)\cup E(G)\rightarrow[0,p+q]
if

(1)
f(E(G))=[1,q]
and
f(u)+f(uv)+f(v)=k
;

(2)
f(E(G))=[p,p+q-1]
and
f(u)+f(uv)+f(v)=k\,^{\prime}
;

(3)
f(E(G))=[0,q-1]
and
f(uv)=f(u)+f(v)~(\bmod~q)
;

(4)
f(E(G))=[1,q]
and
|f(u)+f(v)-f(uv)|=
a constant
k\,^{\prime\prime}
; and

(5)
f(uv)=
an odd number for each edge
uv\in E(G)
holding
f(E(G))=[1,2q-1]^{o}
, and
\{f(u)+f(uv)+f(v):uv\in E(T)\}=[a,b]
with
b-a+1=q
.
\square

Definition 87
.

[
53
]
Let
f
be an odd-graceful labeling of a
(p,q)
-graph
G
defined in Definition
5
, such that
f(V(G))\subset[0,2q-1]^{o}
and
f(E(G))=[1,2q-1]^{o}
. If
\{|a-b|:~a,b\in f(V(G))\}=[1,p]
, then
f
is called a
perfect odd-graceful labeling
of
G
.
\square

Definition 88
.

[
53
]
Suppose that a
(p,q)
-graph
G
admits an
\varepsilon
-labeling
h:V(G)\rightarrow S\subseteq[0,p+q]
. If
\{|a-b|:~a,b\in f(V(G))\}=[1,p]
, we call
f
a
perfect
\varepsilon
-labeling
of
G
.
\square

Definition 89
.

[
54
]
For a given
(p,q)
-tree
G
admitting a 6C-labeling
f
defined in Definition
83
, and another
(p,q)
-tree
H
admits a 6C-labeling
g
, if both graphs
G
and
H
hold
f(V(G))\setminus X^{*}=g(E(H))
,
f(E(G))=g(V(H))\setminus X^{*}
and
f(V(G))\cap g(V(H))=X^{*}=\{z_{0}\}
with
z_{0}=\lfloor\frac{p+q+1}{2}\rfloor
, then both labelings
f
and
g
are pairwise
reciprocal-inverse
. The vertex-coincided graph
\odot_{1}\langle G,H\rangle
obtained by vertex-coinciding the vertex
x_{0}
of
G
having
f(x_{0})=z_{0}
with the vertex
w_{0}
of
H
having
g(w_{0})=z_{0}
is called a
6C-complementary matching
.
\square

Definition 90
.

[
54
]
Suppose that a
(p,q)
-graph
G
admits a
W
-type labeling
f:V(G)\cup E(G)\rightarrow[1,p+q]
, and a
(q,p)
-graph
H
admits another
W
-type labeling
g:V(H)\cup E(H)\rightarrow[1,p+q]
. If
f(E(G))=g(V(H))\setminus X^{*}
and
f(V(G))\setminus X^{*}=g(E(H))
for
X^{*}=f(V(G))\cap g(V(H))\neq\emptyset
, then both
W
-type labelings
f
and
g
are
reciprocal-inverse
(resp.
reciprocal complementary
) from each other, and
H
(resp.
G
) is an
inverse matching
of
G
(resp.
H
).
\square

2.9
Image-type of labelings

2.9.1
Normal image-type of labelings

Definition 91
.

[
53
]
Suppose that a connected graph
G
admits two
W_{i}
-type labelings
f_{i}
for
i=1,2
. There are constants
M,k
and
k\,^{\prime}
in the following constraint conditions:

C-1.

f_{1}(uv)+f_{2}(uv)=M_{edge}
for each edge
uv\in E(G)
, where
M_{edge}=\max\{f_{1}(uv):uv\in E(G)\}+\min\{f_{1}(uv):uv\in E(G)\}
;

C-2.

f_{1}(x)+f_{2}(x)=M_{vertex}
for each vertex
x\in V(G)
, where
M_{vertex}=\max\{f_{1}(x):x\in V(G)\}+\min\{f_{1}(x):x\in V(G)\}
;

C-3.

f_{1}(w)+f_{2}(w)=M
for
w\in V(G)\cup E(G)
;

C-4.

f_{1}(x)+f_{2}(x)=k
for each vertex
x\in V(G)
; and

C-5.

f_{1}(uv)+f_{2}(uv)=k\,^{\prime}
for each edge
uv\in E(G)
.

Then we have
:

(i)

f_{2}
is an
edge-dual labeling
of
f_{1}
if C-
1
holds true.

(ii)

f_{2}
is a
vertex-dual labeling
of
f_{1}
if C-
2
holds true.

(iii)

f_{i}
is a
total dual labeling
of
f_{3-i}
for
i=1,2
if C-
3
holds true.

(iv)

f_{i}
is a
vertex-image labeling
of
f_{3-i}
for
i=1,2
if C-
4
holds true.

(v)

f_{i}
is an
edge-image labeling
of
f_{3-i}
for
i=1,2
if C-
5
holds true.

(vi)

f_{i}
is a
totally image labeling
of
f_{3-i}
for
i=1,2
if C-
4
and C-
5
hold true.
\square

Definition 92
.

[
53
]
Let
f_{i}:V(G)\rightarrow[a,b]
be a labeling of a
(p,q)
-graph
G
and let each edge
uv\in E(G)
have its own color as
f_{i}(uv)=|f_{i}(u)-f_{i}(v)|
with
i=1,2
. If each edge
uv\in E(G)
holds
f_{1}(uv)+f_{2}(uv)=k
true, where
k
is a positive constant, we call both labelings
f_{1}
and
f_{2}
are a pair of
image-labelings
, and
f_{i}
a
mirror-image
of
f_{3-i}
with
i=1,2
(see Fig.
27
).
\square

Both (a) and (b) are a matching of

with
f_{a}(uv)+h_{b}(uv)=17
; both (c) and (d) are a matching of

with
f_{c}(uv)+h_{d}(uv)=32
, cited from
[

]
.

Definition 93
.

[
41
]
Let
f:V(G)\cup E(G)\rightarrow[a,b]
and
g:V(G)\cup E(G)\rightarrow[a\,^{\prime},b\,^{\prime}]
be two labelings of a
(p,q)
-graph
G
, integers
a,b,a\,^{\prime},b\,^{\prime}
satisfy
0\leq a<b
and
0\leq a\,^{\prime}<b\,^{\prime}
.

(1) An equation
f(v)+g(v)=k\,^{\prime}
holds true for each vertex
v\in V(G)
, where
k\,^{\prime}
is a positive constant and it is called
vertex-image coefficient
, then both labelings
f
and
g
are called a matching of
vertex image-labelings
(abbreviated as
v-image-labelings
);

(2) An equation
f(uv)+g(uv)=k\,^{\prime\prime}
holds true for every edge
uv\in E(G)
, and
k\,^{\prime\prime}
is a positive constant, called
edge-image coefficient
, then both labelings
f
and
g
are called a matching of
edge image-labelings
(abbreviated as
e-image-labelings
).
\square

,

.

Definition 94
.

[
53
]
A
(p,q)
-graph
G
admits two
(k,d)
-harmonious labelings
f_{i}:V(G)\rightarrow X_{0}\cup S_{q-1,k,d}
with
i=1,2
, where
X_{0}=\{0,d,2d,\dots,(q-1)d\}
and
S_{q-1,k,d}=\{k,k+d,k+2d,\dots,k+(q-1)d\}
, such that each edge
uv\in E(G)
is colored with
f_{i}(uv)-k=[f_{i}(uv)+f_{i}(uv)-k~(\textrm{mod}~qd)]
with
i=1,2
. If
f_{1}(uv)+f_{2}(uv)=2k+(q-1)d
, we call both labelings
f_{1}
and
f_{2}
a
matching
(k,d)
-harmonious image-labelings
of
G
.
\square

Theorem 26
.

[
41
]
Let
T
be a tree with
p
vertices and
q
edges, its vertex partition is
(X,Y)
, where
|X|=s
and
|Y|=t
. By Definition
93
, if
T
admits a set-ordered graceful labeling, the following assertions are mutually equivalent:

(1)

T
admits a
matching of graceful
v
-image-labelings
with
k=p-1
.

(2)

T
admits a
matching of odd-graceful
v
-image-labelings
with
k=2p-3
.

(3)

T
admits a
matching of felicitous
v
-image-labelings
with
k=p-1
.

(4)

T
admits a
matching of odd-elegant
v
-image-labelings
with
k=p
.

(5)

T
admits a
matching of super edge-magic total
v
-image-labelings
with
k=p+1
.

(6)

T
admits a
matching of set-ordered
(k,d)
-graceful
v
-image-labelings
with
k=k\,^{\prime\prime}+(q-1)d
.

(7)

T
admits a
super
(s+p+3,2)
-edge anti-magic total v-image-laeblling
and a
super
(s+p+4,2)
-edge anti-magic total labeling
, they are a
matching of
v
-image-labelings
with
k=p+1
.

(8)

T
admits a
matching of
(k,2)
-arithmetic total
v
-image-labelings
with
k=k\,^{\prime\prime}+2(t-1)
.

Corollary 27
.

[
41
]
By Definition
93
, if a tree
T
admits a set-ordered graceful labeling, then the following propositions hold and are equivalent from each other.

(1)

T
admits a
matching of edge-magic total v
e
-image-labelings
with
k=p+1
and
k\,^{\prime}=2p+q+1
.

(2)

T
admits a
(s+p+3,2)
-edge anti-magic total labeling and a
(s+p+4,2)
-edge anti-magic total labeling
such that they are a
matching of v
e
-image-labelings
with
k=p+1
and
k\,^{\prime}=2p+q+1
.

(3)

T
admits a
matching of
(k,2)
-arithmetic total v
e
-image-labelings
with
k=k\,^{\prime\prime}+2(t-1)
and
k\,^{\prime}=2(k\,^{\prime\prime}+2t-2)
.

.

[

]

, if a tree

Corollary 29
.

[
41
]
By Definition
93
, if a tree
T
admit a matching of
v
-image-labelings
f,g
and a matching of
e
-image-labelings
f,h
, then the following propositions hold, and

(1) the set
\{f(v)+g(v)+h(v):v\in V(T)\}
is an arithmetic sequence if and only if
\{f(uv)+g(uv)+h(uv):uv\in E(T)\}
is an arithmetic sequence, and the tolerances are equal;

(2) the set
\{f(v)+g(v)+h(v):v\in V(T)\}
contains two arithmetic sequences if and only if
\{f(uv)+g(uv)+h(uv):uv\in E(T)\}
is an arithmetic sequences with a tolerance of 2.

Theorem 30
.

[
41
]
By Definition
93
, if a tree
T
admits a graceful labeling
f
, then it admits an
edge-magic total labeling

g
, so that both labelings
f
and
g
are a
matching of
e
-image-labelings
with
k\,^{\prime}=p+q+1
.

Theorem 31
.

[
41
]
If a tree
T
admits a graceful labeling
f
, then it admits an
set-ordered
(s+p+3,2)
-edge anti-magic total labeling

g
, so that both labelings
f
and
g
are a
matching of
e
-image-labelings
with
k\,^{\prime}=p+q+1
.

2.9.2
Duality image-type of labelings

Let
\theta:V(L)\cup E(L)\rightarrow[1,M]
be a labeling (resp. coloring) of a graph
L
, so we have three parameters:

{\begin{split}M^{v}_{m}(L,\theta)=&\max\theta(V(L))+\min\theta(V(L))\\
M^{e}_{m}(L,\theta)=&\max\theta(E(L))+\min\theta(E(L))\\
M^{ve}_{m}(L,\theta)=&\max\theta(V(L)\cup E(L))+\min\theta(V(L)\cup E(L))\end{split}}

(6)

We show the following duality-image
(W_{f},W_{g})
-type labelings (resp. colorings) including homomorphic labelings and homomorphic colorings:

Definition 95
.

[
65
]
Suppose that a connected
(p,q)
-graph
G
admits a
W_{f}
-type labeling (resp. coloring)
f
, and another connected
(p\,^{\prime},q\,^{\prime})
-graph
H
admits a
W_{g}
-type labeling (resp. coloring)
g
. There is a bijection
\varphi:V(G)\cup E(G)\rightarrow V(H)\cup E(H)
such that the image edge
\varphi(u)\varphi(v)\in E(H)
for each edge
uv\in E(G)
. By the parameters defined in (
6
), there are the following constraint conditions:

(C-1)

g(\varphi(x))=M^{v}_{m}(G,f)-f(x)
for
x\in V(G)
;

(C-2)

g(\varphi(u)\varphi(v))=M^{e}_{m}(G,f)-f(uv)
for
uv\in E(G)
;

(C-3)

g(\varphi(w))=M^{ve}_{m}(G,f)-f(w)
for
w\in V(G)\cup E(G)
and
\varphi(w)\in V(H)\cup E(H)
;

(C-4)

f(\varphi^{-1}(y))=M^{v}_{m}(H,g)-g(y)
for
y\in V(H)
and
\varphi^{-1}(y)\in V(G)
;

(C-5)

f(\varphi^{-1}(w)\varphi^{-1}(z))=M^{e}_{m}(H,g)-g(wz)
for
wz\in E(H)
and
\varphi^{-1}(w)\varphi^{-1}(z)\in E(G)
;

(C-6)

f(\varphi^{-1}(z))=M^{ve}_{m}(H,g)-g(z)
for
z\in V(H)\cup E(H)
and
\varphi^{-1}(z)\in V(G)\cup E(G)
;

(C-7)

f(x)+g(\varphi(x))=A_{v}
for
x\in V(G)
;

(C-8)

f(x)+g(\varphi(u)\varphi(v))=A_{e}
for
uv\in E(G)
; and

(C-9)

f(w)+g(\varphi(w))=A_{ve}
for
w\in V(G)\cup E(G)
and
\varphi(w)\in V(H)\cup E(H)
.

Then

Labe-1.

g
(resp.
f
) is a
vertex-dual labeling (resp. coloring)
of
f
(resp.
g
) if (C-
1
) (resp. (C-

)) holds true.

(resp.

) holds true.

2.10
Improper-type of labelings

Definition 96
.

[
106
]
Let
G
be a
(p,q)
-graph, and
f
be a mapping from
V(G)
to
\{0,1,2,\dots\}
such that
\min f(V(G))=0
and
\max f(V(G))=m
, where
f(V(G))=\{f(x):x\in V(G)\}
. Write
f(E(G))=\{f(uv)=|f(u)-f(v)|:uv\in E(G)\}
. There are four restrictions:

C1.
f(E(G))=[1,q]
and
m\leq q
;

C2.
f(E(G))=[1,q]
and
m>q
;

C3.
f(E(G))=[1,2q-1]^{o}
and
m\leq 2q-1
;

C4.
f(E(G))=[1,2q-1]^{o}
and
m>2q-1
.

Then,
f
is called an
in-improper graceful labeling
(in-imgl) if
f
holds C1, an
out-improper graceful labeling
(out-imgl) if
f
holds C2, an
in-improper odd-graceful labeling
(in-imoddgl) if
f
holds C3, and an
out-improper odd-graceful labeling
(out-imoddgl) if
f
holds C4.

Theorem 32
.

Every tree admits
(i)

in-imgls
or
out-imgls
; and
(ii)

in-imoddgls
or
out-imoddgls
(refer to Definition
96
).

The term “adding leaves to a
(p,q)
-graph
G
” means that we join new vertices
u_{i,1},u_{i,2},\dots,u_{i,m_{i}}
to each vertex
u_{i}
of
G
, here, some
m_{i}
may be zero for
i\in[1,p]
. So
u_{i,1},u_{i,2},\dots,u_{i,m_{i}}
for
i\in[1,p]
are leaves in the resultant graph.

Theorem 33
.

Suppose that a tree
T
admit set-ordered graceful/odd-graceful labelings. Then adding leaves to
T
produces a tree that admits
in-imgls
(refer to Definition
96
) and proper
odd-graceful labelings
.

Theorem 34
.

Every lobster admits
in-imgls
(refer to Definition
96
) and proper
odd-graceful labelings
.

Theorem 35
.

Let
G
be a connected bipartite graph having vertices
w_{1},w_{2},\dots,w_{n}
. For each fixed integer
i\in[1,n]
, identifying a certain vertex of every connected bipartite graph
G_{i,j}
for
j\in[1,m_{i}]
(it is allowed some
m_{i}=0
) with vertex
w_{i}
into one vertex produces a connected graph
H^{*}
admitting
in-imgls
(resp.
in-imoddgls
, refer to Definition
96
) if
G
and every
G_{i,j}
admit set-ordered graceful labelings (resp. set-ordered odd-graceful labelings).

2.11
Pan-labelings

2.11.1
Pan-graceful labelings

Definition 97
.

[
35
]
For a
(p,q)
-graph
G
, if there is a labeling
f:V(G)\rightarrow\{0,a_{1},a_{2},\dots
,
a_{q}\}
with
0<a_{i}<a_{j}
and
1\leq i<j\leq q
such that the edge color set
\{f(uv)=|f(u)-f(v)|:uv\in E(G)\}=\{a_{1},a_{2},\cdots,a_{q}\}
, then we call
f
a
pan-graceful labeling
of
G
.
\square

2.11.2
Pan-type mean labelings

Definition 98
.

∗
A
(p,q)
-graph
G
admits a labeling
f:V(G)\rightarrow[0,M_{f}]
, and the induced edge color
f(uv)=\big\lceil\frac{f(u)+f(v)}{2}\big\rceil
. There are the following constraint conditions:

Cs-1.

f(V(G))\subseteq[0,q]
;

Cs-2.

f(V(G))\subset[0,2q-1]
;

Cs-3.

f(E(G))=[1,q]
; and

Cs-4.

f(E(G))=[1,2q-1]^{o}
.

We call
f
:

MeanL-1.

A
graceful mean labeling
if Cs-
1
and Cs-
3
hold true.

MeanL-2.

A
pan-graceful mean labeling
if Cs-
3
holds true.

MeanL-3.

An
odd-graceful mean labeling
if Cs-
2
and Cs-
4
hold true.

MeanL-4.

A
pan-odd-graceful mean labeling
if Cs-
4
holds true.
\square

For understanding Definition
98
, see Fig.
30
.

Figure 30:
(a) A graceful mean labeling; (b) a pan-graceful mean labeling; (c) an odd-graceful mean labeling; (d) a graph having a triangle does not admit any odd-graceful labeling, but it admits an odd-graceful mean labeling.

Definition 99
.

∗
In Definition
10
of an edge-magic graceful labeling, we substitute the condition “
|f(u)+f(v)-f(uv)|=k
and
f(V(G)\cup E(G))=[1,p+q]
” by one of “
|f(u)+f(v)-f(uv)|=k
or
(p+q)-|f(u)+f(v)-f(uv)|=k
such that
f(V(G)\cup E(G))=[1,p+q]
”. The resulting labeling is called a
pan-edge-magic graceful labeling
.
\square

Definition 100
.

∗
A
pan-edge-magic total graceful labeling

g
is obtained by replacing the restriction “
g(uv)+|g(u)-g(v)|=k
” by one of
g(uv)+|g(u)-g(v)|=k
and
g(uv)+(p+q)-|g(u)-g(v)|=k
in Definition
33
.
\square

Definition 101
.

∗
A
pan-odd-graceful labeling
is obtained by replacing the restriction “
f(uv)=|f(u)-f(v)|
and
f(E(G))=[1,2q-1]^{o}
” by a new one of
f(uv)=|f(u)-f(v)|
,
f(uv)=2q-1-|f(u)-f(v)|
and
f(E(G))=[1,2q-1]^{o}
Definition
5
.
\square

As an example, a lobster
T
demonstrated in Fig.
31
admits:

(1) a pan-edge-magic total labeling
f_{1}
such that
f_{1}(u)+f_{1}(uv)+f_{1}(v)=16
;

(2) a pan-edge-magic total labeling
f_{2}
holding
f_{2}(u)+f_{2}(uv)+f_{2}(v)=27
true;

(3) a felicitous labeling
f_{3}
satisfying
f_{3}(uv)=f_{3}(u)+f_{3}(v)~(\bmod~11)
;

(4) an edge-magic graceful labeling
f_{4}
such that
|f_{4}(u)+f_{4}(v)-f_{4}(uv)|=4
;

(5) an edge-odd-graceful labeling
f_{5}
keeping
f_{5}(uv)=
an odd number,
\{f_{5}(u)+f_{5}(uv)+f_{5}(v):uv\in E(T)\}=[16,26]
.

So,
T
admits an
e-set v-proper labeling
(see Definition
155
) defined as follows: Let
f:V(T)\rightarrow[0,11]
with
f(a)=0
,
f(c)=1
,
f(d)=2
,
f(w)=3
,
f(u)=4
,
f(y)=5
,
f(r)=6
,
f(s)=7
,
f(e)=8
,
f(x)=9
,
f(v)=10
,
f(t)=11
. And each edge of
T
has its own color set as follows:

\begin{array}[]{lll}f^{\prime}(ay)=\{1,5,11,21,22\},&f^{\prime}(cy)=\{2,6,10,19,21\},&f^{\prime}(de)=\{6,10,11,17\},\\
f^{\prime}(dy)=\{3,7,9,17,20\},&f^{\prime}(dr)=\{4,8,15,19\},&f^{\prime}(ds)=\{5,7,9,13,18\},\\
f^{\prime}(dt)=\{2,3,5,9,14\},&f^{\prime}(ew)=\{0,5,7,9,16\},&f^{\prime}(ut)=\{1,4,11,12\},\\
f^{\prime}(xw)=\{1,4,7,8,15\},&f^{\prime}(uv)=\{2,3,10,13\}.\end{array}
