2.12
Sum-type of labelings

Definition 102
.

[
53
]
Let
f:V(G)\rightarrow[0,q]
be a proper labeling of a
(p,q)
-graph
G
, and let

S_{um}(G,f)=\sum_{uv\in E(G)}|f(u)-f(v)|,

we call
f
a
difference-sum labeling
. Find two extremal numbers
\max_{f}S_{um}(G,f)
and
\min_{f}S_{um}(G,f)
over all difference-sum labelings of
G
.
\square

Definition 103
.

[
53
]
Let
f:V(G)\rightarrow[0,q]
be a proper labeling of a
(p,q)
-graph
G
, and let

F_{um}(G,f)=\sum_{uv\in E(G)}[f(u)+f(v)]~(\bmod~q+1),

we call
f
a
felicitous-sum labeling
. Find two extremal numbers
\max_{f}F_{um}(G,f)
and
\min_{f}F_{um}(G,f)
over all felicitous-sum labelings of
G
.
\square

Definition 104
.

[
52
]
A connected
(p,q)
-graph
G
admits a labeling
f:V(G)\cup E(G)\rightarrow[1,p+q]
, such that
f(x)\neq f(w)
for any pair of elements
x,w\in V(G)\cup E(G)
. We have the following sums:

F_{ve}(G,f)=\sum_{uv\in E(G)}\big[f(uv)+|f(u)-f(v)|\big],

(7)

F_{|ve|}(G,f)=\sum_{uv\in E(G)}\big|f(u)+f(v)-f(uv)\big|,

(8)

and

F_{magic}(G,f)=\sum_{uv\in E(G)}\big[f(u)+f(uv)+f(v)\big]=kq.

(9)

We call
f
:

(i)

A
ve-sum-difference labeling
of
G
if it holds (
7
) true.

(ii)

A
ve-difference labeling
of
G
if it holds (
8
) true.

(iii)

A
k-edge-average labeling
of
G
if it holds (
9
) true.
\square

Definition 105
.

[
52
]
Suppose that
G_{M}
and
G_{m}
are two copies of a
(p,q)
-graph
G
, and
G_{M}
admits a difference-sum labeling
f_{M}
holding
S_{um}(G_{M},f_{M})=\max_{f}S_{um}(G,f)
true, and
G_{m}
admits another difference-sum labeling
f_{m}
holding
S_{um}(G_{m},f_{m})=\min_{f}S_{um}(G,f)
true. The identifying graph
H=\odot\langle G_{M},G_{m}\rangle
is called a
Max-Min difference-sum matching partition
, moreover if
H=\odot_{p}\langle G_{M},G_{m}\rangle
holds
E(G_{M})\cap E(G_{m})=\emptyset
true, we call
H
a
perfect Max-Min difference-sum matching partition
. (see a perfect Max-Min difference-sum matching partition shown in Fig.
32
(a))
\square

Definition 106
.

[
52
]
Suppose that
H_{M}
and
H_{m}
are two copies of a
(p,q)
-graph
H
, and
H_{M}
admits a felicitous-sum labeling
h_{M}
holding
F_{um}(H_{M},h_{M})=\max_{f}F_{um}(G,f)
true, and
H_{m}
admits another felicitous-sum labeling
h_{m}
holding
F_{um}(H_{m},h_{m})=\min_{f}F_{um}(G,f)
true. The identifying graph
G=\odot\langle H_{M},H_{m}\rangle
is called a
Max-Min felicitous-sum matching partition
, and furthermore we call
G
a
perfect Max-Min felicitous-sum matching partition
if
G=\odot_{p}\langle H_{M},H_{m}\rangle
holds
E(H_{M})\cap E(H_{m})=\emptyset
true. (see a perfect Max-Min felicitous-sum matching partition shown in Fig.
32
(b))
\square

Figure 32:
(a) A perfect Max-Min difference-sum matching partition
A=\odot_{12}\langle G_{a},G_{d}\rangle
; (b) a perfect Max-Min felicitous-sum matching partition
B=\odot_{9}\langle F_{b},F_{c}\rangle
, cited from
[
52
]
.

2.13
Maximal common factor labelings (gcd-labelings)

2.13.1
Graceful-type of gcd-labelings

Definition 107
.

[
42
]
A
(p,q)
-graph
G
admits a coloring
f:V(G)\rightarrow[1,M]
such that each edge
xy\in E(G)
is colored with
f(xy)=\textrm{gcd}(f(u),f(v))
. If the edge color set
f(E(G))=\{f(xy):xy\in E(G)\}=[1,q]
, we call
f
a
graceful gcd-coloring
, and
f
an
odd-graceful gcd-coloring
if
f(E(G))=\{f(xy):xy\in E(G)\}=[1,2q-1]^{o}
.
\square

Figure 33:
Examples for knowing Definition
107
.

Remark 17
.

In Definition
107
, the number

G_{gcd}(G)=\min_{f}\{\max f(V(G)):~f\textrm{ is a graceful gcd-labeling of $G$}\}

is called a
graceful gcd-labeling number
of the
(p,q)
-graph
G
. Similarly, the parameter

OG_{gcd}(G)=\min_{f}\{\max f(V(G)):~f\textrm{ is an odd-graceful gcd-labeling of $G$}\}

is called an
odd-graceful gcd-labeling number
of the
(p,q)
-graph
G
.

Each graph
T_{i}
of
9
edges shown in Fig.
33
admits a graceful gcd-coloring
f_{i}
for
i\in[1,4]
. We define a parameter
M_{mgrac}(T_{i},f_{i})=\max f_{i}(V(T_{i}))-\min f_{i}(V(T_{i}))
for
i\in[1,4]
, so

M_{mgrac}(T_{1})<M_{mgrac}(T_{2})<M_{mgrac}(T_{3})<M_{mgrac}(T_{4}).

We call
M_{mgrac}(T_{i})=\min_{f}\{M_{mgrac}(T_{i},f)\}
the
graceful gcd-coloring M-m-number
(resp. the
odd-graceful gcd-coloring M-m-number

M_{moddgra}(T_{i},f)=\min_{f}\{M_{mgrac}(T_{i},f)\}
).
\parallel

Definition 108
.

∗
For a
(p,q)
-graph
G
, we have:

(1) A
graceful gcd-labeling
is defined as: A
(p,q)
-graph
G
admits a
proper labeling

f:V(G)\rightarrow[1,M]
such that each edge
xy\in E(G)
is colored with
f(xy)=\textrm{gcd}(f(u),f(v))
and
f(E(G))=\{f(xy):xy\in E(G)\}=[1,q]
, and
M_{mgral}(G,f)=\max f(V(G))-\min f(V(G))
.

(2) An
odd-graceful gcd-labeling
is defined as: A
(p,q)
-graph
G
admits a
proper labeling

f:V(G)\rightarrow[1,M]
such that each edge
xy\in E(G)
is colored with
f(xy)=\textrm{gcd}(f(u),f(v))
and
f(E(G))=\{f(xy):xy\in E(G)\}=[1,2q-1]^{o}
, and
M_{moddgral}(G,f)=\max f(V(G))-\min f(V(G))
.

Accordingly, there are two extremal numbers:
graceful gcd-labeling M-m-number

M_{mgral}(G)=\min_{f}\{M_{mgral}(G,f)\}
,
odd-graceful gcd-labeling M-m-number

M_{moddgral}(G)=\min_{f}\{M_{moddgral}(G,f)\}
.

Definition 109
.

[
42
]
A
(p,q)
-graph
G
admits a proper labeling
f:V(G)\rightarrow[1,M]
, such that
\textrm{gcd}(f(u),f(v))
is just a
prime
for each edge
uv\in E(G)
, we call
f
a
prime labeling
of
G
. Moreover, we call
f
an
edge-prime labeling
if
|f(E(G))|=|E(G)|
.
\square

Remark 18
.

In Definition
109
, the extremal number
P_{rime}(G)=\min_{f}\{M\}
over all prime labelings of the
(p,q)
-graph
G
is called a
prime gcd-labeling number
; and the extremal number
P\,^{\prime}_{rime}(G)=\min_{f}\{M\}
over all edge-prime labelings of the
(p,q)
-graph
G
is called an
edge-prime gcd-labeling number
.
\parallel

2.13.2
Distinguishing gcd-labelings

Definition 110
.

∗
Suppose that a
(p,q)
-graph
G
admits a mapping
f:V(G)\rightarrow[1,M_{f}]
, such that
f(u)\neq f(v)
for distinct vertices
u,v\in V(G)
, and
\textrm{gcd}(f(u),f(v))\neq\textrm{gcd}(f(u),f(w))
for any pair of adjacent edges
uv,uw\in E(G)
. Let
F_{N}(x)=\{\textrm{gcd}(f(x),f(y)):~y\in N(x)\}
for the neighbor set
N(x)
of each vertex
x\in V(G)
. We have the following vertex distinguishing common divisor labeling (distinguishing gcd-labeling):

(1)
f
is called an
adjacent distinguishing gcd-labeling
if
F_{N}(u)\neq F_{N}(v)
for any edge
uv\in E(G)
.

(2)
f
is called a
distinguishing gcd-labeling
if
F_{N}(w)\neq F_{N}(z)
for distinct vertices
w,z\in V(G)
.

For all distinguishing gcd-labelings
f:V(G)\rightarrow[1,M_{f}]
, the minimum number of all
M_{f}
is called the
distinguishing gcd-number
of
G
, denoted as
D_{gcd}(G)
. For all adjacent distinguishing gcd-labelings
g:V(G)\rightarrow[1,M_{g}]
, the minimum number of all
M_{g}
is called the
adjacent distinguishing gcd-number
of
G
, denoted as
D_{agcd}(G)
. If
D_{gcd}(G)=p
(resp.
D_{agcd}(G)=p
), we call
f
a
standard (resp. adjacent) distinguishing gcd-labelings
.
\square

Definition 111
.

∗
Suppose that a
(p,q)
-graph
G
admits a mapping
f:V(G)\rightarrow[1,M_{f}]
, such that
f(u)\neq f(v)
for distinct vertices
u,v\in V(G)
, and
\textrm{gcd}(f(u),f(v))\neq\textrm{gcd}(f(u),f(w))
for any pair of adjacent edges
uv,uw\in E(G)
. Let
F_{N}(x)=\{\textrm{gcd}(f(x),f(y)):~y\in N(x)\}
, and add a restriction “
\textrm{gcd}(f(u),f(v))
to be prime” for any edge
uv\in E(G)
, we get:

(i)
f
is called an
adjacent distinguishing prime-labeling
if
F_{N}(u)\neq F_{N}(v)
for any edge
uv\in E(G)
.

(ii)
f
is called a
distinguishing prime-labeling
if
F_{N}(w)\neq F_{N}(z)
for distinct vertices
w,z\in V(G)
.

For all distinguishing prime-labelings
f:V(G)\rightarrow[1,M_{f}]
, the minimum number of all
M_{f}
is called the
distinguishing prime-number
of
G
, denoted as
D_{pri}(G)
. For all adjacent distinguishing prime-labelings
g:V(G)\rightarrow[1,M_{g}]
, the minimum number of all
M_{g}
is called the
adjacent distinguishing prime-number
of
G
, denoted as
D_{apri}(G)
. If
D_{pri}(G)=p
, or
D_{apri}(G)=p
, we call
f

standard (adjacent) distinguishing prime-labelings
.
\square

2.14
Induced total labelings

A
proper
total coloring of a graph
G
is defined on a subset of
V(G)\cup E(G)
such that two adjacent vertices or incident edges or incident of edges and vertices are colored with different colors. Similarly, a mapping
f
from the vertex set
V(G)
to
[0,q]
is a
proper vertex labeling
of
G
if
f(u)\neq f(v)
for each edge
uv\in E(G)
. Write
f(V(G))=\{f(u):u\in V(G)\}
and
f(E(G))=\{f(uv):uv\in E(G)\}
, or
f(V)
and
f(E)
if there is no confliction.

Definition 112
.

[
81
]
For a mapping
f
from the vertex set
V(G)
to
[0,q]
, let
k=\max f(V(G))
and let
s
indicate the number of distinct colors used in
f(V(G))
, we call
f
a
proper
(k,s)
-vertex labeling
of a
(p,q)
-graph
G
. For a
(p,q)
-graph
G
admitting a
(k,s)
-vertex labeling
f
:
V(G)\rightarrow[0,q]
, an
induced difference edge-labeling

i^{-}_{f}
of
f
in
G
is defined as
i^{-}_{f}(uv)=|f(u)-f(v)|
for each edge
uv\in E(G)
; and an
induced harmonious edge-labeling

i^{+}_{f}
of
f
in
G
is defined as
i^{+}_{f}(uv)=f(u)+f(v)
(mod
q
) for each edge
uv\in E(G)
..
\square

Definition 113
.

[
81
]
A proper
(k,s)
-vertex labeling
f
of a
(p,q)
-graph
G
, from
V(G)
to
[0,q]
, is an
induced difference total labeling
(IDT labeling) of
G
if its induced difference edge-labeling
i^{-}_{f}
is a proper edge coloring of
G
. We say
f
to be a
(k,s,t)
-IDT labeling of
G
, where
t=|i^{-}_{f}(E)|
. The least number of
k
spanning over all
(k,s,t)
-IDT labelings of

G
, denoted as
\chi^{-}(G)
, is called the IDT-
chromatic number
.
\square

Definition 114
.

[
81
]
A proper
(k,s)
-vertex labeling
h
of a
(p,q)
-graph
G
, from
V(G)
to
[0,q]
, is an
induced harmonious total labeling
(IHT labeling) of
G
if its induced harmonious edge-labeling
i^{+}_{h}
is a proper edge coloring of
G
. Let
t=|i^{+}_{h}(E)|
,
f
is also called a
(k,s,t)
-IHT labeling of
G
. The IHT-
chromatic number
of
G
, denoted as
\chi^{+}(G)
, is the smallest number of
k
spanning over all
(k,s,t)
-IHT labelings of
G
.
\square

Remark 19
.

From Definition
113
and Definition
114
, an IDT-type labeling
f
of a
(p,q)
-graph
G
is
consecutive
if
f(V)=[0,p-1]
. Analogously, an IHT-type labeling
h
of
G
is
consecutive
if
h(V)=[0,p-1]
. Clearly, every graph admits a
(k,s,t)
-IDT
labeling and a
(k,s,t)
-IHT labeling. A
(k,s,t)
-IDT labeling of
G
is
strong
if it satisfies two additional requirements that
k=\chi^{-}(G)
and
t
is least, that is to say, for any
(k\,^{\prime},s\,^{\prime},t\,^{\prime})
-IDT labeling of
G
there is
t\leq t\,^{\prime}
. A consecutive
(k,s,t)
-IDT labeling of a graph
G
may be a
graceful labeling
or a
sequential labeling
of a
(p,q)
-graph
G
if
t=q
, but a
bandwidth labeling
of
G
, such a counterexample is a star. A
(k,s,t)
-IHT labeling of
G
may be a
harmonious labeling
or a
felicitous labeling
if
t=q
(
[
8
]
,
[
84
]
).
\parallel

Let
P_{4}=uvwx
denote a path on
4
vertices. A consecutive
(3,4,3)
-IDT labeling
f
of
P_{4}
can be represented as
{\Large\textcircled{\small 3}}3{\Large\textcircled{\small 0}}2{\Large\textcircled{\small 2}}1{\Large\textcircled{\small 1}}
, where
f(u)={\Large\textcircled{\small 3}}
,
i_{f}(uv)=3
,
f(v)={\Large\textcircled{\small 0}}
,
i_{f}(vw)=2
,
f(w)={\Large\textcircled{\small 2}}
,
i_{f}(wx)=1
,
f(x)={\Large\textcircled{\small 1}}
. Again,
P_{4}
admits a
(3,3,3)
-IDT labeling
{\Large\textcircled{\small 3}}3{\Large\textcircled{\small 0}}1{\Large\textcircled{\small 1}}2{\Large\textcircled{\small 3}}
and a consecutive
(3,3,2)
-IDT labeling
{\Large\textcircled{\small 2}}2{\Large\textcircled{\small 0}}1{\Large\textcircled{\small 1}}2{\Large\textcircled{\small 3}}
. We have that
\chi^{-}(P_{4})=2
since
P_{4}
admits a strong
(2,3,2)
-IDT labeling
{\Large\textcircled{\small 1}}1{\Large\textcircled{\small 0}}2{\Large\textcircled{\small 2}}1{\Large\textcircled{\small 1}}
.

2.15
Digraceful labelings

Definition 115
.

[
87
]
Let
f
be a labeling of a digraph
D
from its vertex set
V(D)
to
[0,|A(D)|]
, where
A(D)
is the arc set of
D
, such that for each arc
\overrightarrow{uv}\in A(D)
, the induced are color
f(\overrightarrow{uv})=f(u)-f(v)
if
f(u)>f(v)
, otherwise
f(\overrightarrow{uv})=|A(D)|+1+f(u)-f(v)
. We call
f
a
digraceful labeling
of
D
if the
arc color set

\{f(\overrightarrow{uv}):\overrightarrow{uv}\in A(D)\}=[1,|A(D)|]
, and
D
is called a
digraceful digraph
.
\square

Remark 20
.

[
87
]
(1) It is not hard to understand that a digraceful labeling
f
of a digraph
D
is equivalent to a certain graceful labeling
\theta
of
D
defied in
[
5
]
since
f(u)=\theta(u)
for
u\in V(D)
and
f(\overrightarrow{uv})=|A(D)|+1-\theta(\overrightarrow{uv})
for
\overrightarrow{uv}\in A(D)
, and moreover
f
is the
arc-dual labeling
of
\theta
(see Fig.
34
).

(2) Let
f
be a digraceful labeling of a digraph
D
. An arc
\overrightarrow{uv}
is called a
forward-arc
if its color
f(\overrightarrow{uv})=f(u)-f(v)
, otherwise, a
backward-arc
. The number of forward-arcs and the number of backward-arcs are denoted by
Fa_{D}(f)
and
Ba_{D}(f)
, respectively. We call number
\max_{f}\{Fa_{D}(f)\}
spanning over all digraceful labelings
f
of
D
the
optimal digraceful number
of
D
, denoted by
Odn(D)
. Furthermore, if
Fa_{D}(f)=Odn(D)=|A(D)|
, then
f
is a graceful labeling of the underlying graph
UG(D)
. Clearly, an arc
\overrightarrow{uv}
, an out-degree
d^{+}(u)
, an arc color
f(\overrightarrow{uv})=f(u)-f(v)
and a forward-arc are in the same direction, from the left to the right.

(3) The
converse

H
of a digraph
D
is the digraph obtained from
D
by conversing all arcs of
D
by reversing the arc
\overrightarrow{uv}
, it means that we replace the arc
\overrightarrow{uv}
by the arc
\overrightarrow{vu}
. Notice that
V(H)=V(D)
and
|A(H)|=|A(D)|
. The conversely digraceful labeling
h^{*}
of a digraceful labeling
f
of
D
is defined by
h^{*}(x)=|A(D)|-f(x)
for all
x\in V(H)=V(D)
.

(4) An undirected graph
G
has a
digraceful orientation
if there exists an orientation of
G
which admits a
digraceful labeling
. All orientations of the star
K_{1,5}
are digraceful, see Fig.
35
. Each vertex of degree one in an undirected tree is called a
leaf
. A caterpillar is an undirected tree
T
such that the resultant graph obtained by deleting all leaves of
T
is just a path. A
lobster

H
is an undirected tree such that the graph obtained by deleting all leaves from
H
is just a caterpillar. An in-zero-out ditree is a ditree if, for its every vertex
u
, one of out-degree
d^{+}(u)
and in-degree
d^{-}(u)
is always equal to zero. A
rooted ditree

T
at a fixed vertex
u
satisfies that in-degree
d^{-}(u)=0
, out-degree
d^{+}(u)\geq 1
, and in-degrees
d^{-}(u)=1
for all
x\in V(T)\setminus\{u\}
. A vertex
u
of a ditree
T
is called an in-leaf if
d^{+}_{T}(u)=0
and
d^{-}_{T}(u)=1
, and an out-leaf as if
d^{-}_{T}(u)=0
and
d^{+}_{T}(u)=1
.
\parallel

Figure 34:
(a) A digraceful orientation of
C_{4}
; (b) a digraceful orientation of
C_{6}
; (c) a digraceful orientation of
K_{4}
with a digraceful labeling
f
, and
Fa(f)=Ba(f)=3
; (d) a digraceful orientation of
K_{4}
with the dually digraceful labeling
f^{*}
of the digraceful labeling
f
defined in (c); (e) the conversely digraceful labeling
h^{*}
of the converse of an orientation of
K_{4}
shown in (c), cited from
[
87
]
.

Figure 35:
All digraceful orientations of
K_{1,5}
, cited from
[
87
]
.

3
Graph colorings with labeling-type restrictions

We combine traditional proper total colorings with some graph labelings to build up new colorings for topological coding science. In general, we have the following definition of splitting
\epsilon
-colorings:

Definition 116
.

(
[
51
,
69
]
) A connected
(p,q)
-graph
G
admits a coloring
f:S\rightarrow[a,b]
, where
S\subseteq V(G)\cup E(G)
, and there exists
f(u)=f(v)
for some distinct vertices
u,v\in V(G)
, and the edge color set
f(E(G))
holds an
\epsilon
-condition true, so we call
f
a
splitting
\epsilon
-coloring
of
G
.
\square

3.1
Total colorings with labeling-type of restrictions

Definition 117
.

[
71
]
Suppose that a connected
(p,q)
-graph
G
admits a coloring
f:V(G)\rightarrow[0,q]
(resp.
[0,2q-1]
), such that
f(u)=f(v)
for some pairs of vertices
u,v\in V(G)
, and the edge color set
f(E(G))=\{f(uv)=|f(u)-f(v)|:~uv\in E(G)\}=[1,~q]
(resp.
[1,2q-1]^{o}
), then we call
f
a
splitting gracefully total coloring
(resp.
splitting odd-gracefully total coloring
).
\square

3.1.1
A general definition of new colorings

A group of new colorings, in which some are very similar with that in
[
8
,
53
,
123
,
121
]
, is as follows:

Definition 118
.

[
49
]
Suppose that a connected
(p,q)
-graph
G
(
\neq K_{p}
) admits a total coloring
f:V(G)\cup E(G)\rightarrow[1,M]
, and there are
f(x)=f(y)
for some pairs of vertices
x,y\in V(G)
. Write
f(S)=\{f(w):w\in S\}
for a non-empty set
S\subseteq V(G)\cup E(G)
and let
k
be a fixed positive integer. There are the following constraint conditions:

(1
∘
)

|f(V(G))|<p
;

(2
∘
)

|f(E(G))|=q
;

(3
∘
)

f(V(G))\subseteq[1,M]
,
\min f(V(G))=1
;

(4
∘
)

f(V(G))\subset[1,2q+1]
,
\min f(V(G))=1
;

(5
∘
)

f(E(G))=[1,q]
;

(6
∘
)

f(E(G))=[0,q-1]
;

(7
∘
)

f(E(G))=[1,2q-1]^{o}
;

(8
∘
)

f(E(G))=[2,2q]^{e}
;

(9
∘
)

f(E(G))=[c,c+q-1]
;

(10
∘
)

f(uv)=|f(u)-f(v)|
for each edge
uv\in E(G)
;

(11
∘
)

f(uv)=f(u)+f(v)
for each edge
uv\in E(G)
;

(12
∘
)

For each edge
uv\in E(G)
,
f(uv)=f(u)+f(v)
when
f(u)+f(v)
is even, and
f(uv)=f(u)+f(v)+1
when
f(u)+f(v)
is odd;

(13
∘
)

f(uv)=f(u)+f(v)~(\textrm{mod}~q)
for each edge
uv\in E(G)
;

(14
∘
)

f(uv)=f(u)+f(v)~(\textrm{mod}~2q)
for each edge
uv\in E(G)
;

(15
∘
)

f(uv)+|f(u)-f(v)|=k
for each edge
uv\in E(G)
;

(16
∘
)

\big|f(uv)-|f(u)-f(v)|\big|=k
for each edge
uv\in E(G)
;

(17
∘
)

|f(u)+f(v)-f(uv)|=k
for each edge
uv\in E(G)
;

(18
∘
)

f(u)+f(uv)+f(v)=k
for each edge
uv\in E(G)
;

(19
∘
)

There exists an integer
k
so that
\min\{f(u),f(v)\}\leq k<\max\{f(u),f(v)\}
for each edge
uv\in E(G)
; and

(20
∘
)

(X,Y)
is the bipartition of a bipartite graph
G
such that
\max f(X)<\min f(Y)
.

-type
coloring

set-ordered ev-difference magic total coloring
if (
16
∘
) and (
20
∘
) hold true.

TCL-32.

A
graceful ev-difference magic total coloring
if (
5
∘
) and (
16
∘
) hold true.

TCL-33.

A
set-ordered graceful ev-difference magic total coloring
if (
5
∘
), (
16
∘
) and (
20
∘
) hold true.
\square

Remark 21
.

In Definition
118
:

(1) The number
\chi\,^{\prime\prime}_{W,M}(G)=\min_{f}\{M:~f(V(G))\subseteq[1,M]\}
over all
W
-type total colorings
f
of
G
for a fixed
W\in\{\textrm{TCL}-1,\textrm{TCL}-2,\dots,\textrm{TCL}-33\}
is called the
W
-type total chromatic number
of
G
, and the number
v_{W}(G)=\min_{f}\{|f(V(G))|\}
over all
W
-type colorings
f
of
G
as
W
-type total splitting number
. Clearly,
\chi\,^{\prime\prime}(G)\leq\chi\,^{\prime\prime}_{W,M}(G)
. Fig.
36
shows part of examples for knowing Definition
118
, and the colorings of the examples shown in Fig.
37
are equivalent from each other.

(2) Substituting “total coloring” by “proper total coloring” will get another group of
W
-type total colorings that are similar with and stricter than that defined in Definition
118
.

3.1.2
Matching difference total coloring

Definition 119
.

[
43
]
Let
f:V(G)\cup E(G)\rightarrow[1,k]
be a proper total coloring of a graph
G
. If each edge
uv
of
E(G)
holds
f(uv)=|f(u)-f(v)|
true, we call
f
a
ve-matching difference total
k
-coloring
of
G
, and call the smallest number of
k
over all ve-matching difference total
k
-colorings
ve-matching difference total chromatic number
, denoted as
\chi\,^{\prime\prime}_{ved}(G)
.
\square

Definition 120
.

[
43
]
For a proper total coloring
g:V(G)\cup E(G)\rightarrow[1,m]
on a graph
G
, if each edge
uv
of
E(G)
holds
g(uv)=g(u)+g(v)
true, we call
g
a
ve-matching sum total
m
-coloring
of
G
. The minimal number of
m
over all ve-matching sum total
m
-colorings is denoted as
\chi\,^{\prime\prime}_{ves}(G)
, called the
ve-matching sum total chromatic number
.
\square

See two ve-matching difference total colorings shown in Fig.
38
(a) and (b) for understanding Definition
119
. We can see
\chi\,^{\prime\prime}(G)\leq\chi\,^{\prime\prime}_{ved}(G)
, in general. Moreover, two ve-matching sum total colorings are shown in Fig.
38
(c) and (d), clearly,
\chi\,^{\prime\prime}(G)\leq\chi\,^{\prime\prime}_{ves}(G)
.

Figure 38:
(a) and (b) are two ve-matching difference total colorings, (c) and (d) are two ve-matching sum total colorings, cited from
[
43
]
.

Theorem 36
.

[
43
]
About
\chi\,^{\prime\prime}_{ved}(G)
defined in Definition
119
and
\chi\,^{\prime\prime}_{ves}(G)
defined in Definition
120
, we have:

Result-1.

Suppose that
H
is a connected subgraph of a connected graph
G
, then
\chi\,^{\prime\prime}_{ved}(H)\leq\chi\,^{\prime\prime}_{ved}(G)
and
\chi\,^{\prime\prime}_{ves}(H)\leq\chi\,^{\prime\prime}_{ves}(G)
.

Result-2.

Each tree
T
admits a ve-matching difference total coloring. When
\Delta(T)
is odd, then
\Delta(T)+2\leq\chi\,^{\prime\prime}_{ved}(T)\leq\frac{3}{2}(\Delta(T)+1)
; When
\Delta(T)
is even,
\Delta(T)+1\leq\chi\,^{\prime\prime}_{ved}(T)\leq\frac{3}{2}\Delta(T)+1
.

Result-3.

Each tree
T
admits a ve-matching sum total coloring and satisfies inequalities
\Delta(T)+2\leq\chi\,^{\prime\prime}_{ves}(T)\leq 2\Delta(T)+1
.

Result-4.

Every complete bipartite graph
K_{m,n}
with
n\geq m
admits a ve-matching sum total coloring and satisfies
\chi\,^{\prime\prime}_{ves}(K_{m,n})\leq m+2n
.

Result-5.

Every complete bipartite graph
K_{m,n}
with
n\geq m
admits a ve-matching difference total coloring and satisfies
\chi\,^{\prime\prime}_{ved}(K_{m,n})\leq 2n+\lceil\frac{n}{2}\rceil
.

Result-6.

Each complete graph
K_{n}
admits a ve-matching sum total coloring and holds
\chi\,^{\prime\prime}_{ves}(K_{n})\leq 2n-1
true.

3.2
Graceful extremum numbers and gracefully critical authentications

In
[
49
]
, each bipartite complete graph
K_{m,n}
does not admit a gracefully total coloring
g
with
g(x)=g(y)
for some two distinct vertices
x,y\in V(K_{m,n})
, meanwhile,
K_{m,n}
admits a graceful labeling
f
with
f(u)\neq f(w)
for any pair of vertices
u,w\in V(K_{m,n})
.

Definition 121
.

[
49
]
If a connected
(p,q)
-graph
H^{+}
admits a gracefully total coloring, and adding some new edge
e\not\in E(H^{+})
to
H^{+}
makes a new graph
H^{+}+e
such that the edge-added graph
H^{+}+e
does not admit a gracefully total coloring, we call
H^{+}
a
gracefully
+
critical graph
. If another connected
(s,t)
-graph
H^{-}
does not admit a gracefully total coloring, but removing some edge
e\,^{\prime}\in E(H^{-})
from
H^{-}
produces a new graph
H^{-}-e\,^{\prime}
admitting a gracefully total coloring, we refer to
H^{-}
a
gracefully
-
critical graph
.

A
(p,s)
-gracefully total number

R_{grace}(p,s)
is an extremal number, such that any red-blue edge-coloring of a complete graph
K_{m}
of
m

(\geq R_{grace}(p,s))
vertices induces a copy
H^{+}_{i}
of the gracefully
+
critical graph
H^{+}
of
p
vertices, or a copy
H^{-}_{j}
of the gracefully
-
critical graph
H^{-}
of
s
vertices, where each edge of
H^{+}_{i}
is red and each edge of
H^{-}_{j}
is blue. And, some red-blue edge-coloring of a complete graph
K_{n}
with
n\leq R_{grace}(p,s)-1
does not induce any one of copies of the gracefully
+
critical graph
H^{+}
of
p
vertices and copies of the gracefully
-
critical graph
H^{-}
of
s
vertices.
\square

Definition 122
.

[

]

for any

.

Remark 22
.

In fact, a
(p,s)
-gracefully total number

is a graph Ramsey. In his book
[

]
, West defined: Given simple graphs
G_{1},G_{2},\dots,G_{k}
, the (graph) Ramsey number
R(G_{1},G_{2}
,
\dots
,
G_{k})
is the smallest integer
n
such that every
k
-coloring of
E(K_{n})
contains a copy of
G_{i}
in color
i
for some
i
.
\parallel

Remark 23
.

Let

(as a gracefully

f(x_{i})=i

+

(n+2,n+2)
-gracefully total authentication
[

]
.

Definition 125
.

G

\square

There are many unsolved problems in graph theory, which can persuade people to believe that Topsnut-gpws can resist cipher’s attackers equipped quantum computer and AI technique, such a famous example is: “
If a graph
R
with the maximum
R(s,k)-1
vertices has no a complete graph
K_{s}
of
s
vertices and an independent set of
k
vertices, then we call
R
a
Ramsey graph
and
R(s,k)-1
a
Ramsey number
. As known, it is a terrible job for computer to find Ramsey number
R(5,5)
, although we have known
46\leq R(5,5)\leq 49
”. Joel Spencer said:“
Erdös asks us to imagine an alien force, vastly more powerful than us, landing on Earth and demanding the value of
R(5,5)
or they will destroy our planet. In that case, he claims, we should marshal all our computers and all our mathematicians and attempt to find the value. But suppose, instead, that they ask for
R(6,6)
. In that case, he believes, we should attempt to destroy the aliens
”.

3.3
Tcn-pure total colorings, Labeling-type restrictive total colorings

Coloring each of vertices and edges of a graph
G
with a number in
[1,k]
makes no two adjacent vertices (resp. edges) or incident edge (resp. vertex) having the same color, we call this coloring a
proper total coloring
, the minimum number of
k
for which
G
admits a proper total
k
-colorings is denoted as
\chi^{\prime\prime}(G)
.

Definition 126
.

[
54
]
Let
f:V(G)\cup E(G)\rightarrow[1,\chi^{\prime\prime}(G)]
be a proper total coloring of
G
with
\chi^{\prime\prime}(G)=|\{f(x):x\in V(G)\cup E(G)\}|
, and we call this coloring
f

total chromatic number pure coloring
(tcn-pure coloring). Let
d_{f}(uv)=f(u)+f(uv)+f(v)
for each edge
uv\in E(G)
, and set

B_{tol}(G,f)=\max_{uv\in E(G)}\{d_{f}(uv)\}-\min_{uv\in E(G)}\{d_{f}(uv)\}.

Determine a new parameter
\min_{f}B_{tol}(G,f)
over all tcn-pure colorings of
G
, and call
\min_{f}B_{tol}(G,f)

pan-bandwidth edge-magic total chromatic number
. Especially, a coloring
h
is called an
edge-magic tcn-pure coloring
if
B_{tol}(G,h)=0
, or an
equitably edge-magic tcn-pure coloring
if
B_{tol}(G,h)=1
.
\square

In Fig.
42
, three complete bipartite graphs
K_{2,3}
,
K_{2,4}
and
K_{3,3}
admit six tcn-pure total colorings: each
h_{i}
is the
dually total coloring
(also,
matching total coloring
) of
f_{i}
with
i\in[1,3]
. Moreover,
B_{tol}(K_{2,3},f_{1})=2
,
B_{tol}(K_{2,4},f_{2})=3
and
B_{tol}(K_{3,3},f_{3})=4
;
B_{tol}(K_{2,3},h_{1})=2
,
B_{tol}(K_{2,4},h_{2})=3
,
B_{tol}(K_{3,3},h_{3})=3
.

Figure 42:
Six tcn-pure colorings of three complete bipartite graphs, in which
h_{i}
is the matching total coloring of the tcn-pure coloring
f_{i}
with
i=1,2,3
, cited from
[
54
]
.

Theorem 38
.

[
118
]
Adding a new leaf
u
to a tree
T
admitting an edge-magic tcn-pure total coloring produces another tree
T+uv
with
v\in V(T)
holding
\min_{f}B_{tol}(T,f)=\min_{g}B_{tol}(T+uv,g)
true.

Theorem 39
.

[
118
]
Each tree admits an edge-magic tcn-pure coloring, or an equitably edge-magic tcn-pure coloring.

Theorem 40
.

[
118
]
Any connected graph
G
admitting an edge-magic tcn-pure coloring or an equitably edge-magic tcn-pure coloring is the result of doing vertex-coinciding operation to some tree
T
admitting an edge-magic tcn-pure coloring or an equitably edge-magic tcn-pure coloring, that is,
T
is
graph homomorphism
to
G
, that is,
T\rightarrow G
.

Definition 127
.

[
49
]
For a proper total coloring
f:V(G)\cup E(G)\rightarrow[1,M]
of a graph
G
, we define an
edge-function

c_{f}(uv)
for each edge
uv\in E(G)
, and then have a parameter

B^{*}_{\alpha}(G,f,M)=\max_{uv\in E(G)}\{c_{f}(uv)\}-\min_{xy\in E(G)}\{c_{f}(xy)\}.

(10)

If
B^{*}_{\alpha}(G,f,M)=0
, we call
f
an
\alpha
-proper total coloring
of
G
, the smallest number

\chi\,^{\prime\prime}_{\alpha}(G)=\min_{f}\{M:~B^{*}_{\alpha}(G,f,M)=0\}

(11)

over all
\alpha
-proper total colorings of
G
is called
\alpha
-proper total chromatic number
, and
f
is called a
perfect
\alpha
-proper total coloring
if
\chi\,^{\prime\prime}_{\alpha}(G)=\chi\,^{\prime\prime}(G)
.
Moreover

Tcoloring-1.

We call
f
a
(resp. perfect)
edge-magic proper total coloring
of
G
if
c_{f}(uv)=f(u)+f(v)+f(uv)
, rewrite
B^{*}_{\alpha}(G,f,M)=B^{*}_{emt}(G,f
,
M)
, and
\chi\,^{\prime\prime}_{\alpha}(G)=\chi\,^{\prime\prime}_{emt}(G)
is called
edge-magic total chromatic number
of
G
.

Tcoloring-2.

We call
f
a
(resp. perfect)
edge-difference proper total coloring
of
G
if
c_{f}(uv)=f(uv)+|f(u)-f(v)|
, rewrite
B^{*}_{\alpha}(G,f,M)=B^{*}_{edt}(G,f
,
M)
, and
\chi\,^{\prime\prime}_{\alpha}(G)=\chi\,^{\prime\prime}_{edt}(G)
is called
edge-difference total chromatic number
of
G
.

Tcoloring-3.

We call
f
a
(resp. perfect)
felicitous-difference proper total coloring
of
G
if
c_{f}(uv)=|f(u)+f(v)-f(uv)|
, rewrite
B^{*}_{\alpha}(G,f,M)=B^{*}_{fdt}(G,f,M)
, and
\chi\,^{\prime\prime}_{\alpha}(G)=\chi\,^{\prime\prime}_{fdt}(G)
is is called
felicitous-difference total chromatic number
of
G
.

Tcoloring-4.

We refer to
f
a
(resp. perfect)
graceful-difference proper total coloring
of
G
if
c_{f}(uv)=\big||f(u)-f(v)|-f(uv)\big|
, rewrite
B^{*}_{\alpha}(G,f,M)=B^{*}_{gdt}(G,f,M)
, and
\chi\,^{\prime\prime}_{\alpha}(G)=\chi\,^{\prime\prime}_{gdt}(G)
is called
graceful-difference total chromatic number
of
G
.
\square

As removing “proper” from Definition
127
, we get

Definition 128
.

[
64
]
A randomly growing network model
N(t)
admits a
W
-type total coloring
f_{t}:V(N(t))\cup E(N(t))\rightarrow[1,M(t)]
with
t\in[a,b]^{r}
such that there is a fixed constant
k\geq 0
, and a constraint conditional function
F(f_{t}(x)
,
f_{t}(xy)
,
f_{t}(y))=k
for each edge
xy\in E(N(t))
is one of the following equations:

(E-1)

f_{t}(x)+f_{t}(xy)+f_{t}(y)=k>0
;

(E-2)

\big||f_{t}(x)-f_{t}(y)|-f_{t}(xy)\big|=k\geq 0
;

(E-3)

f_{t}(xy)+|f_{t}(x)-f_{t}(y)|=k>0
; and

(E-4)

\big|f_{t}(x)+f_{t}(y)-f_{t}(xy)\big|=k\geq 0
.

We call
f_{t}
:

C-1.

An
edge-magic total coloring
if (E-
1
) holds true.

C-2.

A
graceful-difference total coloring
if (E-
2
) holds true.

C-3.

An
edge-difference total coloring
if (E-
3
) holds true.

C-4.

A
felicitous-difference total coloring
if (E-
4
) holds true.
\square

We can add another constraint requirement, such that each edge color
f_{t}(xy)
is odd in Definition
128
, so we get new colorings:
odd-edge-magic total coloring
,
odd-graceful-difference total coloring
,
odd-edge-difference total coloring
,
odd-felicitous-difference total coloring
, respectively. Next, we add three parameters to Definition
127
if
G
is bipartite, and get another group of particular total colorings as follows:

Definition 129
.

[
49
]
Suppose that a bipartite graph
G
admits a proper total coloring
f:V(G)\cup E(G)\rightarrow[1,M]
. We define an
edge-function

c_{f}(uv)(a,b,c)
with three non-negative integers
a,b,c
for each edge
uv\in E(G)
, and define a parameter

B^{*}_{\alpha}(G,f,M)(a,b,c)=\max_{uv\in E(G)}\{c_{f}(uv)(a,b,c)\}-\min_{xy\in E(G)}\{c_{f}(xy)(a,b,c)\}.

(12)

If
B^{*}_{\alpha}(G,f,M)(a,b,c)=0
, we call
f
a
parameterized
\alpha
-proper total coloring
of
G
, the smallest number

\chi\,^{\prime\prime}_{\alpha}(G)(a,b,c)=\min_{f}\{M:~B^{*}_{\alpha}(G,f,M)(a,b,c)=0\}

(13)

over all parameterized
\alpha
-proper total colorings of
G
is called
parameterized
\alpha
-proper total chromatic number
, and
f
is called a
perfect
\alpha
-proper total coloring
if
\chi\,^{\prime\prime}_{\alpha}(G)(a,b,c)=\chi\,^{\prime\prime}(G)
.

TCol-1.

We call
f
a
(resp. perfect)
parameterized edge-magic proper total coloring
of
G
if
c_{f}(uv)=af(u)+bf(v)+cf(uv)
, rewrite
B^{*}_{\alpha}(G,f,M)(a,b,c)=B^{*}_{emt}(G,f
,
M)(a,b,c)
, and
\chi\,^{\prime\prime}_{\alpha}(G)(a,b,c)=\chi\,^{\prime\prime}_{emt}(G)(a,b,c)
is called
parameterized edge-magic total chromatic number
of
G
.

TCol-2.

We call
f
a
(resp. perfect)
parameterized edge-difference proper total coloring
of
G
if
c_{f}(uv)=cf(uv)+|af(u)-bf(v)|
, rewrite
B^{*}_{\alpha}(G,f,M)(a,b,c)=B^{*}_{edt}(G,f
,
M)(a,b,c)
, and
\chi\,^{\prime\prime}_{\alpha}(G)(a,b,c)=\chi\,^{\prime\prime}_{edt}(G)(a,b,c)
is called
parameterized edge-difference total chromatic number
of
G
.

TCol-3.

We call
f
a
(resp. perfect)
parameterized felicitous-difference proper total coloring
of
G
if
c_{f}(uv)=|af(u)+bf(v)-cf(uv)|
, rewrite
B^{*}_{\alpha}(G,f,M)(a,b,c)=B^{*}_{fdt}(G,f,M)(a,b,c)
, and
\chi\,^{\prime\prime}_{\alpha}(G)(a,b,c)=\chi\,^{\prime\prime}_{fdt}(G)(a,b,c)
is called
parameterized felicitous-difference total chromatic number
of
G
.

TCol-4.

We refer to
f
as a
(resp. perfect)
parameterized graceful-difference proper total coloring
of
G
if
c_{f}(uv)=\big||af(u)-bf(v)|-cf(uv)\big|
, rewrite

B^{*}_{\alpha}(G,f,M)(a,b,c)=B^{*}_{gdt}(G,f,M)(a,b,c),

and
\chi\,^{\prime\prime}_{\alpha}(G)(a,b,c)=\chi\,^{\prime\prime}_{gdt}(G)(a,b,c)
is called
parameterized graceful-difference total chromatic number
of

.
\square

Remark 24
.

We can put forward various requirements for
(a,b,c)
in Definition
129
to increase the difficulty of attacking topological coding, since the ABC-conjecture (also, Oesterlé-Masser conjecture, 1985) involves the equation
a+b=c
and the relationship between prime numbers. Proving or disproving the ABC-conjecture could impact: Diophantine (polynomial) math problems including Tijdeman’s theorem, Vojta’s conjecture, Erdös-Woods conjecture, Fermat’s last theorem, Wieferich prime and Roth’s theorem, and so on
[
15
]
.
\parallel

Definition 130
.

[
49
]
The
dual colorings
of the colorings defined in Definition
129
as
(a,b,c)=(1,1,1)
are in the following:

Dual
-1.

For an
edge-magic proper total coloring

f_{em}
of a graph
G
, so there exists a constant
k
such that
f_{em}(u)+f_{em}(uv)+f_{em}(v)=k
for each edge
uv\in E(G)
. Let
\max f_{em}=\max\{f_{em}(w):w\in V(G)\cup E(G)\}
and
\min f_{em}=\min\{f_{em}(w):w\in V(G)\cup E(G)\}
. We have the
dual coloring

g_{em}
of
f_{em}
defined as:
g_{em}(w)=(\max f_{em}+\min f_{em})-f_{em}(w)
for
w\in V(G)\cup E(G)
, and then

{\begin{split}g_{em}(u)+g_{em}(uv)+g_{em}(v)&=3(\max f_{em}+\min f_{em})-[f_{em}(u)+f_{em}(uv)+f_{em}(v)]\\
&=3(\max f_{em}+\min f_{em})-k=k\,^{\prime}\end{split}}

(14)

for each edge
uv\in E(G)
.

Dual
-2.

For an
edge-difference proper total coloring

f_{ed}
of a graph
G
, we have a constant
k
holding
f_{ed}(uv)+|f_{ed}(u)-f_{ed}(v)|=k
for each edge
uv\in E(G)
. Let
\max f_{ed}=\max\{f_{ed}(w):w\in V(G)\cup E(G)\}
and
\min f_{ed}=\min\{f_{ed}(w):w\in V(G)\cup E(G)\}
. We have the
dual coloring

g_{ed}
of
f_{ed}
defined by setting
g_{ed}(x)=(\max f_{ed}+\min f_{ed})-f_{ed}(x)
for
x\in V(G)
and
g_{ed}(uv)=f_{ed}(uv)
for
uv\in E(G)
, and then

g_{ed}(uv)+|g_{ed}(u)-g_{ed}(v)|=f_{ed}(uv)+|f_{ed}(u)-f_{ed}(v)|=k

(15)

for every edge
uv\in E(G)
.

Dual
-3.

For a
graceful-difference proper total coloring

f_{gd}
of a graph
G
, there exists a constant
k
such that
\big||f_{gd}(u)-f_{gd}(v)|-f_{gd}(uv)\big|=k
for each edge
uv\in E(G)
. Let
\max f_{gd}=\max\{f_{gd}(w):w\in V(G)\cup E(G)\}
and
\min f_{gd}=\min\{f_{gd}(w):w\in V(G)\cup E(G)\}
. We have the
dual coloring

g_{gd}
of
f_{gd}
defined in the way:
g_{gd}(x)=(\max f_{gd}+\min f_{gd})-f_{gd}(x)
for
x\in V(G)
and
g_{gd}(uv)=f_{gd}(uv)
for each edge
uv\in E(G)
, and then

{\begin{split}\big||g_{gd}(u)-g_{gd}(v)|-g_{gd}(uv)\big|=\big||f_{gd}(u)-f_{gd}(v)|-f_{gd}(uv)\big|=k\end{split}}

(16)

for each edge
uv\in E(G)
.

Dual
-4.

For a
felicitous-difference proper total coloring

f_{fd}
of a graph
G
, we have a constant
k
such that
|f_{fd}(u)+f_{fd}(v)-f_{fd}(uv)|=k
for each edge
uv\in E(G)
. Let
\max f_{fd}=\max\{f_{fd}(w):w\in V(G)\cup E(G)\}
and
\min f_{fd}=\min\{f_{fd}(w):w\in V(G)\cup E(G)\}
. We have the
dual coloring

g_{fd}
of
f_{fd}
defined as:
g_{fd}(w)=(\max f_{fd}+\min f_{fd})-f_{fd}(w)
for
w\in V(G)\cup E(G)
, and then

{\begin{split}|g_{fd}(u)+g_{fd}(v)-g_{fd}(uv)|&=|(\max f_{fd}+\min f_{fd})+f_{fd}(u)+f_{fd}(v)-f_{fd}(uv)|\\
&=(\max f_{fd}+\min f_{fd})\pm k\end{split}}

(17)

for each edge
uv\in E(G)
. Here, if
f_{fd}
is edge-ordered, that is,
f_{fd}(x)+f_{fd}(y)\geq f_{fd}(xy)
for each edge
xy\in E(G)
, then

|g_{fd}(u)+g_{fd}(v)-g_{fd}(uv)|=(\max f_{fd}+\min f_{fd})+k=k\,^{\prime}.

We have

|g_{fd}(u)+g_{fd}(v)-g_{fd}(uv)|=(\max f_{fd}+\min f_{fd})-k=k\,^{\prime},

if
f_{fd}(x)+f_{fd}(y)<f_{fd}(xy)
for each edge
xy\in E(G)
.
\square

A randomly growing graph admits an edge-difference proper total coloring holding

, cited from
[
49
]
.

Remark 25
.

Removing “proper” from Definition
127
produces: A simple graph
G
admits a total coloring
f:V(G)\cup E(G)\rightarrow[1,M]
such that
f(u)\neq f(v)
for each edge
uv\in E(G)
, and
f(xy)\neq f(xw)
for two adjacent edges
xy,xw\in E(G)
. So, this particular total coloring allows
f(u)=f(uv)
for some edge
uv\in E(G)
, and is weak than that in Definition
127
. Similarly, removing “proper” from Definition
129
produces four parameterized
\alpha
-proper total colorings that are weak than that defined in Definition
129
.
\parallel

Definition 131
.

[
49
]
A
rainbow proper total coloring

f
of a connected graph
G
holds: For any path
x_{1}x_{2}x_{3}x_{4}x_{5}
of
G
, edge colors
f(x_{i}x_{i+1})\neq f(x_{j}x_{j+1})
with
i,j\in[1,4]
, and each
f(x_{j}x_{j+1})
is one of
f(x_{j}x_{j+1})=f(x_{i})+f(x_{i}x_{i+1})+f(x_{i+1})
,
f(x_{j}x_{j+1})=f(x_{i}x_{i+1})+|f(x_{i})-f(x_{i+1})|
,
f(x_{j}x_{j+1})=|f(x_{i})+f(x_{i+1})-f(x_{i}x_{i+1})|
and
f(x_{j}x_{j+1})=\big||f(x_{i})-f(x_{i+1})|-f(x_{i}x_{i+1})\big|
.
\square

Definition 132
.

[
49
]
Let
f:V(G)\cup E(G)\rightarrow[1,M]
be a proper total coloring of a simple graph
G
. If
E(G)=\bigcup^{4}_{i=1}E_{i}
with
E_{i}\cap E_{j}=\emptyset
and
E_{i}\neq\emptyset
for
1\leq i,j\leq 4
, such that
f(u)+f(uv)+f(v)=k_{1}
for each edge
uv\in E_{1}
,
f(xy)+|f(x)-f(y)|=k_{2}
for each edge
xy\in E_{2}
,
|f(s)+f(t)-f(st)|=k_{3}
for each edge
st\in E_{3}
, and
\big||f(a)-f(b)|-f(ab)\big|=k_{4}
for each edge
ab\in E_{4}
. We call

4-ice-flower proper total coloring
of

, and the smallest number of
\max\{f(w):w\in V(G)\cup E(G)\}
for which
G
admits a 4-ice-flower proper total coloring is denoted as
\chi\,^{\prime\prime}_{\textrm{4ice}}(G)
, called
4-ice-flower total chromatic number
.
\square

Definition 133
.

[
49
]
Let
G
be a totally colored graph admitting a
W_{G}
-type proper total coloring
f_{G}
, and let
H
be another totally colored graph with a
W_{H}
-type proper total coloring
g_{H}
. We say
G=H
if there is a bijection
\varphi:V(G)\rightarrow V(H)
such that

(i)
G\wedge u\cong H\wedge\varphi(u)
for each vertex
u\in V(G)
with
\textrm{deg}_{G}(u)\geq 2
(refer to Theorem
3
); and

(ii) for each element
w\in V(G)\cup E(G)
, there exists an element
w\,^{\prime}\in V(H)\cup E(H)
holding
g_{H}(w\,^{\prime})=f_{G}(w)
when
w\,^{\prime}=\varphi(w)
.
\square

Definition 134
.

[
49
]
Suppose that a connected graph
G
admits a coloring
f
. If there is a spanning subgraph
T
of
G
, such that
f
is just a
W
-type coloring of
T
, we call

an
inner
W
-type coloring
of

, and say
G
admits a
coloring including a
W
-type coloring
. Moreover, if there are
L

H_{i},H_{2},\dots,H_{L}
with
E(H_{i})\neq E(H_{j})
for distinct
i,j\in[1,L]
and
L\geq 2
such that
f_{i}~(=f)
is an
inner
W_{i}
-type coloring
of
H_{i}
with
i\in[1,L]
, we call
f
a
coloring including
L
-multiple colorings
of
G
, and furthermore

is called an
(W_{i})^{L}_{1}
-type coloring
of

if
E(G)=\bigcup^{L}_{i=1}E(H_{i})
.
\square

Definition 135
.

[
49
]
If a proper total coloring
f:V(G)\cup E(G)\rightarrow[1,M]
for a bipartite
(p,q)
-graph
G
holds:

(i) (e-magic)
f(uv)+|f(u)-f(v)|=k
;

(ii) (ee-difference) each edge
uv
matches with another edge
xy
holding
f(uv)=|f(x)-f(y)|
(or
f(uv)=2(p+q)-|f(x)-f(y)|
);

(iii) (ee-balanced) let
s(uv)=|f(u)-f(v)|-f(uv)
for
uv\in E(G)
, then there exists a constant
k\,^{\prime}
such that each edge
uv
matches with another edge
u\,^{\prime}v\,^{\prime}
holding
s(uv)+s(u\,^{\prime}v\,^{\prime})=k\,^{\prime}
(or
2(p+q)+s(uv)+s(u\,^{\prime}v\,^{\prime})=k\,^{\prime}
) true;

(iv) (set-ordered)
\max f(X)<\min f(Y)
(or
\min f(X)>\max f(Y)
) for the bipartition
(X,Y)
of
V(G)
.

(v) (edge-fulfilled)
f(E(T))=[1,q]
.

We call
f
a
5
C-total coloring
of
G
.
\square

3.4
New parameters on proper vertex colorings

Definition 136
.

[
54
]
Let
f:V(G)\rightarrow[1,k]
be a proper vertex coloring of a graph
G
with
k=\chi(G)
. We define a parameter

B_{sub}(G,f)=\sum_{xy\in E(G)}|f(x)-f(y)|,

(18)

and try to determine
\min_{f}B_{sub}(G,f)
and
\max_{f}B_{sub}(G,f)
. Clearly, if
G
is a bipartite graph, then

\min_{f}B_{sub}(G,f)=\max_{f}B_{sub}(G,f)=|E(G)|.

If there exists a proper vertex coloring
f_{M}
of a connected graph
G
such that
B_{sub}(G,f_{M})=M
for each
M
holding

\min_{f}B_{sub}(G,f)<M<\max_{f}B_{sub}(G,f),

(19)

we say that
G
admits
a group of consecutive difference proper vertex colorings
(see an example shown in Fig.
45
).
\square

Figure 45:
A graph
C_{5}+uv
admits a group of
consecutive difference proper vertex colorings
, which forms a Topsnut-matching chain, cited from
[
54
]
.

Definition 137
.

[
54
]
Let
g:V(G)\rightarrow[1,k]
be a proper vertex coloring of a graph
G
with
k=\chi(G)
. We define another parameter

B_{sum}(G,g)=\sum_{xy\in E(G)}[g(x)+g(y)]

(20)

and try to compute two extremal values
\min_{g}B_{sum}(G,g)
and
\max_{g}B_{sum}(G,g)
. If
G
admits a proper vertex coloring
g_{Q}
for each
Q
satisfying

\min_{g}B_{sum}(G,g)<Q<\max_{h}B_{sum}(G,h)

(21)

such that
B_{sum}(G,g_{Q})=Q
, we say that
G
admits
a group of consecutive sum proper vertex colorings
.
\square

3.5
Weak gracefully total colorings

Definition 138
.

[
49
]
If a connected
(p,q)
-graph
G
admits a total coloring
f:V(G)\cup E(G)\rightarrow[1,M]
, such that
f(uv)=|f(u)-f(v)|
and
f(u)\neq f(v)
for each edge
uv\in E(G)
, and
f(E(G))=[1,q]
, as well as
f(V(G))\subseteq[1,q+1]
, we call
f
a
weak-gracefully total coloring
. It may happen
f(u)=2f(v)
or
f(v)=2f(u)
in a weak gracefully total coloring. Moreover, if
G
is bipartite, and
\max f(X)<\min f(Y)
for the bipartition
(X,Y)
of vertex set of
G
, we call
f
a
set-ordered weak gracefully total coloring.

\square

Definition 139
.

[
49
,
71
]
A connected
(p,q)
-graph
G
admits a total coloring
f:V(G)\cup E(G)\rightarrow[1,M]
, and
f(x)=f(y)
for some pair of vertices
x,y\in V(G)
. There are the following constraint conditions:

(i)

each edge
uv\in E(G)
holds
f(uv)=|f(u)-f(v)|
true;

(ii)

edge color set
f(E(G))=[1,q]
;

(iii)

vertex color set
f(V(G))\subseteq[1,q+1]
;

(iv)

edge color set
f(E(G))=[1,2q-1]^{o}
;

(v)

vertex color set
f(V(G))\subseteq[1,2q]
; and

(vi)

G
is bipartite and its own vertex set
V(G)=X\cup Y
with
X\cap Y=\emptyset
, such that each edge
uv\in E(G)
holds
u\in X
and
v\in Y
, the total coloring
f
holds
\max f(X)<\min f(Y)
true.

We have:

Weak-1.

If (
i
) and (
ii
) hold true, we call
f
a
prospective weak gracefully total coloring
.

Weak-2.

If (
i
), (
ii
) and (
iii
) hold true, so we call
f
a
weak gracefully total coloring
.

Weak-3.

If (
i
), (
ii
) and (
vi
) hold true, then we call
f
a
prospective set-ordered weak gracefully total coloring
.

Weak-4.

If (
i
), (
ii
), (
iii
) and (
vi
) hold true, then we call
f
a
set-ordered weak gracefully total coloring
.

Weak-5.

If (
i
) and (
iv
) hold true, then we call
f
a
prospective weak odd-gracefully total coloring
.

Weak-6.

If (
i
), (
iv
) and (
v
) hold true, so we call
f
a
weak odd-gracefully total coloring
.

Weak-7.

If (
i
), (
iv
) and (
vi
) hold true, we call
f
a
prospective set-ordered weak odd-gracefully total coloring
.

Weak-8.

If (
i
), (
iv
), (
v
) and (
vi
) hold true, then we call
f
a
set-ordered weak odd-gracefully total coloring
.
\square

Remark 26
.

(1) Each total coloring defined in Definition
139
is a mixed thing of graceful labeling and a total coloring.

(2) Definition
139
induces a parameter: A connected
(p,q)
-graph
G
admits a weak graceful (resp. odd-graceful) total coloring
h
, if
|h(V(G))|\leq|f(V(G))|
holds true for any
weak graceful (resp. odd-graceful) total coloring

f
of
G
, we write
v_{gtc}(G)=|h(V(T))|=\min_{f}\{|f(V(T))|\}
over all weak graceful (resp. odd-graceful) total colorings of
G
, called a
weak graceful (resp. odd-graceful) total chromatic number
. It is not easy to compute the exact value of
v_{gtc}(G)
, since it is related with Total Coloring Conjecture.

(3) If a total coloring
f
defined in Definition
139
is
proper
, so we have
f(v)\neq f(uv)=|f(u)-f(v)|
and
2f(v)\neq f(u)
, or
f(u)\neq f(uv)=|f(u)-f(v)|
and
2f(u)\neq f(v)
. In general, a total coloring
f
defined in Definition
139
and its dual total coloring may be not
proper
. If a total coloring defined in Definition
139
is
proper
, so we call it a graceful (resp. odd-graceful) proper total coloring, or a weak graceful (resp. odd-graceful) proper total coloring, or a set-ordered weak graceful (resp. odd-graceful) total coloring, or a prospective weak graceful (resp. odd-graceful) proper total coloring, or a prospective set-ordered weak graceful (resp. odd-graceful) proper total coloring.
\parallel

3.6
Parameterized total colorings

Let integers
a,k,m\geq 0
,
d\geq 1
and
q\geq 1
in this subsection. We have two parameterized sets
S_{m,k,a,d}=\{k+ad,k+(a+1)d,\dots,k+(a+m)d\}
and
O_{2q-1,k,d}=\{k+d,k+3d,\dots,k+(2q-1)d\}
. The
cardinality
of a set
S
is denoted as
|S|
, so
|S_{m,k,a,d}|=m+1
and
|O_{2q-1,k,d}|=q
. Motivated from the parameterized labelings: the
(k,d)
-arithmetic labeling, the
(k,d)
-graceful labeling, the
(k,d)
-harmonious labeling and the new
(k,d)
-type labelings introduced in
[
39
]
. Based on the well-defined parameterized labelings, we present the following parameterized total colorings:

Definition 140
.

[
63
]
Let
G
be a bipartite and connected
(p,q)
-graph, so its vertex set
V(G)=X\cup Y
with
X\cap Y=\emptyset
such that each edge
uv\in E(G)
holds
u\in X
and
v\in Y
. If there is a coloring
f:X\rightarrow S_{m,0,0,d}=\{0,d,\dots,md\}
and
f:Y\cup E(G)\rightarrow S_{n,k,0,d}=\{k,k+d,\dots,k+nd\}
, here it is allowed
f(x)=f(y)
for some distinct vertices
x,y\in V(G)
. Let
c
be a non-negative integer.
We have
:

Ptol-1.

If
f(uv)=|f(u)-f(v)|
for
uv\in E(G)
,
f(E(G))=S_{q-1,k,0,d}
and
f(V(G)\cup E(G))\subseteq S_{m,0,0,d}\cup S_{q-1,k,0,d}
, then
f
is called a
(k,d)
-gracefully total coloring
; and moreover
f
is called a
(k,d)
-strongly gracefully total coloring
if
f(x)+f(y)=k+(q-1)d
for each matching edge
xy
of a matching
M
of
G
.

Ptol-2.

If
f(uv)=|f(u)-f(v)|
for
uv\in E(G)
,
f(E(G))=O_{2q-1,k,d}
and
f(V(G)\cup E(G))\subseteq S_{m,0,0,d}\cup S_{2q-1,k,0,d}
, then
f
is called a
(k,d)
-odd-gracefully total coloring
; and moreover
f
is called a
(k,d)
-strongly odd-gracefully total coloring
if
f(x)+f(y)=k+(2q-1)d
for each matching edge
xy
of a matching
M
of
G
.

Ptol-3.

If the color set

\{f(u)+f(uv)+f(v):uv\in E(G)\}=\{2k+2ad,2k+2(a+1)d,\dots,2k+2(a+q-1)d\}

with
a\geq 0
and
f(V(G)\cup E(G))\subseteq S_{m,0,0,d}\cup S_{2(a+q-1),k,a,d}
, then
f
is called a
(k,d)
-edge antimagic total coloring
.

Ptol-4.

If
f(uv)=f(u)+f(v)~(\bmod^{*}qd)
defined by
f(uv)-k=[f(u)+f(v)-k](\bmod~qd)
for
uv\in E(G)
, and
f(E(G))=S_{q-1,k,0,d}
, then
f
is called a
(k,d)
-harmonious total coloring
.

Ptol-5.

If
f(uv)=f(u)+f(v)~(\bmod^{*}qd)
defined by
f(uv)-k=[f(u)+f(v)-k](\bmod~qd)
for
uv\in E(G)
, and
f(E(G))=O_{2q-1,k,d}
, then
f
is called a
(k,d)
-odd-elegant total coloring
.

Ptol-6.

If
f(u)+f(uv)+f(v)=c
for each edge
uv\in E(G)
,
f(E(G))=S_{q-1,k,0,d}
and
f(V(G)\cup E(G))\subseteq S_{m,0,0,d}\cup S_{q-1,k,0,d}
, then
f
is called a
strongly
(k,d)
-edge-magic total coloring
; and moreover
f
is called an
edge-magic
(k,d)
-total coloring
if
|f(E(G))|\leq q
and
f(u)+f(uv)+f(v)=c
for
uv\in E(G)
.

Ptol-7.

If
f(uv)+|f(u)-f(v)|=c
for each edge
uv\in E(G)
and
f(E(G))=S_{q-1,k,0,d}
, then
f
is called a
strongly edge-difference
(k,d)
-total coloring
; and moreover
f
is called an
edge-difference
(k,d)
-total coloring
if
|f(E(G))|\leq q
and
f(uv)+|f(u)-f(v)|=c
for
uv\in E(G)
.

Ptol-8.

If
|f(u)+f(v)-f(uv)|=c
for each edge
uv\in E(G)
and
f(E(G))=S_{q-1,k,0,d}
, then
f
is called a
strongly felicitous-difference
(k,d)
-total coloring
; and moreover
f
is called a
felicitous-difference
(k,d)
-total coloring
if
|f(E(G))|\leq q
and
|f(u)+f(v)-f(uv)|=c
for
uv\in E(G)
.

Ptol-9.

If
\big||f(u)-f(v)|-f(uv)\big|=c
for each edge
uv\in E(G)
and
f(E(G))=S_{q-1,k,0,d}
, then
f
is called a
strongly graceful-difference
(k,d)
-total coloring
; and
f
is called a
graceful-difference
(k,d)
-total coloring
if
|f(E(G))|\leq q
and
\big||f(u)-f(v)|-f(uv)\big|=c
for
uv\in E(G)
.
\square

Definition 141
.

∗
A total coloring
f
defined in Definition
140
is
proper
if
f(u)\neq f(v)
for each edge
uv\in E(G)
, and
f(uv)\neq f(uw)
for any two adjacent edges
uv,uw\in E(G)
, so we call
f
a
W
-type proper
(k,d)
-total coloring
of
G
.
\square

Remark 27
.

In general, we call
f
a
W
-type
(k,d)
-total coloring
if the constraint function
F(f(u)
,
f(uv)
,
f(v))=0
and the edge color set
f(E(G))
hold one of the well-defined total colorings defined in Definition
140
.

(1)

We have some new parameters of graphs based on Definition
140
. For a graph
G
, we have two
W
-type
(k,d)
-total colorings
g_{\min}
and
g_{\max}
of
G
such that

|g_{\min}(V(G))|\leq|g(V(G))|\leq|g_{\max}(V(G))|

for each
W
-type
(k,d)
-total coloring
g
of
G
. As this
W
-type
(k,d)
-total coloring is the graceful
(k,d)
-total coloring, then a graceful
(1,1)
-total coloring
g_{\max}
of a tree
T
means that
|g_{\max}(V(T))|
is the approximate value of a graceful labeling of
T
. The graceful tree conjecture says:
|g_{\max}(V(T))|=|V(T)|
.

(2)

In application, we may reduce the restrictions of some
W
-type
(k,d)
-total colorings of graphs, such as a
W
-type
(k,d)
-total coloring
g
of a bipartite graph
G
with bipartition
(X,Y)
holds
g:X\rightarrow\{-md,-(m-1)d,\dots,-2d,-d\}\cup\{0,d,\dots,md\}
and
g:Y\cup E(G)\rightarrow\{-k,-k-d,\dots,-k-nd\}\cup\{-k+d,-k+2d,\dots,-k+nd\}\cup\{k-d,k-2d,\dots,k-nd\}\cup\{k,k+d,\dots,k+nd\}
with
m,n,k,d>0
.
\parallel

3.7
Multiple dimension total colorings

We put three number-based strings
0112304
,
4500123
and
0023111
into one number-based string

011230445001230023111,\textrm{or }002311101123044500123,\textrm{or }450012301123040023111

and we call them
ordered permutations
, and each one of
0112304
,
4500123
and
0023111
is called a
coordinate
. In general, a
n
-dimension number-based string is defined by an ordered permutation
a_{1}a_{2}\cdots a_{n}
and each coordinate
a_{i}=\beta_{i,1}\beta_{i,2}\cdots\beta_{i,b_{i}}
with
\beta_{i,j}\in[0,9]
for
j\in[1,b_{i}]
, and moreover the number
||a_{i}||=\sum^{b_{i}}_{r=1}\beta_{i,r}
is called the
norm
of the coordinate
a_{i}
. If
\theta_{i,r}=0
for
r\in[1,s]
and
\theta_{i,s+1}\geq 1
with
s+1\leq b_{i}
in some coordinate
a_{i}=\theta_{i,1}\theta_{i,2}\cdots\theta_{i,b_{i}}
of a
n
-dimension number-based string
a_{1}a_{2}\cdots a_{n}
, as a substitution, we use
\theta_{i,s+1}\theta_{i,s+2}\cdots\theta_{i,b_{i}}\in Z^{0}
to participate in addition operation, subtraction operation, multiplication operation and and other mathematical operations. Let
S(n,Z^{0})
be the set of all
n
-dimension number-based strings. We present new colorings of graphs, which are like parameterized colorings in
[
57
]
.

Definition 142
.

[
57
]
A
(p,q)
-graph
G
admits a coloring
f:S\subseteq V(G)\cup E(G)\rightarrow S(n,Z^{0})
. Let
f(u)=a_{1}a_{2}\cdots a_{n}
,
f(v)=b_{1}b_{2}\cdots b_{n}
,
f(uv)=c_{1}c_{2}\cdots c_{n}
for each edge
uv\in E(G)
, and
\gamma
be a non-negative integer. There are the following constraint conditions:

Res-1.

S=V(G)
;

Res-2.

S=E(G)
;

Res-3.

S=V(G)\cup E(G)
;

Res-4.

f(u)\neq f(v)
for each edge
uv\in E(G)
;

Res-5.

f(uv)\neq f(uw)
for any pair of two adjacent edges
uv,uw\in E(G)
;

Res-6.

f(u)\neq f(uv)
and
f(v)\neq f(uv)
for each edge
uv\in E(G)
;

Res-7.

c_{j}=|a_{j}-b_{j}|
with
j\in[1,n]
;

Res-8.

c_{j}=|a_{j}-b_{j}|
, and each
c_{j}
is odd with
j\in[1,n]
;

Res-9.

c_{j}=a_{j}+b_{j}~(\bmod~q)
with
j\in[1,n]
;

Res-10.

a_{j}+b_{j}+c_{j}=\gamma
with
j\in[1,n]
;

Res-11.

c_{j}+|a_{j}-b_{j}|=\gamma
with
j\in[1,n]
;

Res-12.

\big|c_{j}-|a_{j}-b_{j}|\big|=\gamma
with
j\in[1,n]
;

Res-13.

|a_{j}+b_{j}-c_{j}|=\gamma
with
j\in[1,n]
;

Res-14.

c_{j}=\textrm{gcd}(a_{j},b_{j})
for each
j\in[1,n]
;

Res-15.

Some
j\in[1,n]
holds
c_{j}=\textrm{gcd}(a_{j},b_{j})
;

Res-16.

c_{i}\neq c_{j}
for any pair of
c_{i}
and
c_{j}
;

Res-17.

f(E(G))=\{f(e_{k})=c_{k,1}c_{k,2}\cdots c_{k,n}:k\in[1
,
q]\}
such that each
k\in[1,q]
holds
c_{k,j}=k
for some
c_{k,j}
of
f(e_{k})
;

Res-18.

f(E(G))=\{f(e_{j})=t_{j,1}t_{j,2}\cdots t_{j,n}:j\in[1
,
2q-1]^{o}\}
such that each
j\in[1,2q-1]^{o}
holds
t_{j,r}=j
for some
t_{j,r}
of
f(e_{j})
;

Res-19.

f(E(G))=\{f(e_{k})=c_{k,1}c_{k,2}\cdots c_{k,n}:k\in[1
,
q]\}
such that
c_{k,r}=k\in[1,q]
for each
r\in[1,n]
; and

Res-20.

f(E(G))=\{f(e_{j})=t_{j,1}t_{j,2}\cdots t_{j,n}:j\in[1
,
2q-1]^{o}\}
such that
t_{j,r}=j\in[1,2q-1]^{o}
for each
r\in[1,n]
.

We call
f
:

——
traditional types

Dtc-1.

A
n
-dimension proper vertex coloring
if Res-

and Res-
4
hold true.

Remark 28
.

We, also, call

defined in Definition

a

-type

.

[

]
Every connected simple graph admits a

.

.

[

.

.

[

]
Every tree admits a

-dimension sub-proper total coloring
.

We introduce the following compounded-type multiple dimension total colorings:

Definition 143
.

∗
By Definition
140
, let
G
be a bipartite and connected
(p,q)
-graph, so its vertex set
V(G)=X\cup Y
with
X\cap Y=\emptyset
such that each edge
uv\in E(G)
holds
u\in X
and
v\in Y
. There are a group of colorings
f_{s}:X\rightarrow S_{m,0,0,d}=\{0,d,\dots,md\}
and
f_{s}:Y\cup E(G)\rightarrow S_{n,k,0,d}=\{k,k+d,\dots,k+nd\}
(here it is allowed
f_{s}(u)=f_{s}(w)
for some distinct vertices
u,w\in V(G)
) for

with integer

, such that
f_{s}\in\{

(k_{s},d_{s})
-strongly gracefully total coloring,
(k_{s},d_{s})
-strongly odd-gracefully total coloring,
(k_{s},d_{s})
-edge antimagic total coloring,
(k_{s},d_{s})
-harmonious total coloring,
(k_{s},d_{s})
-odd-elegant total coloring, strongly
(k_{s},d_{s})
-edge-magic total coloring, edge-magic
(k_{s},d_{s})
-total coloring, strongly edge-difference
(k_{s},d_{s})
-total coloring, edge-difference
(k_{s},d_{s})
-total coloring, strongly felicitous-difference
(k_{s},d_{s})
-total coloring, felicitous-difference
(k_{s},d_{s})
-total coloring, strongly graceful-difference
(k_{s},d_{s})
-total coloring, graceful-difference
(k_{s},d_{s})
-total coloring
\}
with
s\in[1,B]
. Then
G
admits a
parameterized compounded
B
-dimension total coloring

F
holding
F(u)=f_{1}(u)f_{2}(u)\cdots f_{B}(u)
,
F(uv)=f_{1}(uv)f_{2}(uv)\cdots f_{B}(uv)
and
F(v)=f_{1}(v)f_{2}(v)\cdots f_{B}(v)
for each edge
uv\in E(G)
.
\square

Definition 144
.

∗
A
(p,q)
-graph
G
admits a coloring
g_{j}:S\subseteq V(G)\cup E(G)\rightarrow S(n,Z^{0})
for
i\in[1,B]
, and
g_{j}
is one of
Dtc-
s
total colorings
with
j\in[1,27]
defined in Definition
142
. Then
G
admits a
compounded
B
-dimension total coloring

F
holding
F(u)=g_{1}(u)g_{2}(u)\cdots g_{B}(u)
,
F(uv)=g_{1}(uv)g_{2}(uv)\cdots g_{B}(uv)
and
F(v)=g_{1}(v)g_{2}(v)\cdots g_{B}(v)
for each edge
uv\in E(G)
.
\square

Remark 29
.

In Definition
143
, each sequence
\{(k_{s},d_{s})\}^{B}_{1}
will induce random number-based strings generated from the Topcode-matrix of
G
admitting a parameterized compounded
B
-dimension total coloring. Similarly, a compounded
B
-dimension total coloring
F
of
G
defined in Definition
144
is more complex than any one total coloring defined in Definition
142
.

3.8
Colorings based on graphic groups and string groups

Using every-zero graphic groups and edge-every-zero graphic groups to encrypting networks are similar with graph colorings.

Definition 145
.

[
52
]
Let
H
be a
(p,q)
-graph, and
\{F_{f}(G),\oplus\}
be an every-zero graphic group containing Topsnut-gpws
G_{i}
with
i\in[1,M_{G}]
. Suppose that
H
admits a mapping
\theta:S\rightarrow F_{f}(G)
with
S\subseteq V(H)\cup E(H)
. Write
\theta(S)=\{\theta(w):w\in S\}
,
\theta(N_{ei}(u))=\{\theta(v):v\in N_{ei}(u)\}
and
\theta(N\,^{\prime}_{ei}(u))=\{\theta(uv):v\in N_{ei}(u)\}
. There are the following so-called
graphic group constraints
:

Gg-1.

S=V(H)
;

Gg-2.

S=E(H)
;

Gg-3.

S=V(H)\cup E(H)
;

Gg-4.

\theta(u)\neq\theta(v)
for
v\in N_{ei}(u)
;

Gg-5.

\theta(uv)\neq\theta(uw)
for
v,w\in N_{ei}(u)
;

Gg-6.

\theta(N_{ei}(x))\neq\theta(N_{ei}(y))
for any pair of vertices
x,y\in V(H)
;

Gg-7.

\theta(N_{ei}(u))\neq\theta(N_{ei}(v))
for each edge
uv\in E(H)
;

Gg-8.

\theta(N\,^{\prime}_{ei}(x))\neq\theta(N\,^{\prime}_{ei}(y))
for any pair of vertices
x,y\in V(H)
;

Gg-9.

\theta(N\,^{\prime}_{ei}(u))\neq\theta(N\,^{\prime}_{ei}(v))
for each edge
uv\in E(H)
;

Gg-10.

\big||\theta(N_{ei}(u))|-|\theta(N_{ei}(v))|\big|\leq 1
for each edge
uv\in E(H)
;

Gg-11.

\big||\theta(N\,^{\prime}_{ei}(u))|-|\theta(N\,^{\prime}_{ei}(v))|\big|\leq 1
for each edge
uv\in E(H)
;

Gg-12.

\{\theta(u)\}\cup\theta(N_{ei}(u))\cup\theta(N\,^{\prime}_{ei}(u))\neq\{\theta(v)\}\cup\theta(N_{ei}(v))\cup\theta(N\,^{\prime}_{ei}(v))
for each edge
uv\in E(H)
;

Gg-13.

There exists an index
k_{uv}
such that
\theta(uv)=\theta(u)\oplus_{k_{uv}}\theta(v)\in F_{f}(G)
for each edge
uv\in E(H)
; and

Gg-14.

Each vertex
w
is assigned with a set
\{\theta(ww_{i})\oplus_{k_{ij}}\theta(ww_{j}):w_{i},w_{j}\in N_{ei}(w)\}
.

Then we call
\theta
:

1-ggc.

A
proper gg-coloring
if Gg-
1
and Gg-
4
hold true.

2-ggc.

A
proper edge-gg-coloring
if Gg-
2
and Gg-
5
hold true.

3-ggc.

A
proper total gg-coloring
if Gg-
3
, Gg-
4
and Gg-
5
hold true.

4-ggc.

A
vertex distinguishing proper gg-coloring
if Gg-
1
, Gg-
4
and Gg-
6
hold true.

5-ggc.

An
adjacent vertex distinguishing proper gg-coloring
if Gg-

, Gg-

and Gg-

hold true.

6-ggc.

An
edge distinguishing proper gg-coloring
if Gg-

, Gg-

and Gg-

hold true.

7-ggc.

An
adjacent edge distinguishing proper gg-coloring
if Gg-

, Gg-

and Gg-

hold true.

8-ggc.

An
equitable adjacent-v proper gg-coloring
if Gg-
1
, Gg-

and Gg-

hold true.

9-ggc.

An

if Gg-

, Gg-

hold true.

10-ggc.

An

if Gg-

, Gg-

A

\square

Remark 30
.

Based on Definition
145
, we have new parameters:

New-1.

\chi_{gg}(H)
is the minimum number of
M_{G}
Topsnut-gpws
G_{i}
in some every-zero graphic group
\{F_{f}(G),\oplus\}

admits a

(Ref.
[

[

3.9
Directed total colorings with labeling restrictions

Definition 146
.

[
49
]
Let
\overrightarrow{G}
be a directed connected graph with
p
vertices and
Q
arcs. If
G
admits a proper total coloring
f:V(\overrightarrow{G})\cup A(\overrightarrow{G})\rightarrow[1,M]
such that
f(\overrightarrow{uv})=f(u)-f(v)
for each arc
\overrightarrow{uv}\in A(\overrightarrow{G})
and
\{|f(\overrightarrow{uv})|:\overrightarrow{uv}\in A(\overrightarrow{G})\}=[1,Q]
, then we call
f
a
directed gracefully total coloring
of
\overrightarrow{G}
, and moreover
f
is a
proper directed gracefully total coloring
if
M=Q+1
. (see an example shown in Fig.
48
(b))
\square

Figure 48:
(a) A half-directed caterpillar
T^{*}
with its topological vector
V_{ec}(T^{*})=(-5,0,4,3,-1,0,-6)
; (b) a proper directed gracefully total coloring of another directed caterpillar, cited from
[
49
]
.

Definition 147
.

∗
A digraph
D
admits a coloring
\theta:V(D)\rightarrow[1,|A(D)|+1]
(resp.
[1,2|A(D)|]
), where
A(D)
is the arc set of
D
, such that each arc
\overrightarrow{uv}\in A(D)
is colored as
\theta(\overrightarrow{uv})=\theta(u)-\theta(v)
if
\theta(u)>\theta(v)
, and
\theta(\overrightarrow{uv})=|A(D)|+1+\theta(u)-\theta(v)
(resp.
\theta(\overrightarrow{uv})=2|A(D)|+\theta(u)-\theta(v)
) if
\theta(u)<\theta(v)
. We call
\theta
a
digraceful labeling
(resp. an
odd-digraceful labeling
) of
D
if the
arc color set

\theta(A(D))=\{\theta(\overrightarrow{uv}):\overrightarrow{uv}\in A(D)\}=[1,|A(D)|]
(resp.
[1,2|A(D)|-1]^{o}
). Therefore,
D
is called a
digraceful digraph
(resp. an
odd-digraceful digraph
) (see Fig.
49
and Fig.
50
).
\square

Figure 49:
(a) and (b) are two digraceful colorings; (c) and (d) are two odd-digraceful colorings.

Remark 31
.

Suppose that a
(p,q)
-digraph
D
admits a digraceful labeling (resp. an odd-digraceful labeling)
f
, then the
dual digraceful labeling

h
of the digraceful labeling
f
is defined as:

h(x)=\max f(V(D))+\min f(V(D))-f(x)
for each vertex
x\in V(D)
, and

h(\overrightarrow{uv})=\max f(A(D))+\min f(A(D))-f(\overrightarrow{uv})
for each arc
\overrightarrow{uv}\in A(D)
.

where
f(V(D))=\{f(x):x\in V(D)\}
and
f(A(D))=\{f(\overrightarrow{uv}):\overrightarrow{uv}\in A(D)\}
(see examples shown in Fig.
50
and Fig.
51
).
\parallel

Figure 50:
Four digraceful colorings of a ditree.

Figure 51:
The dual digraceful colorings of four digraceful colorings shown in Fig.
50
.

4
Sequence labelings, sequence colorings

4.1
Sequence labelings

Definition 148
.

[
74
]
Let
G
be a
(p,q)
-graph, and let
A_{M}=\{a_{1},a_{2},\dots,a_{M}\}=\{a_{i}\}_{1}^{M}
and
B_{q}=\{b_{1},b_{2},\dots,b_{q}\}=\{b_{j}\}_{1}^{q}
be two integer sequences with
0\leq a_{i}<a_{i+1}
,
0\leq b_{j}<b_{j+1}
and
m\geq p
, and
k
be a non-negative integer. There are the following labelings and constraint conditions:

Seq-1.

A vertex labeling
f:V(G)\rightarrow A_{M}
such that
f(u)\neq f(v)
for any pair of distinct vertices
u,v\in V(G)
;

Seq-2.

an edge labeling
h:E(G)\rightarrow B_{q}
such that
h(uv)\neq h(uw)
for distinct edges
uv,uw\in E(G)
;

Seq-3.

a total labeling
g:V(G)\cup E(G)\rightarrow A_{M}\cup B_{q}
such that
g(x)\neq g(y)
for any pair of adjacent or incident elements
x,y\in V(G)\cup E(G)
;

Seq-4.

f(V(G))\subseteq A_{M}
and induced edge color set
f(E(G))=B_{q}
;

Seq-5.

f(V(G))=A_{M}
;

Seq-6.

f(V(G))=A_{M}
and induced edge color set
f(E(G))=B_{q}
;

Seq-7.

g(V(G)\cup E(G))\subseteq A_{M}\cup B_{q}
;

Seq-8.

g(V(G))\subseteq A_{M}
and
g(E(G))=B_{q}
;

Seq-9.

g(V(G)\cup E(G))=A_{M}\cup B_{q}
;

Seq-10.

h(E(G))=B_{q}
;

Seq-11.

a
matching equation

\alpha(x)+\alpha(y)=s(p)
for each matching edge
xy
of a perfect matching
M
of
G
, where
s(p)
is a function of variable
p
, and
\alpha
is one of the above labelings
f
and
g
;

Seq-12.

G
is a bipartite graph with vertex bipartition
(X,Y)
, such that
\max\beta(X)<\min\beta(Y)
, where
\beta
is one of the above labelings
f
and
g
;

Seq-13.

an
induced function

f(uv)=O(f(u),f(v))
for each edge
uv\in E(G)
;

Seq-14.

an
F
-equation

F(g(u),g(uv),g(v))=k
for each edge
uv\in E(G)
; and

Seq-15.

an
M
-equation

M(f(u),h(uv),f(v))=0
for each edge
uv\in E(G)
.

We call
f
:

(1)

An
induced sequence labeling
if Seq-
1
and Seq-
13
hold true.

(2)

A
set-ordered induced sequence labeling
if Seq-
1
, Seq-
13
and Seq-
12
hold true.

(3)

A
strongly induced sequence labeling
if Seq-
1
, Seq-
13
and Seq-
11
hold true.

(4)

An
edge sequence-graceful labeling
if Seq-
2
and Seq-
10
hold true.

(5)

A
generalized edge-magic sequence labeling
if Seq-
3
, Seq-
7
and Seq-
14
hold true, where the
F
-equation is
g(u)+g(uv)+g(v)=k
.

(6)

An
edge-magic total sequence labeling
if Seq-
3
, Seq-
9
and Seq-
14
hold true, where the
F
-equation is
g(u)+g(uv)+g(v)=k
.

(7)

An
F
-magic sequence-graceful labeling
if Seq-
3
, Seq-
8
and Seq-
14
hold true.

(8)

A
sequence-graceful labeling
if Seq-
1
, Seq-
6
and Seq-
13
hold true, where the induced function is
f(uv)=|f(u)-f(v)|
.

(9)

A
set-ordered sequence-graceful labeling
if Seq-
1
, Seq-
6
, Seq-
13
and Seq-
12
hold true, where the induced function is
f(uv)=|f(u)-f(v)|
.

(10)

A
strongly sequence-graceful labeling
if Seq-
1
, Seq-
6
, Seq-
13
and Seq-
11
hold true, where the induced function is
f(uv)=|f(u)-f(v)|
.

(11)

A
pan-sequence-graceful labeling
if Seq-
1
, Seq-
4
and Seq-
13
hold true, where the induced function is
f(uv)=|f(u)-f(v)|
.

(12)

A
set-ordered pan-sequence-graceful labeling
if Seq-
1
, Seq-
4
, Seq-
13
and Seq-
12
hold true, where the induced function is
f(uv)=|f(u)-f(v)|
.

(13)

A
strongly pan-sequence-graceful labeling
if Seq-
1
, Seq-
4
, Seq-
13
and Seq-
11
hold true, where the induced function is
f(uv)=|f(u)-f(v)|
.

(14)

An
M
-magic sequence-graceful labeling
if Seq-
1
, Seq-
2
, Seq-
4
, Seq-
5
, Seq-
10
and Seq-
15
hold true.
\square

Remark 32
.

As two sequences to be consecutive sets, namely,
A_{m}=[a_{1},a_{m}]
and
B_{q}=[b_{1},b_{q}]
, the sequence-type of labellins in Definition
148
are popular labelings. In fact, Definition
148
is a pan-definition, there are more labeling-type of constraint conditions as follows:

Cot
-1.

The induced function
f(uv)=O(f(u),f(v))
for each edge
uv\in E(G)
including
f(uv)=|f(u)-f(v)|
,
f(uv)=f(u)+f(v)~(\bmod~\epsilon(q))
,
f(uv)=f(u)+f(v)+r
with
r\geq 0
,
f(uv)=np-[f(u)+f(v)]
,
f(uv)=\left\lceil\frac{f(u)+f(v)}{2}\right\rceil
,
f(uv)=\textrm{gcd}(f(u),f(v))
; odd or even
f(uv)=|f(u)-f(v)|
, odd or even
f(uv)=f(u)+f(v)~(\bmod~\epsilon(q))
, odd or even
f(uv)=f(u)+f(v)+r
with
r\geq 0
.

Other functions are as: (1) Linear functions:
f(xy)=|2f(y)-1-2f(x)|
for each edge
xy\in E(G)
if
V(G)=X\cup Y
and
x\in X
and
y\in Y
for a bipartite graph
G
, or
f(xy)=|af(y)+b-[cf(x)+d]|
subject to integers
a,c\geq 1
and
b,d\geq 0
.

(2) Non-linear functions:
f(xy)=\big\lceil|\sin f(y)-\cos f(x)|\big\rceil
for each edge
xy\in E(G)
,
etc.

Cot
-2.

The
F
-equation
F(g(u),g(uv),g(v))=k
for each edge
uv\in E(G)
including

(i) Popular-type: edge-magic
g(u)+g(uv)+g(v)=k
, edge-difference
g(uv)+|g(u)-g(v)|=k
, graceful-difference
\big|g(uv)-|g(u)-g(v)|\big|=k
, felicitous-difference
|g(u)+g(v)-g(uv)|=k
.

(ii) Generalized-type: parameterized edge-magic
ag(u)+cg(uv)+bg(v)=k
, parameterized edge-difference
cg(uv)+|bg(u)-bg(v)|=k
, parameterized graceful-difference
|cg(uv)-|ag(u)-bg(v)|\big|=k
, parameterized felicitous-difference
|ag(u)+bg(v)-cg(uv)|=k
.

Cot
-3.

An
M
-equation
M(f(u),h(uv),f(v))=0
for each edge
uv\in E(G)
is under a composition of a vertex labeling
f
and an edge labeling
h
, in general, but it differs from a total labeling. Very often,
M
-equations may be composed by constraint conditions of vertex labelings and constraint conditions of edge labelings.

Cot
-4.

Topological structures are as constraint conditions, such as
path
,
cycle
,
clique
,
star tree
K_{1,s}
,
face
,
bipartite graph
,
extremum graph
, or other
particular subgraphs
. For example,
face labelings
of planar graphs,
subgraph labelings
of graphs.

In Definition
148
, the vertex labeling
f
is belong to the topological constraint condition of
K_{1,1}
, both labelings
h
and
g
are restricted by the topological constraint condition of star tree
K_{1,d(u)}
, where
d(u)
is the degree of vertex
u\in V(G)
.
\parallel

Definition 149
.

∗
Let
S
be a set of colors.

(1) Let
H
be a maximal planar graph admitting a vertex labeling
f:V(H)\rightarrow S
, the
induced face-color function
is
f(\Delta_{xyz})=f(x)+f(y)+f(z)
for each triangle face
\Delta_{xyz}
with vertices
x,y,z
in
H
, and we call
f
a
W
-type face labeling
if the face-color set
\{f(\Delta_{xyz}):~\Delta_{xyz}\subset H\}
holds a
W
-condition true.

(2) A
(p,q)
-graph
G
admits a vertex labeling
g:V(G)\rightarrow S
, the
induced cycle-color function
is
g(C)=\sum^{n}_{i=1}g(x_{i})
for each cycle
C=x_{1}x_{2}\cdots x_{n}x_{1}
of
G
, and we call
g
a
\{W_{i}\}^{m}_{1}
-cycle labeling
if the cycle-color set
\{g(C):~C=x_{1}x_{2}\cdots x_{n}x_{1}\subset G\}
holds a a group of
W_{i}
-conditions with
i\in[1,m]
true.

(3) A connected graph
G
admits a total labeling
h:V(G)\cup E(G)\rightarrow S
, the spanning tree labeling function is
h(T)=F(h(V(T)),h(E(T)))
for each spanning tree
T
of
G
, we call
h
a
\{W_{i}\}^{m}_{1}
-spanning tree labeling
if the spanning tree-color set
\{h(T):T~\textrm{is a spanning tree of}~G\}
over all spanning trees of
G
holds a group of
W_{i}
-conditions with
i\in[1,m]
true.
\square

Remark 33
.

Replacing “labeling” by “coloring” in Definition
149
will result:
W
-type face coloring
,
\{W_{i}\}^{m}_{1}
-cycle coloring
and
\{W_{i}\}^{m}_{1}
-spanning tree coloring
.
\parallel

Definition 150
.

[
75
]
A
sequence
(\{a_{i}\}^{M}_{1},\{b_{j}\}^{q}_{1})
-harmonious labeling
of a
(p,q)
-graph
G
, where
\{a_{i}\}^{M}_{1}
and
\{b_{j}\}^{q}_{1}
are two monotonic decreasing sequences of non-negative numbers with
M\geq p
, is defined by
f:V(G)\rightarrow\{a_{i}\}^{M}_{1}
such that
f(u)\neq f(v)
for distinct
u,v\in V(G)
, and the induced edge color is
f(uv)=f(u)+f(v)~(\bmod~\epsilon(q))
such that
f(E(G))=\{b_{j}\}^{q}_{1}
, where
\epsilon(q)
is a function of variable
q
.
\square

4.2
Sequence colorings

Some
sequence-type labelings
of graphs were introduced in
[
74
]
, so we present sequence-type of graph colorings as follows.

Definition 151
.

[
63
]
Let
G
be a
(p,q)
-graph, and let a sequence
A_{M}=\{a_{i}\}_{1}^{M}
hold
0\leq a_{i}<a_{i+1}
for
i\in[1,M-1]
and
p\leq M
, and let another sequence
B_{q}=\{b_{j}\}_{1}^{q}
hold
0\leq b_{j}<b_{j+1}
for
j\in[1,q-1]
, and let
k
be a constant.
G
admits a coloring
f:S\rightarrow C
with
f(S)=\{f(x):x\in S\}
and
S\subseteq V(G)\cup E(G)
. There are the following constraint conditions:

Rec-1.

S=V(G)
and
C=A_{M}
;

Rec-2.

S=V(G)\cup E(G)
and
C=A_{M}\cup B_{q}
;

Rec-3.

f(u)\neq f(v)
for any edge
uv\in E(G)
;

Rec-4.

f(u)\neq f(uv)
and
f(v)\neq f(uv)
for each edge
uv\in E(G)
;

Rec-5.

f(uv)\neq f(uw)
for any two vertices
v,w\in N(u)
;

Rec-6.

f(E(G))\subseteq B_{q}
;

Rec-7.

f(V(G))\subseteq A_{M}
and
p=|V(G)|
;

Rec-8.

f(E(G))=B_{q}
;

Rec-9.

an induced function
f(uv)=O(f(u),f(v))
for each edge
uv\in E(G)
;

Rec-10.

an
F
-equation
F(f(u),f(uv),f(v))=k
for each edge
uv\in E(G)
; and

Rec-11.

G
is a bipartite graph with vertex bipartition
(X,Y)
such that
\min f(X)<\max f(Y)
.

We refer to
f
as
:

——
traditional-type

Coloring-1.

An
edge-induced sequence coloring
of
G
if Rec-
1
and Rec-
9
hold true.

gracefully edge-induced sequence coloring
of

and Rec-

hold true.

hold true.

hold true.

f(uv)=O(f(u),f(v))=|f(u)-f(v)|
.

We say a sentence “a

-type sequence coloring
” to represent one of colorings with no “set-ordered” defined in Definition

, and employ another sentence “a

W
-type sequence coloring
” to stand for one of colorings having “set-ordered” defined in Definition
151
.

Lemma 44
.

[
63
]
Let
G
be a bipartite and connected
(p,q)
-graph with its vertex bipartition
V(G)=X\cup Y
such that
X\cap Y=\emptyset
,
X=\{x_{i}:i\in[1,s]\}
and
Y=\{y_{j}:j\in[1,t]\}
holding
s+t=p
. Suppose that
G
admits a
(k,d)
-gracefully total coloring, then any leaf-added graph
G+L_{\textrm{eaf}}
admits a
(k,d)
-gracefully total coloring
too.

Theorem 45
.

[
63
]
Each tree admits a
(k,d)
-gracefully total coloring
defined in Definition
140
, also, a
set-ordered gracefully total coloring
as
(k,d)=(1,1)
.

Theorem 46
.

[
63
]
Every tree
T
with diameter
D(T)\geq 3
and
s+1=\left\lceil\frac{D(T)}{2}\right\rceil
admits at least
2^{s}
different
gracefully total sequence colorings
if two sequences
A_{M},B_{q}
holding
0<b_{j}-a_{i}\in B_{q}
for
a_{i}\in A_{M}
and
b_{j}\in B_{q}
.

Lemma 47
.

[
63
]
Suppose that a bipartite and connected graph
G
admits a gracefully total sequence coloring based on two sequences
A_{M},B_{q}
holding
0<b_{j}-a_{i}\in B_{q}
for
a_{i}\in A_{M}
and
b_{j}\in B_{q}
, then a new bipartite and connected graph obtained by adding randomly leaves to
G
admits a
gracefully total sequence coloring
based on two sequences
A\,^{\prime}_{M},B\,^{\prime}_{q}
holding
0<b\,^{\prime}_{j}-a\,^{\prime}_{i}\in B\,^{\prime}_{q}
for
a\,^{\prime}_{i}\in A\,^{\prime}_{M}
and
b\,^{\prime}_{j}\in B\,^{\prime}_{q}
.

The
(k,d)
-graceful preparation
. Let
G
be a bipartite and connected
(p,q)
-graph with its vertex set
V(G)=X\cup Y
such that
X\cap Y=\emptyset
,
X=\{x_{i}:i\in[1,s]\}
and
Y=\{y_{j}:j\in[1,t]\}
holding
s+t=p
, and each edge is
x_{i}y_{j}
with
x_{i}\in X
and
y_{j}\in Y
. Suppose that
G
admits a
(k,d)
-gracefully total coloring
f
, so we have
f:X\rightarrow S_{m,0,d}
and
f:Y\cup E(G)\rightarrow S_{q-1,k,d}
with
k\geq 1
, without loss of generality, there are
0=f(x_{1})\leq f(x_{i})\leq f(x_{i+1})
for
i\in[1,s-1]
and
f(x_{s})\leq f(y_{j})\leq f(y_{j+1})\leq f(y_{t})=k+(q-1)d
for
j\in[1,t-1]
, as well as
f(E(G))=S_{q-1,k,d}
.

LEAF-adding algorithm

[
63
]
By The
(k,d)
-graceful preparation we have

Step 1.
Color each vertex
x_{i}\in X\subset X\,^{\prime}
with
g(x_{i})=f(x_{i})
for
i\in[1,s]
, and color each vertex
y_{j}\in Y\subset Y^{\prime}
with
g(y_{j})=f(y_{j})+[A(s)+B(t)]d
for
j\in[1,t]
, and color edges
x_{i}y_{j}\in E(G)\subset E(G+L_{\textrm{eaf}})
by

{\begin{split}g(x_{i}y_{j})=g(y_{j})-g(x_{i})=f(y_{j})+[A(s)+B(t)]d-f(x_{i})=f(x_{i}y_{j})+[A(s)+B(t)]d.\end{split}}

So, we get an edge color set

{\begin{split}g(E(G))=\{k+[A(s)+B(t)]d,k+[A(s)+B(t)+1]d,\dots,k+[A(s)+B(t)+q-1]d\}.\end{split}}

Step 2.
Let edges
e_{i_{1}}e_{i_{2}}\dots e_{i_{A(s)+B(t)}}
be a permutation of edges
x_{i}x_{i,r}
for
r\in[1,a_{i}]
and
i\in[1,s]
and edges
y_{j}y_{j,r}
for
r\in[1,b_{j}]
and
j\in[1,t]
, that is,
e_{i_{1}}e_{i_{2}}\dots e_{i_{A(s)+B(t)}}
is a permutation of
x_{1}x_{1,1}

x_{1}x_{1,2}

\cdots

x_{1}x_{1,a_{1}}

x_{2}x_{2,1}

x_{2}x_{2,2}

\cdots

x_{2}x_{2,a_{2}}

\cdots

x_{i}x_{i,1}

x_{i}x_{i,2}

\cdots

x_{i}x_{i,a_{i}}

\cdots

x_{s}x_{s,1}

x_{s}x_{s,2}

\cdots

x_{s}x_{s,a_{s}}

y_{1}y_{1,1}

y_{1}y_{1,2}

\cdots

y_{1}y_{1,b_{1}}

y_{2}y_{2,1}

y_{2}y_{2,2}

\cdots

y_{2}y_{2,b_{2}}

\cdots

y_{j}y_{j,1}

y_{j}y_{j,2}

\cdots

y_{j}y_{j,b_{j}}

\cdots

y_{t}y_{t,1}

y_{t}y_{t,2}

\cdots

y_{t}y_{t,b_{t}}
. We color each edge
e_{i_{r}}
with
g(e_{i_{r}})=k+(r-1)d
with
r\in[1,A(s)+B(t)]
, and color each vertex
x_{i,r}
with
g(x_{i,r})=f(x_{i})+g(e_{i_{r}})
if vertex
x_{i,r}
is an end of the edge
e_{i_{r}}
, and color each vertex
y_{j,r}
with
g(y_{j,r})=g(y_{j})-g(e_{i_{r}})
if vertex
y_{j,r}
is an end of the edge
e_{i_{r}}
.

See Fig.
52
and Fig.
53
for understanding the
(k,d)
-graceful preparation and the LEAF-adding algorithm, and by them and Lemma
47
we get:

Theorem 48
.

[
63
]
Each tree on
q
edges admits a proper graceful total sequence coloring based on two sequences
A_{M}
and
B_{q}
holding
0<b_{j}-a_{i}\in B_{q}
for
a_{i}\in A_{M}
and
b_{j}\in B_{q}
.

Figure 52:
A process for understanding the LEAF-adding algorithm, cited from
[
63
]
.

Figure 53:
For understanding the LEAF-adding algorithm, cited from
[
63
]
.

Remark 34
.

We can make number-based strings with long bytes by the help of
Fibonacci-Lucas sequence-sequences

\{F[w_{i},z_{i}]_{n}\}^{N}_{i=1}
, where each
F[w_{i},z_{i}]_{n}=\{w_{i}
,
z_{i}
,
c_{i,3}
,
c_{i,4}
,
\dots,c_{i,n-2}\}
is a
Fibonacci-Lucas sequence
with integers
w_{i}\geq 1
and
z_{i}\geq 1
,
c_{i,3}=w_{i}+z_{i}
,
c_{i,4}=c_{i,3}+z_{i}
, and
c_{i,s+1}=c_{i,s}+c_{i,s-1}
for
s\in[4,n-2]
and
i\in[1,N]
. Particularly, a
Fibonacci sequence
is
F[1,1]_{n}
, and a
Lucas sequence
is
F[1,3]_{n}
. Moreover, as
w_{i}-w_{j}\geq 1
and
z_{i}-z_{j}\geq 1
, we have

F[w_{i},z_{i}]_{n}+F[w_{j},z_{j}]_{n}=F[w_{i}+w_{j},z_{i}+z_{j}]_{n},~F[w_{i},z_{i}]_{n}-F[w_{j},z_{j}]_{n}=F[w_{i}-w_{j},z_{i}-z_{j}]_{n}

Thereby, we get
(n\cdot N)!
permutations
FL_{j}=m_{j_{1}}

m_{j_{2}}

\cdots

m_{j_{nN}}
of
w_{1}

z_{1}

c_{1,3}

c_{1,4}

\cdots c_{1,n-2}

w_{2}

z_{2}

c_{2,3}

c_{2,4}

\cdots c_{2,n-2}

\cdots

w_{i}

z_{i}

c_{i,3}

c_{i,4}

\cdots c_{i,n-2}

\cdots

w_{N}

z_{N}

c_{N,3}

c_{N,4}

\cdots c_{N,n-2}
from a Fibonacci-Lucas sequence-sequence
\{F[w_{i},z_{i}]_{n}\}^{N}_{i=1}
. Clearly, each permutation
FL_{j}
is just a number-based string that can be used to encrypt digital files. Obviously, it is not easy to partition a number-based string
FL_{j}
into a Fibonacci-Lucas sequence-sequence
\{F[w_{i},z_{i}]_{n}\}^{N}_{i=1}
.
\parallel

4.3
Colorings based on abstract sequences

Definition 152
.

[
63
]
Suppose that a
(p,q)
-graph
G
admits a
graceful coloring

f:V(G)\rightarrow[0,M]
such that
f(E(G))=\{f(wz)=|f(w)-f(z)|:wz\in E(G)\}=[1,q]
. Let
C_{M}=(c_{i})^{M}_{i=0}
and
D_{q}=(d_{j})^{q}_{j=1}
be two
abstract sequences
. We define a new coloring
f^{*}
of
G
by letting
f^{*}(w)=c_{i}
if
f(w)=i
for vertex
w\in V(G)
, and
f^{*}(wz)=d_{j}
if
f(wz)=j
for edge
wz\in E(G)
. Then we call
f^{*}
a
graceful abstract-sequence coloring
of
G
if
f^{*}(E(G))=D_{q}
. Here an abstract sequence
C_{M}=(c_{i})^{M}_{i=0}
or
D_{q}=(d_{j})^{q}_{j=1}
is consisted of any things in the world. Thereby,
f^{*}
is an
abstract substitution
of
f
, conversely,
f
is a
mapping homomorphism
of
f^{*}
. Let
E(G)=\{e_{i}=x_{i}y_{i}:~i\in[1,q]\}
. Then this
(p,q)
-graph
G
has its another Topcode-matrix
T^{*}_{code}(G)
defined as

{\begin{split}T^{*}_{code}(G)=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccccccccc}f^{*}(x_{1})&f^{*}(x_{2})&\cdots&f^{*}(x_{q})\\
f^{*}(e_{1})&f^{*}(e_{2})&\cdots&f^{*}(e_{q})\\
f^{*}(y_{1})&f^{*}(y_{2})&\cdots&f^{*}(y_{q})\end{array}\right)_{3\times q}=(f^{*}(X),f^{*}(E),f^{*}(Y))^{T}\end{split}}

(22)

where three
abstract vectors

f^{*}(X)=(f^{*}(x_{1}),f^{*}(x_{2}),\cdots,f^{*}(x_{q}))
,
f^{*}(E)=(f^{*}(e_{1}),f^{*}(e_{2})
,
\cdots
,
f^{*}(e_{q}))
and
f^{*}(Y)=(f^{*}(y_{1})
,
f^{*}(y_{2})
,
\cdots
,
f^{*}(y_{q}))
.
\square

In particular case of a Fibonacci-Lucas sequence (refer to Remark
34
), we have
f^{*}(w)=c_{i}=F[w_{i},z_{i}]_{n}
if
f(w)=i
for vertex
w\in V(G)
and
f^{*}(wz)=d_{j}=F[w_{j},z_{j}]_{n}
if
f(wz)=j
for edge
wz\in E(G)
. Theorem
45
tells that each tree
T
admits a
(k,d)
-gracefully total coloring
, also, a
set-ordered gracefully total coloring
as
(k,d)=(1,1)
and a
set-ordered odd-gracefully total coloring
as
(k,d)=(1,2)
, by the way, these colorings can be used to provide examples for Ringel-Kotzig Decomposition Conjecture
[
27
]
. Thereby, we have

Theorem 49
.

[
63
]
Each tree admits a
set-ordered graceful abstract sequence coloring
.

Set-type (vector-type) of colorings and labelings

5.1
Graceful set-colorings

Definition 153
.

∗
A
v-set e-proper
\varepsilon
-labeling
(resp.
\varepsilon
-
coloring
) of a
(p,q)
-graph
G
is a mapping
f:V(G)\cup E(G)\rightarrow\Omega
, where
\Omega
consists of numbers and sets, such that
f(u)
is a set for each vertex
u\in V(G)
,
f(xy)
is a number for each edge
xy\in E(G)
, and the edge color set
f(E(G))
satisfies a
\varepsilon
-condition.
\square

As
f(E(G))=[1,q]
in Definition
153
, we get a
v-set e-proper graceful labeling
, and moreover a
v-set e-proper odd-graceful labeling
is
f(E(G))=[1,2q-1]^{o}
. Euler’s graphs and Hamilton cycles are popular researching objects in graph theory. Sun
et al.

[
40
]
show a connection between Euler’s graphs and Hamilton cycles by an operation, called
non-adjacent identifying operation and the 2-edge-connected 2-degree-vertex splitting operation
. An example is shown in Fig.
54
.

Figure 54:
A procedure of connecting an Euler’s graph and a Hamilton cycle of length 10 by the

and the
2-edge-connected 2-degree-vertex vertex-splitting operation
introduced in
[
40
]
.

Sun
et al.

[
34
]
introduced some v-set e-proper
\varepsilon
-labelings on Euler’s graphs, where
\varepsilon\in\{
graceful, odd-graceful, harmonious,
k
-graceful, odd sequential, elegant, odd-elegant, felicitous, odd-harmonious, edge-magic total
\}
, see examples displayed in Fig.

.

Figure 55:
(a) A cycle admitting a graceful labeling; (a-1) and (a-2) are two Euler’s graphs admitting two v-set e-proper graceful labelings; (b) a cycle admitting an odd-graceful labeling; (b-1) and (b-2) are two Euler’s graphs admitting two v-set e-proper odd-graceful labelings, cited from
